=====FILE: belgenny-farm.md  NAME: Belgenny Farm=====
---
venue_name: "[AU-farm] Belgenny Farm"
country: Australia
region: Camden, NSW
cell: "AU-farm + NZ-nature + CA-nature (combined census, three cells)"
website: https://kidstofarms.com.au/farm/belgenny-farm-camden/
date_read: 2026-08-09
sources:
  - https://kidstofarms.com.au/farm/belgenny-farm-camden/
  - https://www.belgennyfarm.com.au/education
  - https://www.belgennyfarm.com.au/
  - https://e09b7fcb-5fb8-482c-89cc-0eaa088a2226.filesusr.com/ugd/af7c66_61ed2ef288e648818d1780f6ffdb54f0.pdf
---

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** offer_without_activity_detail

**programme_names:** School Excursions – The Dairy Industry at Belgenny Farm; A Smart Farmer: mobile dairy classroom

**activities_verbatim:** none listed as a named activity list. The education page describes topics rather than discrete activities: "How milk is produced, processed and supplied"; "The role of science and technology in dairy farming"; "Changes in dairy practices over time"; "Animal care, hygiene and biosecurity"; "The importance of dairying to regional communities and food systems". The kidstofarms.com.au listing page states "Customised programs are also available" with a link to "Primary and High School excursion details", but that linked document is a risk management plan and COVID safety plan rather than an activity list.

**duration:** "School programs run from 10:00AM – 2:00pm" (kidstofarms.com.au)

**group_size_min_max:** "Preferred number of students: 60 – 80. Maximum: 100 students."

**participant_thresholds:** "Groups of fewer than 20 students pay a flat minimum group fee." Preferred 60-80 students, maximum 100 students.

## Inventory

**facilities:** "wide, open spaces of the 1,600-hectare farm"; bus access; toilets; signposted; wheelchair access; "Creamery Interpretative Centre" showcasing the dairy industry, "supported by a virtual tour and online resources" (home page); historic farm buildings

**equipment_provided:** none listed

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "Curriculum-based farm excursions are delivered by the NSW Department of Primary Industries School programs team." "All staff at Belgenny Farm have Working with Children Checks" (also recorded under child safety checks below). Risk management plan names "Plan prepared by: Michelle Fifield, Position: Coordinator School programs".

**accessibility_provision:** "wheelchair access"; "meets safety requirements" (kidstofarms.com.au); a general "Accessibility" page is linked in site footer but was not one of the four required pages

**animal_species_held:** none listed by species name on the pages read. The education page discusses "the dairy industry" and "animal care, hygiene and biosecurity" without naming a species; the home page's "Back to Belgenny 2026" open-day listing includes "Farm Animals" and "Sheep Shearing" and "Dog Trials" as event activities, but these describe a public open day, not a stated year-round holding of species.

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** "Excursions align with the NSW Primary and Secondary syllabus, with curriculum links to: Science and Technology, History, Geography" (education page). kidstofarms.com.au states: "Excursion programs align to NSW syllabus for Kindergarten to Year 12 including outcomes for Agriculture, Food Technology, Geography, History, Science, Technology and Technology Mandatory." "Programs can be tailored to suit different year levels and learning outcomes."

**teacher_materials:** "NSW DPIRD Dairy Education Resources — A range of K–12 education resources focused on agriculture and dairying are available through the NSW DPIRD website, including: Curriculum-mapped teaching resources; Teacher professional development; Careers information related to the dairy and agricultural sectors. These resources support classroom learning before and after a visit to Belgenny Farm." A downloadable "Primary and High School excursion details" PDF is linked from kidstofarms.com.au, but its content is a Work Health and Safety risk management plan and a COVID Safety Plan, not teacher curriculum material.

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** "Risk assessment is available here" (kidstofarms.com.au, linking to a downloaded "Belgenny Farm- Risk management plan", Work Health and Safety Directorate, dated December 2018, prepared by Michelle Fifield, Coordinator School programs, 27.11.2020). The plan covers hazards for transport to/from the farm, moving around the farm, encountering wildlife/farm animals/electric fences, adverse weather, insect bites/allergic reactions, becoming lost, undertaking practical activities, and diet/food during excursions, each with stated control measures.

**insurance_stated:** "Belgenny Farm has public liability insurance"

**child_safety_checks_stated:** "All staff at Belgenny Farm have Working with Children Checks"

**accreditations_displayed:** Managed by the "NSW Department of Primary Industries and Regional Development (DPIRD)"; "This project is supported by the Department of Agriculture, Fisheries and Forestry through funding from the Australian Government's Educating Kids About Agriculture initiative" (Kids to Farms footer); site is "Heritage Listed"

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** "Students: $16.50 per student, Supervising teachers and teacher aides FREE, Additional parents: $16.50 per person." "Groups of fewer than 20 students pay a flat minimum group fee" (amount not stated).

**adults_free_ratio:** "Supervising teachers and teacher aides FREE" — no numeric ratio of free adults per student group stated. The separate COVID Safety Plan document states "we ask that you please bring with you as many staff as possible to cover one adult per group (groups sizes are determined by the number of students attending and the activities to be run)".

**catering:** none listed

**cancellation_and_weather:** none listed on the schools/pricing pages read. The COVID Safety Plan document (dated last updated February 2021) states "Please reschedule your visit if you or anyone within your schools has symptoms of COVID infection" and describes a wet-weather alternative for rain gear ("rain coats – wet weather alternative available onsite") in the risk management plan, but no general weather-cancellation policy for excursions is stated.

**booking_lead_time:** none listed

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** "Book your visit now by contacting the farm directly!" Phone: 02 4654 6800. Email: belgenny.farm@dpi.nsw.gov.au. Website: https://www.belgennyfarm.com.au/education

**price_visible_without_asking:** Yes — per-student pricing is published directly on the kidstofarms.com.au listing page

**downloadable_pack:** Yes — risk management plan and COVID Safety Plan PDF linked from the kidstofarms.com.au listing as "Primary and High School excursion details"

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed

**title_and_opening_line:** Title: "School Excursions – The Dairy Industry at Belgenny Farm". Opening line: "Belgenny Farm offers school excursion experiences focused on the science, technology and history of the dairy industry."

# Bidgee Strawberries and Cream

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** activity_list_no_timings

**programme_names:**
- High School Students - Educational Tour
- Primary School Students - Educational Tour
- Preschool - Non-educational picking excursion (available during picking season only)
- Meet the farmer

**activities_verbatim:**
- "an educational experience by a qualified expert suited to the needs of the group"
- "educational worksheet/s can be provided (please provide the subject and topics when making your booking)"
- "a choice of one of the following (i) small ice cream or (ii) small sorbet"
- "colouring in, find-a-word, dot-to-dot, quiz or scavenger hunt (provided based on age group)"
- "entry to the strawberry patch"
- "During the picking season, students can pick a small punnet for an additional cost of $5 per student"
- "Your tour guide will take you on an adventure through the farm and provide an introduction to hydroponic farming and Integrated Pest Management (IPM) techniques"
- "Included in the tour is a visit to the scar tree, where you can take a photo feeding the Damara sheep, check out the live display bee hive, and while picking your own punnet of strawberries, enjoy tasting a few samples of the sweet juicy fruit"
- "you will have the opportunity to taste some of the products, including chocolate coated freeze dried strawberries, a sweet strawberry treat from our display cabinet, as well as our homemade strawberry sorbet and ice cream" (this line under "Meet the farmer")

**duration:** "We recommend you allow two hours for the educational experience" (High School and Primary tours); "Time required 2 hours" (Meet the farmer). No clock times or ordered sequence given, so no timed programme.

**group_size_min_max:** "Cost $150/person or $600/group (minimum of 2, maximum of 8)" for Meet the farmer. School tours state pricing tiers with a minimum fee (see price_and_structure) but no explicit group headcount minimum or maximum stated for the High School, Primary or Preschool tours.

**participant_thresholds:**
- Preschool tour: "a minimum of one adult is required for every three children to assist with picking" (supervision ratio, not a headcount limit)
- Meet the farmer: minimum of 2, maximum of 8 people per group

## Inventory

**facilities:** "We have a grassed area or a café with tables and chairs for students to enjoy their lunch"; "trampoline, sandpit"; "large grassy area"; roadside stall; café; shed with roller door for ticketing on the pick-your-own page.

**equipment_provided:** "sanitised scissors and punnets" (pick-your-own context, not stated as part of the school tour specifically).

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "an educational experience by a qualified expert suited to the needs of the group": "qualified expert" stated, no qualification named.

**accessibility_provision:** none listed.

**animal_species_held:** bees ("visit the bees"; "live display bee hive"); chickens ("visit the... chickens"; "check out the chickens"); sheep, stated as Damara sheep ("visit the... sheep"; "feeding the Damara sheep"; "the sausages are Damara lamb, grown on the farm"); lambs, named Harry and Billy ("meet Harry and Billy, our lambs who love a feed and pat"); cattle, a Jersey calf named Buttercup ("meet Buttercup the Jersey calf").

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** none listed.

**teacher_materials:** none listed. (Educational worksheets are offered but described as provided on request per topic, not as a downloadable teacher pack.)

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** none listed.

**insurance_stated:** none listed.

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed.

**accreditations_displayed:** none listed.

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:**
- High School Students - Educational Tour: "$25 per student for the first 12 (Minimum fee: $300 per group), then $10 per additional student"
- Primary School Students - Educational Tour: "$22 per student for the first 14 (Minimum fee: $300 per group), then $10 per additional student"
- Preschool non-educational picking excursion: "$12 per person, plus a minimum of one large punnet for $30 per group. More punnets may be purchased"
- Meet the farmer: "Cost $150/person or $600/group (minimum of 2, maximum of 8)"
- Pick Your Own general entry: "Adult $10", "Child $3", "Family $25" (2 adults + up to 3 kids under 15)

**adults_free_ratio:** "We don't charge the teachers accompanying the students for the tour (the option to purchase items is available)." No numeric ratio of free adults per paying students stated. Separately, for the preschool tour, "Adults are not charged to enter."

**catering:** "If your visit occurs during lunchtime, the students can bring their lunch, or we can prepare a sausage sizzle for $3 per sausage sandwich; the sausages are Damara lamb, grown on the farm. We have a grassed area or a café with tables and chairs for students to enjoy their lunch." Café on site (mentioned in navigation and pick-your-own page).

**cancellation_and_weather:** "All bookings require a 20% non-refundable deposit" (Meet the farmer); "Once we confirm a date, we will send through an invoice for a 10% non-refundable deposit. Your booking will be secured once the deposit has been received" (school tours). Pick-your-own page notes "(weather conditions may affect strawberries and u pick availability)" but this is not stated as part of the school programme's cancellation terms.

**booking_lead_time:** none listed as a specific number of days/weeks. Process stated: "*To make a booking, please email info@bidgeestrawberriesandcream.com.au with your two preferred dates, arrival time, approximate number of participants, the topic or topics you'll be covering in class, and the age or year level of the students." "Tours are available Monday to Friday."

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** "*To make a booking, please email info@bidgeestrawberriesandcream.com.au with your two preferred dates, arrival time, approximate number of participants, the topic or topics you'll be covering in class, and the age or year level of the students." For "Meet the farmer": "Please click on the link to make a booking" / "Book Now", with an alternative "If the available dates do not suit, send an email with your preferred dates to info@bidgeestrawberriesandcream.com.au."

**price_visible_without_asking:** yes; full price tiers for all four programmes are published on the Educational Farm Tours page itself.

**downloadable_pack:** none listed. Worksheets are offered but described as supplied on request, not as a downloadable file.

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed.

**title_and_opening_line:** Page title "Tours". Opening line: "We've developed our educational experiences to suit all the different age groups and interests of the people who visit our farm. For the little ones, we've got colouring-in activities, and for older guests or school groups, we offer guided educational tours. You can come and visit the bees, chickens, and sheep, take a peek inside our scar tree, and enjoy tasting some of the delicious products we make right here on the farm. If you are a bus company, we provide a customised service. More information available via email."

# Bilpin Fruit Bowl

Note on search: the site's main navigation (What We Grow, Pick Your Own, Find Us, Gallery, Contact) contains no page addressed to schools, teachers, education groups, student groups or home-school groups. The full sitemap.xml was pulled and checked; it lists no page or slug containing "school," "education," "excursion," "field trip," "student," "teacher," or "learning." A page titled "healthy-kids-association" was found in the sitemap and read; it is an empty stub page carrying only the site's standard header and footer and no body content. On this evidence, this venue is coded no_school_offer.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** no_school_offer

**programme_names:** none listed as a school programme. The nearest text found, on the pricing/booking page for the general public pick-your-own product, reads: "Orchard tours and fruit picking for groups available during the week. Bookings essential to do this." This line names "groups" generically, not schools, and names no activity detail, price, duration or contact process specific to it.

**activities_verbatim:** none listed for a school programme.

**duration:** none listed for a school programme. General public pick-your-own bookings are stated as "Standard bookings are for 2 hours."

**group_size_min_max:** none listed for a school or group programme, beyond the general public cap: "Bookings limited to 200 people every 2 hours."

**participant_thresholds:** none listed for a school programme. General public threshold: "Bookings limited to 200 people every 2 hours."

## Inventory

**facilities:** "Our shop/cafe onsite will be open for trade"; "toilets, counters and hand washing sink" (named as high-traffic areas kept sanitised); "designated picnic area"; farm and carpark, with "No cars are permitted into the farm... please park in the carpark and walk out to the farm."

**equipment_provided:** "We supply environmentally friendly picking bags on entry (@ a fee of $2 each) but we also encourage you to BYO bags and scissors for strawberry picking."

**staff_qualifications_stated:** none listed.

**accessibility_provision:** none listed.

**animal_species_held:** none listed. ("No pets in the farm" is a visitor restriction, not a statement of animals held by the venue.)

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** none listed.

**teacher_materials:** none listed.

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** none listed.

**insurance_stated:** none listed.

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed.

**accreditations_displayed:** none listed. (The page slug "healthy-kids-association" suggests a possible past affiliation but the page itself carries no content confirming or describing it.)

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** For general public pick-your-own entry: "$20 for adults, $10 for children aged between 3 and 12. Free for children under 3 years of age. ... This entry fee includes the fruit you eat when on the farm. You just pay for the fruit and veggies that you take home with you." Strawberry punnets: "Cost will be $10 for a 500gm punnet." No school-specific price is published.

**adults_free_ratio:** none listed.

**catering:** "Our shop/cafe onsite will be open for trade"; "Picnics are allowed... Please bring your own picnic rugs and chairs. Picnics to be in designated picnic area, No Picnics in the farm."

**cancellation_and_weather:** "No refunds on bookings only when we cancel the event. If we choose to cancel the activity you will receive a full refund." No weather-specific policy stated.

**booking_lead_time:** none listed as a specific lead time. "Bookings are essential. Book early as these limited spots mean that they are taken early."

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** General public: online booking via the "Pick Your Own Booking" product page. For the group/orchard-tour line: "Orchard tours and fruit picking for groups available during the week. Bookings essential to do this," with no separate contact method given beyond the venue's general phone and email ("Farm Enquiries 0404 061 262"; "fruit275@tpg.com.au"; "For all enquiries regarding pick your own, please call Margaret on 0404 061262").

**price_visible_without_asking:** For the general public pick-your-own product, yes, price is published on the Pick Your Own and booking pages. For the "groups" orchard tour line, no price is stated.

**downloadable_pack:** none listed.

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed.

**title_and_opening_line:** No school programme page exists to carry a title and opening line. The nearest page, "Pick your own | Bilpin Fruit Bowl," opens: "As we are providing an experience to you and your friends/family we want it to be a pleasant one."

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** Preschool to Primary programme; Secondary programme; The Alpaca Connection Tour (general group tour, not school-specific)

**activities_verbatim:** "Meet the Blackwattle Team"; "Introduction to the farm and safety discussion"; "Listen, Learn and Participate Session"; "Meet and pat one of our llamas (preschool and primary)"; "Take our alpacas and llamas for a walk (secondary)"; "Hand feeding session"; optional lunch package add-on

**duration:** Preschool to Primary: 2.5 hours. Secondary: 3.5 hours. Catering package adds 30 minutes. (The Alpaca Connection Tour, a separate non-school group tour, runs 45 minutes: 15-minute educational talk, 15-minute meet-and-greet with a llama, 15-minute hand-feeding session, plus tour chat)

**group_size_min_max:** School Groups: maximum 70 students at one time; no minimum stated (larger groups asked to contact the farm directly). The Alpaca Connection Tour: minimum 10 people, maximum capacity of 70 guests

**participant_thresholds:** maximum 70 students per school visit; minimum 10 people for The Alpaca Connection Tour

## Inventory

**facilities:** large indoor learning hub described as "all year and all weather"; farm shop (free entry); on-site cafe; bus parking; outdoor seating areas

**equipment_provided:** none listed

**staff_qualifications_stated:** none listed

**accessibility_provision:** accessibility details held on a separate page; height restrictions apply for children handling alpacas; children under 16 must be supervised by paying adults; assistance dogs permitted with advance notice

**animal_species_held:** alpacas; llamas

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** Australian Curriculum (AC Codes 8.4 and 9); Early Years Learning Framework (EYLF Outcome 2)

**teacher_materials:** e-Learn Together Workbook, age-differentiated for preschool, Early Stage 1/Stage 1, and Stage 2/Stage 3

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** yes, stated as available

**insurance_stated:** "Certificate of Currency (Public Liability)" available; farm described as "fully insured"

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed

**accreditations_displayed:** Work, Health and Safety Management Plan stated as available (not an external accreditation, but the only trust-adjacent document beyond risk assessment and insurance)

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** Preschool to Primary (2.5 hr): $30 per student. Secondary (3.5 hr): $35 per student. Catering: from $3 per student. The Alpaca Connection Tour (non-school): $30 per person

**adults_free_ratio:** "Teachers: Free (excluding catering)"

**catering:** catering package available from $3 per student; general farm policy states "Only food and drinks purchased from us can be consumed on our farm"

**cancellation_and_weather:** "Bookings and voucher purchases are non-refundable" but transferable to another person; cancellations with 24+ hours notice can reschedule or receive a voucher valid for three years; cancellations within 24 hours or no-shows forfeit all monies paid with no rescheduling or voucher; if the farm cancels, customers receive a full refund or the option to reschedule; "Tours run in light to moderate rain. Wet weather options are available"; severe weather may trigger postponement, decided as late as possible, with guests notified by phone

**booking_lead_time:** none listed

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** contact the farm directly by phone (0403 991 612) providing group size, preferred date/time, special needs, accessibility requirements and add-on interests; email info@blackwattlealpacas.com.au

**price_visible_without_asking:** yes, prices are stated directly on the School Groups page

**downloadable_pack:** e-Learn Together Workbook stated as available (age-differentiated); not confirmed as directly downloadable versus provided on booking

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed

**title_and_opening_line:** "School Groups" / "Take your students out of the classroom and onto the farm!"

## Note

School group visits are stated as running "Thursdays only, between 10am–4pm", outside the farm's public opening hours of Friday to Sunday, 10am-4pm. The farm is located "315 Patemans Lane, Murrumbateman NSW 2582", stated as 30 minutes from Canberra. Entry to the farm and farm shop is free without a booked tour; animal interaction requires a booking.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** activity_list_no_timings

**programme_names:** Farm Camp; Lake Camp (the two campsites run by Camp Coolamatong; this entry concerns the Farm Camp).

**activities_verbatim:**

- Abseiling/Rock Climbing Tower
- Canoeing
- Stand Up Paddle Boarding
- Bush cooking
- Farming
- Mountain Biking
- Archery
- Low Ropes
- Raft Building
- Adventure Race
- Survivor (Initiatives Course)
- Geocaching
- Night Hike
- Boat Trip on 49 seat boat, the CC Neill

At extra cost: Overnight Hiking Expedition, Water Tubing, Sailing, Indigenous Experience, Sea Kayaking, White Water Rafting.

**duration:** none listed

**group_size_min_max:** Accommodation capacity up to 130 students plus staff.

**participant_thresholds:** Boat Trip stated to be on a "49 seat boat, the CC Neill" (a capacity figure for that activity, not a stated group minimum/maximum for booking).

## Inventory

**facilities:** Five cabins (15-17 each), eight bunkrooms (6-9 each), three leader rooms (2-3, with en suites); one cabin provides full disabled access; dining hall (seats 200); Shearing Shed (games hall); Multipurpose Hall (seats 300+); sports ovals; beach volleyball; basketball courts; fitness course; BBQ area; 170 acres of farm and bushland with direct access to the Gippsland Lakes.

**equipment_provided:** none listed beyond what is implied by activity names (no explicit equipment list on the pages read).

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "All activities are run by qualified Coolamatong staff." Staff and program volunteers stated to be "Working with Children Check compliant and ChildSafe Trained."

**accessibility_provision:** "One cabin provides full disabled access."

**animal_species_held:** none listed (Farm Camp page states "Lots of farm animals to visit" without naming species).

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** none listed

**teacher_materials:** none listed as a teacher pack; however, the Resources page provides downloadable Activity Plan/Risk Assessment documents for 26 named activities (e.g. Adventure Race, Archery, Beach Games, Boat Trip, Bushcraft, Canoeing, Climbing & Abseiling, Combat Archery, Farming, Geocaching, Indoor Climbing, Low Ropes, Night Games & Walks, Offsite/Onsite Cycling, Overnight Hike, Raft Building, Day Hike, Stand Up Paddle Boarding, Survivor Lake, Survivor Farm, Swimming, Tribal Challenge), plus an Emergency Management Plan, a Certificate of Insurance, a Child Safety Policy, and an Adverse Environmental Conditions Policy.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** none listed as a single named "programme" product. The venue instead operates as a self-catered/fully-catered residential campsite that a school books and staffs (via its own or Candlebark's optional instructors), assembling its own itinerary from an activities list. The downloadable "Sample Program" is headed with a sample school name and dates ("Big Hill Primary School 1-3 August") and is offered as a template, not a fixed product name.

**activities_verbatim (from the Activities page, in the order given):**
Super Swooper (led by 2 instructors)
Archery (instructor led)
Canoeing (instructor led)
Flying Fox (instructor led)
Swimming Pool (lifeguard led)
All Aboard
Boundary Walk
Camp Fire
GaGa Ball
Games Shed
Golf Frisbee
Low Ropes Course
Night Trail
Orienteering
Photo Find and Treasure Hunt
Rock Room
Ski Race
Spider Web
Table Tennis and Games Shed

The Campsite Booklet's "ON-SITE ACTIVITIES" table separately categorises these as Physical Activities, Group Initiative Activities and Passive Activities, each with a stated duration (see duration field).

**duration:** From the Campsite Booklet's on-site activities table: Archery 1 hr; Boundary Walk 1 hr; Canoeing 1 hr; Flying fox 1 hr; Gaga Ball 1 hr; Games Shed 1 hr; Golf Frisbee 1 hr; Low ropes Course 1 hr; Night trail 1 hr; Rock Room 1 hr; Super Swooper 1 hr; Swimming 1-2 hrs; All aboard 20 min; Ski race 20 min; Spider Web 30 min; Camp Fire 1 hr; Orienteering 1 hr; Photo Find 1 hr; Treasure Hunt 1 hr. The Sample Program (a 3-day/2-night itinerary for "Big Hill Primary School") shows a full timed schedule: e.g. Monday Session 1 starts 12:45 PM, 60 minutes; Session 2 starts 1:45 PM, 45 minutes; snack/rotation breaks of 30 minutes between sessions; Tuesday's six activity sessions each run 60 minutes from 9:15 AM to 5:00 PM; the camp runs from arrival at camp 10:30 AM Monday to arrival back at school 4:00 PM Wednesday.

**group_size_min_max:** "Candlebark Farm Enquiry Form (we are for groups of 40 to 112)". The Campsite Booklet states: "Activities are generally designed for one supervising adult led group of 10-15 campers." The campsite sleeps 112 across 3 accommodation blocks (16 bunkrooms plus a teacher's room).

**participant_thresholds:** Super Swooper: "90kg weight limit applies, best with two groups over two sessions"; "maximum turnover for the Super Swooper is 78 students per day"; Flying Fox: "70kg weight limit applies"; instructor-led optional extras have a stated minimum: "a minimum of 40 applies even if you have fewer" (per-person costed optional extras); linen provision "In minimum lots of 10"; recommended supervision ratio "one adult leader to ten campers (1:10) for schools and twice that for family groups (2:10)"; the enquiry form separately notes "at least 1 adult leader per 10-13 children" for schools running their own activities.

## Inventory

**facilities:** Candlebark Lodge (sleeping 54 in 9 ensuite bunkrooms); Stringybark Lodge (sleeping 26 in 3 ensuite bunkrooms and teacher's lounge); Blackwood Lodge (sleeping 32 in 4 ensuite bunkrooms); air-conditioned Conference Hall (9 x 18 m, carpeted, data projector, screen, surround sound, bluetooth/DVD/USB player, whiteboard); Games Shed with table tennis and Rock Room; air-conditioned dining room seating 112 (in Candlebark Lodge); air-conditioned art/craft room (in Stringybark Lodge); swimming pool (fenced); GaGa Pit; Giant Checkers Board; Camp Fire area; hard-wired smoke detectors in all bunkrooms, separated by fire-rated walls. "STOP PRESS: Our 16 ensuite bunkrooms now have air-conditioning" and "Our 30 kWh solar panels help offset our carbon footprint."

**equipment_provided:** 7 canoes (for Canoeing); modern archery gear (for Archery); the Super Swooper and Flying Fox rigs; low ropes course equipment; "we provide a towel and linen for participants to make their own bed" (linen is an extra-cost optional, $12.00 per person, in minimum lots of 10; beds are not made up by staff). Camper groups are required to bring their own first aid kit: "groups are required to bring their own first-aider with first aid kit and a vehicle for emergency transport of ill campers" (Enquiry Form).

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "Swimming Pool requires a Lifeguard with either AUSTSWIM or Bronze Medallion. Canoeing requires an Australian Canoeing Inc Level 1 FlatWater Guide/Instructor. The qualified person must be present at all times." "Candlebark Farm staff do not supervise campers. User group leaders must supervise campers." "There is no first aider resident on site." Resident Manager named as "Michael Hill" (facilities page). Staff and volunteers are stated (Child Protection Policy) to require a Working With Children Check or equivalent, or a Police Check where a WWCC does not apply.

**accessibility_provision:** none listed on the pages read (the ACA directory record's "Accessibility Details" field for this venue was blank/NULL)

**animal_species_held:** "The farm has kangaroos, wombats, possums, goannas, and a good bird population." (Campsite Booklet, "Wildlife" section). These are free-ranging wildlife on the property, not a held/managed collection; the venue is a residential camp, not an animal park.

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** none listed by curriculum code or subject name

**teacher_materials:** "Candlebark Farm Booklet" (47-page downloadable PDF covering facilities, activities, local hazards, hirer responsibilities, room allocation, dietary requirements form, menu plan, a 3-day sample program, "A GUIDE TO CAMP PROGRAMMING", a check list, camper briefing notes, camper information, an emergency management plan, and a full activities list with descriptions); "Sample Activity Program" (downloadable timetable template); "Map of Candlebark Farm"; activity templates for "Treasure Hunt", "Orienteering" and "Photo find", offered explicitly for schools "you may wish to include in your own campsite booklet"; a "Room Allocation" form; a "Dietary Requirements Form"; "Campers Information" (what to bring); "Leader EMP Information" (leader-facing emergency procedures); "Candlebark Farm Child Protection" policy document; a "COVID Safe Plan".

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** none listed as a pre-prepared risk assessment document for the hirer to use as-is. The Campsite Booklet instead places this responsibility on the hirer: "Please note: Any safety issues associated with excursions are to be identified by the hirer and any necessary precautions taken." Candlebark Farm supplies its own Emergency Management Plan and posts emergency phone numbers on site, with a condensed leader version in the booklet (pages 17-18), described as "suitable for your Group Leaders. This should be copied and handed to each Camp Leader so they know what to do in an emergency."

**insurance_stated:** "Although Candlebark Farm carries its own Public Liability insurance, this will not necessarily protect your organization/leaders from claims made by your participants against your organization/group. Your organization/ group is responsible for ensuring that they have appropriate public liability insurance and so it is highly recommended that your organization/group seek advice from an insurance broker about the event you wish to hold here."

**child_safety_checks_stated:** Candlebark Farm's own Child Protection Policy document states that staff, contractors and volunteers are required to hold a Working With Children Check ("WWCC") or other state equivalent, or, where a WWCC does not apply, to provide a Police Check, before commencing duties.

**accreditations_displayed:** ACA directory record shows "ATAP Accreditation" = yes, dated 30 June 2020, and "ATAP Camps Activity Accreditation" = yes, also dated 30 June 2020; and a "NARTA Accredited Date" of 30 June 1989. The ACA search-results listing tags the venue "QT accredited". The venue's own homepage links out to trustthetickvic.com.au/pages/camping (a tick-safety external accreditation reference), not opened as part of the four core pages for this record.

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** No price list or per-person rate is published on any page read. The Enquiry Form is explicitly the mechanism for costing: "To check costs and availability of dates for your camp/retreat, please fill in this form." The form distinguishes "standard price" from a "packaged price" for schools: "For school groups we can provide a packaged price and full program assistance which includes transport, entry to Healesville Sanctuary and instructor numbers geared to the number of children attending." Stated per-item optional-extra charges: linen $12.00 per person (minimum lots of 10); Duty Groups taken over by staff, extra $7.00 per person per meal; instructor-led extras (Super Swooper, Archery, Canoeing, Flying Fox, Swimming Pool lifeguard, "Extra Instructor") are costed on request with "a minimum of 40 applies even if you have fewer".

**adults_free_ratio:** none listed as a free-adult ratio. The stated ratios are supervision requirements, not free-entry allowances: "one adult leader to ten campers (1:10) for schools" (Campsite Booklet); "at least 1 adult leader per 10-13 children" (Enquiry Form).

**catering:** "We offer fully catered camps with a range of different meals and can cater for various dietary requirements, when notice is given." "Self catering is only possible when a food safety qualified person is in charge of the meals and kitchen." Vegetarian or vegan catering "available by arrangement." A downloadable Dietary Requirements Form and sample menu are provided. The Enquiry Form asks whether catering is "Self catered (SC) or Fully catered (FC)" and notes "Bringing your own lunch the first day is more economical."

**cancellation_and_weather:** "Activities may be modified or cancelled by camp or group leaders to address; weather, participants abilities, needs, behaviour and comfort requirements." No cancellation/refund policy is stated on the pages read.

**booking_lead_time:** none listed as a minimum notice period; "Room Allocation, Menu selection, Dietary Requirements Form" are to be submitted "Two weeks before you arrive" (Downloads page)

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** "Detailed Enquiry Form" (the PDF "Candlebark Farm Enquiry Form", filled in and emailed to info@candlebarkfarm.com.au) to "check costs and availability of dates". The form collects organiser and group details, expected and maximum numbers, dates, catering choice, optional extras, budget, and how the enquirer heard about Candlebark.

**price_visible_without_asking:** no — no per-person or per-night rate appears on any page read; price is obtained only by submitting the Enquiry Form

**downloadable_pack:** yes, extensively: Enquiry Form, Campsite Booklet, Site Map, Room Allocation form, Sample Menu, Dietary Requirements Form, Sample Activity Program, Treasure Hunt template, Orienteering map, Photo Find template, Campers Information, Leader EMP Information, Child Protection policy document, COVID Safe Plan

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed on the pages read

**title_and_opening_line:** ACA directory listing text: "Candlebark farm is an accredited campsite nestled on 100 acres of the treed foothills overlooking the beautiful Yarra Valley near Healesville. Just 65 km east of Melbourne, it is an easy hours drive from the city's centre. We provide a range of facilities and outdoor experiences which help to develop confidence and a caring, supportive attitude among campers that can contribute to important lifeskills." Campsite Booklet opening line: "Candlebark's goal is to provide an enjoyable group life experience for all. Its facilities and range of outdoor challenges combine to develop confidence with a caring, supportive attitude helping in all aspects of daily life."

The page itself functions as the risk-management document (headed "EXCURSION INFO, RISK MANAGEMENT, EMERGENCY PLAN"), covering driveway guidelines, bus parking, briefing on arrival, assigned area, supervision, small groups, identification of participants, child protection, communication strategies, students arriving late, respect for other patrons, drinking water, toilets, allergies, accessibility, surfaces, water features, safety signage, weather conditions, photography, souvenir shop, direct animal contact, animal feeding, animal welfare, first aid, and an emergency response plan.

The linked PDF (saved alongside this profile) shows: Insurer "Certain Underwriters at Lloyds"; Policy Number "PUW/SYD0003582-25/PL"; Interest "Public & Products Liability Insurance"; Insured "Caversham Wildlife Park & Zoo Pty Ltd"; Business Description "Principally Wildlife Park/Zoo including animal encounters and walk through exhibits"; Period of Insurance "12 Months 4:00PM 30/09/2025 to 30/09/2026 Local Standard Time"; Sum Insured "Public Liability: AUD20,000,000 any one occurrence", "Products Liability: AUD20,000,000 any one occurrence & in the aggregate", "Goods in Care Custody & Control AUD100,000".

"ADMISSION PRICE INCLUDES: Kangaroo Feed, Farm Show, Meet A Koala (photo opportunity!), Meet the Wombat & Friends (interactive meet & greet!), Penguin Keeper Talk & Feed, Molly's Farmyard, Reptile & Nocturnal House, and more!!!"

We ask that groups kindly do not utilise this seating area." No on-site catering for school groups is offered; groups bring their own food to the assigned picnic area.

Students are encouraged to be adequately protected (rain coats, umbrellas, sunscreen, hats, drinking water, etc) for weather conditions. # Chesterfield Farm

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "School Excursions" (School Excursion Info flyer); "Kinder Excursion" (Kinder Excursion Info flyer); "VM Animal Studies" (VM Animal Studies Guideline, offered alongside the school and kinder options on the same booking form, with programme choices on the form listed as "Kinder/Preschool", "Primary School", "VM Animal Studies").

**activities_verbatim:**

From the School Excursion Info flyer, "Your students' day at Chesterfield Farm will include ~":

- Animal feeding (emus, goats, deer, kangaroos and much more)
- Visiting and handling many of the farm's newest additions in our large animal nursery.
- A ride in our fun tractor-drawn wagon.
- A visit to our pig stys.
- A visit to our educational discovery barn where children will talk with one of our farmers about Australian farming eras and farm produce (* suitable for primary aged children only).
- And of course our fun farm shows which include sheepdog work and hand milking a cow with a show.

From the VM Animal Studies Guideline, "ACTIVITY GUIDELINE" (this timed schedule is stated on the guideline for the VM Animal Studies booking option, not stated as applying to the general School Excursion):

- 10:00AM Morning Tea
- 10:30AM Activity 1: Rabbit/Guinea Pig handling/weighing for worming
- 11:15AM Hand milking
- 11:30AM Milking Show
- 11:45AM LUNCH
- 12:15PM Tractor
- 12:30PM Activity 2*: Chicken run handling and health check/feed trail
- 1:00PM Dog Show
- 1:15PM Activity 3: Mock sheep drenching and treatment record keeping (with Scout)
- *Can swap out Activity 1 for dog grooming/handling.
- *Can swap out Activity 2 for nursery or feed trail time.

Activity detail, verbatim, from the same guideline:

- ACTIVITY 1: Safely handle and pick up rabbits/GP's using a towel and keeping four paws in contact with floor or handler's chest/lap; Move animal to weighing scale and weigh in bucket (make sure to zero the scale first!); Record weight and choose correct dosage rate for small animal wormer; Record treatment in appropriate paperwork; Safely move animal back to their original pen
- ACTIVITY 2: Safe handling of chickens and overall health check?
- ACTIVITY 3: Move sheep into raceway next to shearing yards, then tie Scout up on show barn stage; Discuss drenching – why it is done and how often, how the drench gun works, how to drench safely; Practise mock drenching (no liquid in barrel); Record treatment details and note down estimated weight of sheep (80kg) and dose rate; Scout's station – practise record taking for wormer tablets/flea treatment and health check of dogs

**duration:** "Our school excursion programs on weekdays from 10am to 2pm" (School Excursion Info flyer). The VM Animal Studies Guideline schedule runs from 10:00AM to 1:15PM as the last listed timed item; no explicit finishing time is stated beyond that.

**group_size_min_max:** none listed for school excursions. (The booking form states, for the separate party package: "minimum 10 children aged 3 and over plus adults for party package".)

**participant_thresholds:** "minimum 10 children aged 3 and over plus adults for party package" (stated on the booking form for the birthday/party package, not for the school excursion).

## Inventory

**facilities:** many under-cover picnic areas; large animal nursery; pig stys; educational discovery barn; Show Barn (history page: "The feedlot is now the Show Barn"); shearing yards; raceway.

**equipment_provided:** none listed.

**staff_qualifications_stated:** none listed. ("Each group will be accompanied by one of our friendly farm staff for some of the duration of their visit" — role only, no qualification stated.)

**accessibility_provision:** none listed.

**animal_species_held:** emus, goats, deer, kangaroos, cows (dairy cows; also "Highland Cow Experience"), pigs, rabbits, guinea pigs, chickens, sheep, dogs, reptiles ("Reptile Talk" — species not named).

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** none listed.

**teacher_materials:** "School Excursion Info" flyer (PDF); "Kinder Excursion Info" flyer (PDF); "VM Animal Studies Guideline" activity guideline (PDF).

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** none listed.

**insurance_stated:** none listed.

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed.

**accreditations_displayed:** none listed.

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** "Prices for School Excursion bookings are as follows: Students ~ $15.00 each; Teachers & 2 parent helpers per farm group ~ free of charge; Extra parents ~ $15.00 each (GST included)."

**adults_free_ratio:** "Teachers & 2 parent helpers per farm group ~ free of charge."

**catering:** "Visitors are required to provide their own morning tea and lunch."

**cancellation_and_weather:** none listed.

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** "If you have any further enquiries or would like to make a booking, please access our group booking page on our website www.chesterfieldfarm.com.au and fill out the details form, contact us on 9763 1588 between 10am and 3pm or email us at admin@chesterfieldfarm.com.au." The online form asks for three preferred date options, school name and address, contact person details, phone and email, number of children, parents/adults, and teachers/carers, and additional notes.

# Collingwood Children's Farm: School Programme Profile

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:**
School Tours
Self-Guided Groups
Camping Groups

**activities_verbatim:**
(School Tours page, "Itinerary") Students often get to meet and pat our guinea pigs, goats and/or sheep and typically get to see pigs, cows, horses, chickens, ducks, goats, cats and peacocks. We usually visit one of our food growing spaces such as the market garden.
(School Tours page, "Learning Intentions") To explore the Farm using the senses.
To learn and use correct terminology for living things, their features and their offspring.
To explore where our animals live and what we do to keep them healthy and happy.
To identify the food and fibre products which we get from our Farm animals.
(Self-Guided Groups page) Self-guided groups are welcome to join in on the daily activities put on by our friendly farmers.
(Camping Groups page) Immersive & Educational: Get a firsthand look at sustainable urban farming, animal care, and environmental stewardship.
A Peaceful, Nature-Based Retreat: Step away from screens and city noise for a night of fresh air, open space, and simple living.
Hands-On Learning: Connect with the rhythms of farm life while gaining practical outdoor skills.
A Safe & Well-Equipped Site: Stay in designated camping areas with access to the historic Barn, toilets, and a secure overnight setup.

**duration:**
School Tours: "A one-and-a-half-hour tour running from 10:30 am to 12:00 pm." Groups must arrive by 10:00 am.
Self-Guided Groups: check-in "any time from 9.30am"; no stated end time.
Camping Groups: "Arrival from 3:30 pm, departure by 9:00 am" (overnight).
Aged Care and Mobility Access (non-school group product, vehicular access): "Duration approximately 2 ½ hours."

**group_size_min_max:**
School Tours: "up to three classes" run in parallel, "strict maximum of 30 people per class, including supervising adults."
Self-Guided Groups: "Minimum group size of 10 children", "up to 60 people"; "Larger groups must divide into smaller groups of no more than 10 children while exploring the Farm."
Camping Groups: "Suitable for primary and high school groups (8-24 people, including adults)."

**participant_thresholds:**
School Tours minimum adult-to-student ratios: "Early Childhood - 1:5", "Foundation through Year two - 1:8", "Years three through six - 1:10".
School Tours: "strict maximum of 30 people per class, including supervising adults."
Self-Guided Groups: minimum 10 children; maximum 60 people; subgroup cap of "no more than 10 children" per exploring group.
Camping Groups: 8-24 people including adults.
Aged Care and Mobility Access: "$175 per vehicle and up to 12 passengers."

## Inventory

**facilities:** Farm Cafe; gardens and productive growing spaces; bushland along the Birrarung river; the Barn (available for events, holds up to 150 guests, and used for shelter and campfire cooking on Camping Groups bookings); designated camping areas in Stables Paddock; toilets; Community Gardens; The Apiary; gift shop.

**equipment_provided:** none listed beyond "maps and site details provided" for vehicle access on Camping Groups bookings.

**staff_qualifications_stated:** none listed. Tours are led by "one of our knowledgeable Farmers" / "a Farmer leading each class" — role title only, no qualification stated.

**accessibility_provision:** Aged Care and Mobility Access page: "Vehicular access for Aged Care Homes, NDIS providers and other organizations catering to people with special mobility needs", "$175 per vehicle and up to 12 passengers, plus booking fees", duration approximately 2½ hours, guests may "join in public activities, roam the Farm, enjoy a coffee or a snack at the Farm Cafe."

**animal_species_held:** guinea pigs, goats, sheep, pigs, cows, horses, chickens, ducks, cats, peacocks (School Tours "Itinerary"). Homepage additionally references chickens and goats and "farm animals" generally.

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:**
School Tours are "linked to the Victorian Curriculum Foundation - Level 2." Stated codes:
Science (Foundation-Level 2): "Participate in guided investigations, including making observations using the senses, to explore and answer questions (VCSIS051)"; "Living things have a variety of external features and live in different places where their basic needs, including food, water and shelter, are met (VCSSU042)"; "Living things grow, change and have offspring similar to themselves (VCSSU043)".
English (Foundation Level): "Understand the use of vocabulary in familiar contexts related to everyday experiences, personal interests and topics taught at school (VCELA167)".
Design and Technologies (Foundation-Level 2): "Explore how plants and animals are grown for food, clothing and shelter (VCDSTC015)".
Page also notes: "For specialised tours for primary school, secondary, tertiary or other organisations, please enquire here. Please note the standard School Tour prices will not be applicable."

**teacher_materials:** School Tours "Supporting Documents": Risk Assessment; Social Story; Child Safe Policy; Pre-visit Information Pack. Self-Guided Groups "Supporting Documents": Terms and conditions; Child-safe Policy, Code of Conduct and Procedure; Social Story; an "Information Pack" is also sent in the booking confirmation email. Camping Groups: a confirmation email "outlines the key logistical, behavioural and safety information"; "Supporting Documents, which includes guidance for teachers' or carers' risk assessments."

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** yes — School Tours page lists "Risk Assessment" as a downloadable Supporting Document; Camping Groups page references "guidance for teachers' or carers' risk assessments" within its Supporting Documents.

**insurance_stated:** none listed

**child_safety_checks_stated:** School Tours and Self-Guided Groups Supporting Documents each list a "Child Safe Policy" / "Child-safe Policy, Code of Conduct and Procedure" as a downloadable document; no Working with Children Check statement found on the pages read.

**accreditations_displayed:** About Us page states Collingwood Children's Farm holds "Social Traders certification" as a social enterprise.

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:**
School Tours: "$430 per class plus booking fees."
Self-Guided Groups: "$10 per child", "$20 per supervising adult"; an "Initial Deposit" ticket "includes entry for 10 children" secures the booking date.
Camping Groups: no price stated on the page read.
Aged Care and Mobility Access (non-school): "$175 per vehicle and up to 12 passengers, plus booking fees."
General admission (Tickets and Pricing page, not school-specific): Adult $20; Child 24 months-17 years $10; Infant under 24 months free; Student Card Holder $15; Victorian Senior Card Holder $15; Adult Concession Card Holder $10; Concession Child $0 where named on card; Carer $10.

**adults_free_ratio:** none listed for School Tours or Camping Groups. Self-Guided Groups: adults are charged, not free ("$20 per supervising adult").

**catering:** School Tours page does not mention catering. Self-Guided Groups: "We recommend bringing a packed picnic lunch to enjoy on the grass. The Farm Cafe may be open for coffees and food but, as they are a separate business, please check their opening hours." Camping Groups: "Catering options available through The Farm Café."

**cancellation_and_weather:** School Tours and Self-Guided Groups each reference a "Terms and Conditions" document for cancellation policy, but no cancellation terms are stated in the body text of the pages read. No weather policy stated.

**booking_lead_time:** Self-Guided Groups: "Entry for additional children and supervising adults can be purchased up to one business day before your booking, once you have finalised numbers."

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** School Tours and Camping Groups: booked online via partner Humanitix, payment "via Credit Card or Invoice"; "Book now!" / "Make A Booking" buttons. Self-Guided Groups: "purchase the ticket titled 'Initial Deposit'" to secure the date, "Book Now!" button. Phone (03) 9417 5806 listed on all pages read.

**price_visible_without_asking:** yes, for School Tours, Self-Guided Groups, general admission and Aged Care and Mobility Access. Not stated for Camping Groups.

**downloadable_pack:** yes — Risk Assessment, Social Story, Child Safe Policy and Pre-visit Information Pack are named as downloadable Supporting Documents on the School Tours page.

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed

**title_and_opening_line:** School Tours page opening line: "Collingwood Children's Farm is a not-for-profit, inner-city farm that provides green space for students and community members to grow their understanding of and connection to animals and nature. We offer School Tours to learners of all ages which are informative, engaging, interactive and are linked to the Victorian Curriculum." Homepage: "Welcome to Collingwood Children's Farm."

# Edendale Community Environment Farm: School Programme Profile

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:**
Early Years
Primary
Secondary
Teachers' Environment Network
"Education for Environmental and Climate Action"

**activities_verbatim:**

Early Years:
Farm Tour
Minibeasts (invertebrates) - A Bug's Life
Eltham Copper Butterfly
Waste not
Grow Your Own
Environmental art (Calico Bag Prints, Sculptural Junk)
Wonderful Worms
Buzzing Around
Habitat Heroes

Primary (grouped by theme; the venue's own activity names are listed under each theme):
Water: What waterbug is that? (Aquatic macroinvertebrates); Tale of a Creek (Year 2+); Water Heroes (Year 2+); Edendale farm tour (water quality focus); Water quality testing (Year 7+)
Waste: What a Load of Rubbish; Soil Matters; Consumption Down! (Year 4+); Sculptural Junk (F-3); Edendale farm tour (waste focus)
Biodiversity: Habitat Heroes; It's a Bug's Life; Who am I? Eltham Copper Butterfly; What waterbug is that? (Aquatic macroinvertebrates); Buzzing Around (F-2); Fun with Frogs (F-2)
Energy and Climate (Year 5+): Cut Your Carbon; Home Green Home; Re-Generate; Jump into Climate Action; Food Footprint
Food: Edendale farm tour (Food focus); Grow Your Own (F-2); Soil Matters; Food Footprint (Year 4+)
Sustainable Living (Year 4+): Consumption Down!; Water Heroes; Cut Your Carbon (Year 5+); Habitat Heroes; Food Footprint; Tale of a Creek; Sustainable Edendale farm tour; Young Changemakers (Year 5+)
Environmental Art: Calico Bag Prints; Sculptural Junk; Build a Bushkin (natural material sculptures)

Secondary (grouped by theme):
Water: Aquatic macro-invertebrates (identification, classification and interactions); Tale of a creek (story and role play, water quality focus); Edendale Farm tour (water conservation focus); Extended water quality testing for Year 7 Geography
Waste: Soil matters (soil composition, composting and worm farming); Consumption Down! (waste reduction hierarchy, smarter shopping and a brief recap of what goes in what bin, and why); Fast Fashion (environmental impact of what we wear)
Biodiversity: Plant local (lifecycles, propagation, planting - seasonal); Aquatic macro-invertebrates; Who Am I? Eltham Copper Butterfly (local endangered species focus)
Energy and Climate: Cut your carbon; Home green home; Re-Generate; Food footprint; Jump into climate action; Young changemakers
Food: Sustainable Edendale Farm tour (produce focus); Food footprint; Soil matters; Future of food (food security, potential foods of the future)
Sustainable Living: Consumption Down!; Cut your carbon; Plant local; Food footprint; Tale of a creek; Sustainable Edendale farm tour; Young changemakers

**duration:**
Early Years excursions: "Each activity runs for approximately 50 minutes."
Early Years incursions: "Each session runs for approximately one hour."
Primary and Secondary excursions: "Each activity runs for about 45 minutes" (Primary) / "generally runs for about 45 minutes" (Secondary).
Excursion length options (Early Years): 1 hour (1 activity); 2.5 hours (2 activities); 3.25 hours (3 activities).
Excursion length options (Primary and Secondary): 1 hour (1 activity) through 5 hours (5 activities) in Primary; 1 hour through 5 hours in Secondary.

**group_size_min_max:**
Early Years excursions and incursions: "minimum group size of 15 children."
Primary excursions and incursions: "minimum class size of 20 students" / "minimum 20 children per class."
Secondary excursions and incursions: "minimum class size of 20 students" / "minimum 20 children per class."

**participant_thresholds:**
Early Years: minimum 15 children (both excursion and incursion).
Primary and Secondary: minimum 20 students (both excursion and incursion).

## Inventory

**facilities:** teaching spaces, toilets, car parks, paths, the cafe (per the Getting Around Edendale map description); indigenous plant nursery; veggie gardens; worm farms; dam; amphitheatre with "an expanse of shade sails"; disabled parking (two spaces in the main carpark); quieter/reduced-stimulation spaces around the farm.

**equipment_provided:** identification charts for waterbug activities; "biodegradable pots" for seed planting (Grow Your Own); clover hay for feeding sheep and chicken feed mix during farm tours; human-powered bikes for the Cut Your Carbon activity; a "giant rubbish bin" and bin-sort props for waste activities.

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "A Level 2 first aid qualified staff member is also present on the farm every day, as well as other staff who have CPR training." "All Edendale staff and volunteers over 18 are required to have a Working with Children Check to be on site (or other relevant documentation such as Victorian Institute of Teaching registration)."

**accessibility_provision:** "There are number of accessibility tools to help plan a safe, fun and meaningful trip to Edendale for your students. These tools include a Visual Story and Sensory Guide, 360 Virtual Tour and an Audio Guide." "There are two disabled parking spaces in the main carpark within the site." "There are a number of quieter spaces around the farm if a student needs some time with reduced stimulation." "Mobility assistance equipment (e.g. crutches, wheelchair) should be brought by the student / school. While most activities will be run on reasonably flat surfaces and accessible via pathways, there are some undulating areas."

**animal_species_held:** guinea pigs; small native animals including stick insects, southern pygmy perch, and yabbies; sheep; alpacas; goats; ponies; chickens (Farm and Animals page "Our animals" list). FAQ answer on the same page: "Goats, sheep, alpacas, ponies, spiny leaf stick insects, guinea pigs, native fish and various breeds of poultry."

**teacher_materials:** "2026-Edendale-Education-Early-Years-brochure-FINAL.pdf (PDF, 3MB)"; "2026-Edendale-Education-Schools-brochure.pdf (PDF, 5MB)" (linked from both Primary and Secondary pages); "education risk assessment worksheet (PDF, 499KB)"; a bus drop-off map (JPG, 155KB); a facilities/Getting Around map (JPG, 4MB); an evacuation-procedures map (PNG, 2MB).

# Golden Ridge Animal Farm

Note on scope: the venue's own site, goldenridgeanimalfarm.com.au, states the Dural farm has closed to on-site visits. Its home page reads "Our beautiful farm at Dural is now closed to visitors...." and "JUST THE DURAL FARM IS CLOSED", and it directs visitors to a second domain, mobilefarms.com.au, stated on the home page as "OUR MOBILE FARMS ARE STILL AVAILIABLE" and on the Mobile Farmyard page as "We now have a Website 100% dedicated to our mobile farms." Because the venue's own current school offer (an incursion, not a site visit) is published only on that second domain under the same business name and same owner ("Alisa"), both domains were read in full and both are recorded here as the venue's published record.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** activity_list_no_timings

**programme_names:**
- "Educational - Primary K, 1 & 2" (mobilefarms.com.au)
- "Early Childhood" / "FUN VISIT PACKAGES (non educational)" for Preschools, Early Learning Centres & Childcare Centres (mobilefarms.com.au)
- "OSHC - Vacation Care" (named in site navigation, not read as it falls outside the four required page types)

**activities_verbatim (Educational - Primary K, 1 & 2):**
- "Introduction: We sit the children down and introduce ourselves and our animals to them. We ask them questions about the animals, what sounds they make and products they give us."
- "Our talk is tailored to suit your topic and cover outcomes for you - please advise what topic you are doing on booking."
- "Handling: The children will sit on the ground in small groups or lines we will let the children hold, pat and feel the smaller animals."
- "Open Pen: We open up our pen to let the children go inside and hand feed and brush our animals. If the children are not sure about going in we allow them to touch and feed the animals from the outside."
- "Question Time: If there is time at the sessions end we will have a quick questions time for the children to ask the farmers any farm animal questions they may have."

**activities_verbatim (Early Childhood / FUN VISIT PACKAGES):**
- "A hands on FUN farmy experience where the children get to hold, pat and feed a variety of hand raised farm animals"
- "there is a little talk to say hello and an introduction to our animals"
- "The children then hold the smaller animals"
- "After holding the animals the children then get to pat and feed the baby lambs and goats chaff they can also brush them"

**duration:**
- Educational Primary K-2: "Visit Length up to 2 hours"; "extra hours are available for larger schools or centres (price will be quoted)"; "SCHOOLS - Depending on student numbers, the visit is broken into 2 x 1 hour sessions - with no breaks or the schools smaller break (recess)"
- Early Childhood / Preschools: "Visit Length up to 2 hours"; "extra hours are available for larger centers (price will be quoted)"; "EARLY CHILDHOOD - Depending on the number enrolled on the day. The visit is broken up into age groups or rooms. 2 x 50 min sessions leaving a small amount of time for 0-2 yr olds to come and say hi"; "FUN VISIT PACKAGES ... suitable for Monday - Friday in the school term. Between the hours of 9am and END by 2pm"

No clock-stamped schedule is published for either programme, so this does not meet the timed_programme test; it meets activity_list_no_timings.

**cancellation_and_weather:** full refund/transfer up to 7 days before the visit; transferable up to 3 days before; non-refundable within 3 days "unless exceptional circumstances"; Green Connect offers transfer/refund if it cancels; tours "proceed in rain unless dangerous storms"

**booking_lead_time:** booking form asks for "AT LEAST two suitable date/time options"; arrive 10 minutes early on the day; no minimum weeks/days-ahead lead time stated

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** online booking form (requesting institution name, contact details, student numbers, learning stage, preferred dates, visit length, curriculum topic/syllabus outcomes); phone (02) 4243 1537 or farm tours line 0460 041 105; email activities@green-connect.com.au

**price_visible_without_asking:** yes, prices are stated directly on the Early Childhood, Secondary and general school-tour pages

**downloadable_pack:** yes, downloadable risk assessment and "essential information" documents

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed

**title_and_opening_line:** "Field trip to a working farm - Green Connect Farm" / "A visit to the Green Connect farm will allow students to connect their classroom learning to a real-world farm environment."

## Note

COVID-19 protocol still published: no visit within 14 days of hotspot exposure or with cold/flu symptoms. Allergen advice covers domestic animals, bees, wasps and macadamia trees, with an EpiPen required if a student is allergic. Custom timetabling offered: "We are happy to work with you to develop a custom timetable."

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** Educational Farm Tours; Organic Farm Adventures, Bilpin

**activities_verbatim:**

From "What Students Can Expect" (Educational Farm Tours page):
- Explore our seasonal veggie gardens and learn what plants need to thrive
- Pick and taste fresh produce straight from the farm
- Meet the worms in our giant worm farms and learn how they help build healthy soil
- Discover the role of beneficial insects in regenerative farming
- Learn about sustainable food systems and the paddock-to-plate journey
- Understand how we use food waste to create compost and improve soil health
- Breathe in fresh Bilpin air, connect with nature, and have some fun outdoors!

From "During their visit to the Bilpin Harvest Farm, students will" (curriculum PDF):
- Explore the huge worm farms, hold a worm, and understand why they are so important
- Walk through the vegetable gardens and learn the characteristics and needs of different vegetables and fruit
- Harvest some of the vegetables to taste the delicious difference between store bought and fresh from the farm! (Seasons will determine what is available)
- Learn about insects and why some are so beneficial, and others are not so for the farm
- Dig in the soil and learn why it's so suitable for growing delicious, household veggies
- Discover why it is important for people to have the opportunity to be more directly involved in the farms where their food is grown
- Explore the unique characteristics of Bilpin and why it is such a productive farming area
- Identify and describe what plants need to grow in a sustainable, restorative farm environment
- Understand how waste can be reduced and how food waste is used to improve soil health
- Discover where the food goes once it is harvested and how the vegetables are used
- Leave all their worries behind. Breath in the fresh mountain air, get muddy, laugh, play, and totally immerse themselves in the farm experience

**duration:** "Approx. 3.5 hours (10:30 AM–2:00 PM)". Tours run "year-round on Monday or Friday from 10 AM – 2 PM (by prior arrangement only)". Note: page states 3.5 hours for a 10:30–2:00 window, and separately a 10 AM–2 PM (4-hour) window for scheduling; both figures are given verbatim as they appear.

**group_size_min_max:** "13–22 students: $35 per student" and "23+ students: $22 per student" implies a minimum of 13 students; no stated maximum on the pages read. The general (non-school) Farm, Wine & Wilderness / eco tours page states "Group sizes of 10–15 people keep the experience personal and interactive", but this refers to the general tour offering, not the Educational Farm Tours.

**participant_thresholds:** School tour pricing bands: 13–22 students at $35 per student; 23+ students at $22 per student. This implies a 13-student minimum for the stated pricing to apply.

# Little Star Bee Sanctuary

Note on page mapping: this venue has no page titled "schools" or "excursions". The four required page types were searched for via the site's own navigation (Home, Our story, Hives and Products, Experiences, Connect, Resources, Privacy Policy). "Experiences" was read as the programme/activities page, "Connect" and "Our story" as the closest equivalents to a schools/education page and a booking page, and "Hives and Products" and "Resources" as the closest equivalents to a pricing page and a general "what is here" page. All were read in full.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** offer_without_activity_detail

Reasoning transcribed from the source: the "Our story" page states, under a heading "Education", "The main arm of our business is education at the Bee Sanctuary. We attend various shows, conferences, festivals and schools, as well as host courses and experiences in our education centre." and "We beelieve that educating the next generation of bee advocates is the way of the future." This states an education offer that includes schools, but names no specific activity, timing, price or format for a school visit. The "Experiences" page opens with "Honeybee and Native Bee" and the statement "We are currently not offering courses or experiences for 2024", followed by descriptions of an "Introduction to Beekeeping weekend course" and a "Native Bees" workshop, then a "Bee Experiences" section addressed to "a resident, visiting or staying in the Nambucca Valley", not to schools specifically.

**programme_names:** none listed as a named school programme. Named non-school offerings found: "Introduction to Beekeeping weekend course"; a native bee workshop (untitled, run by "Dr Tim Heard"); "Bee Experiences" (a tailored package for residents/visitors, listing "Glamping", "Introduction to Beekeeping courses", "Advanced Beekeeping techniques", "Native Bee workshops", "A tailored 'bee' experience", "Beeswax wraps, mead, cosmetic and fermenting workshops", "Apiary and farm tours", "Accompanying 'honey' inspired meals"). Also referenced but off-domain: "100 Hives in 100 Schools" project, described as "auspiced as a tax deductible charity through the Starfish Foundation," at https://100hivesin100schools.net.au/. This is a donation-based charity project, not a bookable school-visit programme, and its own site was not in scope for this venue.

**activities_verbatim:** none listed for a school-specific programme. General "Bee Experiences" list (not addressed to schools): "Glamping", "Introduction to Beekeeping courses", "Advanced Beekeeping techniques", "Native Bee workshops", "A tailored 'bee' experience", "Beeswax wraps, mead, cosmetic and fermenting workshops", "Apiary and farm tours", "Accompanying 'honey' inspired meals".

**duration:** none listed.

**group_size_min_max:** none listed.

**participant_thresholds:** none listed.

## Inventory

**facilities:** "our education centre" (named but not described); "2 further surrounding properties to enhance fodder for our bees and preserve our sanctuary".

**equipment_provided:** none listed.

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "Steven and his family, including honey extraction and tasting" described as "one of the Mid North Coast's most experienced Natural Beekeepers" (Introduction to Beekeeping course, not a school programme); native bee workshop is run by "scientist and educator Dr Tim Heard".
