# Myuna Farm: School Programme Profile

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:**
Preschool: Hands on Farming
Foundation to Grade 2: Living things on the farm
Grade 3 to 6: Education for sustainability
Year 7 - 12: Sustainability & Food Production
Year 9 - Tertiary: Animal husbandry
Environmental tour

**activities_verbatim:**

Preschool: Hands on Farming: Farm show presentation; Animal nursery; Paddock Feed Trail; Train ride to the wetlands; Pony rides (optional add-on).

Foundation to Grade 2: Living things on the farm (teachers select four or five units): Farm show (recommended); Animal nursery (recommended); Train ride to the wetlands (recommended); Paddock feed (recommended); Produce Garden; Waste (recommended for Grades One and Two); Farm school (recommended for Grades One and Two).

Grade 3 to 6: Education for sustainability: Wetland Biodiversity; Gardening for Sustainability; Waste and Recycling; Green Living.

Year 7-12: Sustainability & Food Production: Eco Tour; Wetlands Biodiversity; Green Groceries; Soil Health.

Year 9-Tertiary: Animal husbandry - core units: Program Introduction; Nutrition of Farm Mammals. Elective units (choose 2 of 4): Paddocks - Large Mammal Husbandry; Small Mammal Husbandry; Farm Nursery - Large Mammal Husbandry; Poultry Health and Care.

Environmental tour: Farm History & Approach; Sustainable Features; Wetlands Exploration; Local Food Production.

**duration:**
Preschool: farm show 40 minutes, other units 30 minutes; arrive 10:00am for morning tea, activities begin 10:20am, program ends 1:00pm (1:30pm with pony rides).
Foundation to Grade 2: each unit approximately 40 minutes with a 30-40 minute lunch break; arrive 10:00am, activities start 10:20am; 4-unit program finishes 1:40pm, 5-unit program finishes 2:20pm.
Grade 3 to 6: arrive 10:00am, program starts 10:20am, 40-minute lunch break, concludes 2:20pm.
Year 7-12: "from 10am till 2.20pm and can be scheduled to accommodate your group's needs."
Year 9-Tertiary: "10.00 am to 2.00 pm" with flexible timing available; core units 30 minutes and 1.5 hours; elective units 45 minutes each.
Environmental tour: "two hours"; available "Monday to Friday, between 11am and 2pm."

**group_size_min_max:**
Preschool: minimum 18 students; "maximum of 4 groups on a given day with up to 30 children per group."
Foundation to Grade 2: minimum 18 students; "maximum of 5 groups with up to 26 students per group."
Grade 3 to 6: minimum 18 students; up to 3 groups with "up to 26 students per group."
Year 7-12: minimum 18 students (payment minimum); "maximum of 2 groups per day with up to 26 students per group."
Year 9-Tertiary: minimum 10 students; "maximum of 2 groups per day with up to 20 students per group" (overall daily maximum 30 per the During Your Visit page).
Environmental tour: "Maximum of 20 participants, no minimum required."
Overall daily cap (During Your Visit page): "Maximum 120 students per day (up to 4 groups)"; "Hands-on programs: 20-35 students per group (minimum 18)"; "Animal Husbandry: 10-15 students per group (10-40 per booking)."

**participant_thresholds:**
Preschool: minimum 18 students; maximum 30 per group; free-adult ratio 1:5.
Foundation to Grade 2: minimum 18 students; maximum 26 per group; free-adult ratio 1:5.
Grade 3 to 6: minimum 18 students; maximum 26 per group; free-adult ratio 1:5 (below-ratio adults charged).
Year 7-12: minimum 18 students; maximum 26 per group; free-adult ratio 1:5.
Year 9-Tertiary: minimum 10 students; maximum 20 per group, 30 per day.
Environmental tour: maximum 20 participants, no minimum.

## Inventory

**facilities:** Animal Display Centre (described as sustainable building design); Animal Nursery; community vegetable gardens; Wildlife Garden; wetlands boardwalk alongside Dandenong Creek (approximately 1.5km circuit); train to the wetlands; "giant rubbish bin" waste-sorting exhibit; worm farm; playground; picnic areas with free gas BBQs; on-site café; accessible toilet block with all-gender accessible toilet, separate men's/boys' and women's/girls' facilities, and baby change area; Paddock Feed Trail (stated as accessible to prams/wheelchairs).

**equipment_provided:** identification/netting equipment for macroinvertebrate activities ("hands-on netting activity"); animal feed for paddock feeding and animal nursery interactions; biodegradable pots for the Produce Garden unit; PPE for the Animal Husbandry program introduction ("Occupational health, safety, and PPE instruction").

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "Staff possess level 2 first aid training" (During Your Visit page). Year 9-Tertiary Animal Husbandry program introduction includes: "Meet the animal husbandry team, discuss background and career progression" — no formal qualification named.

**accessibility_provision:** "An 'Access Key to the farm' is available through Accessibility Australia for those with accessibility needs" (During Your Visit page). Paddock Feed Trail described as accessible to prams/wheelchairs. Accessible toilet block with all-gender accessible toilet described on the Things to do page.

**animal_species_held:** From the Animal Information page's species-selector list: alpaca, llama, cattle, donkey, emu, pigs, pony. From the Things to Do and programme pages, additional species named: lambs, goat kids, calves, rabbits, guinea pigs, kangaroos, sheep, cows, deer, camels, reptiles/lizards, turtles, chickens, ducks, turkeys, goats (milking goats), Grey-headed flying foxes (wetlands).

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:**
Foundation to Grade 2 page states alignment to "Victorian Curriculum F-10 Version 2.0," with codes: "Plants and animals have observable features that can be used to group them in different ways (VC2S2U01)"; "Plants and animals have basic needs, including air, water, food and shelter; the places where they live meet those needs (VC2S2U02)"; "Plants and animals have external features that perform different functions to enable their survival... (VC2S2U03)".
Grade 3 to 6 page states the program addresses "Sustainability as a Cross-curriculum priority" under "Victorian Curriculum F-10 Version 2.0," covering ecosystems, biodiversity, habitat, and waste management.
Preschool page: a curriculum-link PDF is linked ("Curriculum link for Hands on Farming").
Year 7-12 and Year 9-Tertiary and Environmental tour pages: no curriculum codes stated.

**teacher_materials:** Foundation to Grade 2 page, "Before and after excursion activities": Lesson Plan for healthy eating follow-up activity; Flour & buckwheat flour recipes - foundation to grade 2; Post-excursion activity - grades 1 and 2; Myuna Farm animal word find and answers - grades 1 to 4; Myuna Farm wetlands word find and answers - grades 1 to 4; Green Living colouring in sheets; Farm food and healthy eating. Preschool page: a downloadable curriculum-link PDF.

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** none listed

**insurance_stated:** none listed

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed

**accreditations_displayed:** none listed

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:**
Preschool: Hands on Farming (4 units) $13.90 per person; with pony rides $17.50.
Foundation to Grade 2: four-unit program $13.90 per person; five-unit program $17.50 per person.
Grade 3 to 6: $16.50 per student.
Year 7-12: $17.50 per student.
Year 9-Tertiary: "$32.90 per student" (includes GST); teachers free.
Environmental tour: "Flat rate of $260."
General admission (Things to Do page, non-school): tractor rides $3.60 per person (age 1+); pony rides $3.60 per child (age 1+).

**adults_free_ratio:** "Adults are free when there is one adult for every five students. Extra adults: $12.40 each" (stated identically on Preschool, Foundation to Grade 2, Grade 3 to 6 and Year 7-12 pages). Year 9-Tertiary: "teachers free" (no ratio given). Environmental tour: no adult ratio stated.

**catering:** "Visitors must bring packed lunches." Guidance on "healthy snack options" is referenced. "Crows at the property may steal food from plastic bags" — pack lunches in plastic containers or zipped backpacks.

**cancellation_and_weather:** "Intermittent rain: Programs may proceed with possible substitutions." "Heavy rain: Rescheduling will be discussed." "Please inform us of a cancellation by 2pm the week day prior." "Late cancellation fee: 25% of booking fee applies." "No extra charge if rescheduled before the visit date" for weather-related changes.

**booking_lead_time:** none listed as a specific number of days; cancellation must be notified "by 2pm the week day prior."

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** "Booking enquiries can be made via the online booking form," or by emailing myunafarm@casey.vic.gov.au or calling 9709 9944 (stated consistently across all programme pages).

**price_visible_without_asking:** yes - per-student prices are published on every programme page.

**downloadable_pack:** yes - curriculum-link PDF (Preschool); post-excursion activity sheets, word finds, colouring sheets and lesson plans (Foundation to Grade 2).

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed

**title_and_opening_line:** Things to Do page opening line: "Myuna Farm is a working community and children's farm in Melbourne's Southeast. Providing a hands-on educational experience about Australian farming and sustainability." Education Programs and Tours page states: "All programs are built on practical activities and are curriculum aligned."

# Northey Street City Farm

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "School Activities"; "Basic Farm Tour"; "Farm Tour + Hands-On Activity"; "Primary school Curriculum Linked topics + Hands-on activities"; "Biomes and Food Security Curriculum Linked Tour + Hands-on activities"; "First Nations Schools" (free tour offer); "Group Tours and Experiences" (the general, non-school version of the same tour options); "Workshops for Teachers" (comprising "Successful School Gardening" and "Outdoor Learning and the Curriculum").

**activities_verbatim:**

From the School Activities page:

- Basic Farm Tour (1.5h) - All Year levels: "Take an interactive and sensory walk through the food gardens, fruit orchards, food forests, and recycling systems of Northey Street City Farm. Meet the farm and wildlife which make NSCF their safe haven and taste the yummy honey from our bees."
- Farm Tour + Hands-On Activity (2-2.5h) - All Year levels, one of:
  - Compost-making (2h): "Explore the different compost systems on the farm, experience what is needed to create compost, and learn about its benefits."
  - Propagation (2.5h): "Discuss different methods of propagation and get your hands dirty while propagating some of the plants found around our gardens."
  - Soil investigation (2h): "Investigate soil health and explore its role in the web of life. Collect soil samples from our gardens and conduct tests to understand the elements that may impact plant growth and erosion."
  - Australian Bush plants exploration (2h): "Take a walk around the farm to explore the smells, textures, and tastes of some Australian native plants."
  - Earth Connection (2h): "Take a sensory walk through the bush regeneration area and observe the flora and fauna around you. This grounding activity may involve learning bird language, practicing sit spots, journaling, and/or creating ephemeral nature mosaics."
- Primary school Curriculum Linked topics + Hands-on activities (2-2.5h) - Prep to Year 6: "Take a guided tour of the City Farm to see permaculture in action, with a focus on curriculum linked topics according to the year level and interests of the group. After the tour, students have a chance to engage in one of the hands-on activities described below." Hands-on activities to choose from: Compost-making (2h); Soil investigation (2h); Propagation (2.5h) (descriptions as above).
- Biomes and Food Security Curriculum Linked Tour + Hands-on activities (2-2.5h) - Years 9 to 12: "Take a guided tour of the City Farm to see permaculture in action. Students learn how permaculture can contribute to food security and provide an important alternative to conventional agriculture. An education kit with curriculum-linked worksheets and resources for teachers is available for the 'Biomes and Food Security' Year 9 Geography unit." Hands-on activities in groups of up to 20 participants: Compost-making (2h); Soil investigation (2h); Propagation (2.5h); Mapping (2h): "Explore the concept of permaculture zones, food forests, and experience what is involved in designing a garden that contributes to food security. Participants can choose to map different areas of the farm, including the market garden, the kitchen garden, the backyard garden, the native food and medicine garden, the orchard, the allotment gardens, and the woodlot, or create their own design."
- "First Nations Schools: We provide free tours to one year level for at least one First Nations school per year."

From the Group Tours and Experiences page (worded slightly differently from the School Activities page for the same option set, and adding options not listed on the school page):

- Basic Tour (1.5h) - All Ages: same wording as above with "delicious honey" in place of "yummy honey".
- Farm tour + Hands-on Activity (2-2.5h) - All ages, additional options beyond those on the School Activities page:
  - Salad-making (2.5h): "Harvest fresh leaf greens, edible flowers, and fruits from our gardens and orchards to create a nutritious and delicious salad to share with your group. This hands-on activity is subjected to seasons and produce availability."
  - Eco-arts making (2h): "Express your creativity using natural, repurposed, and recyclable materials. This grounding, sensory activity may involve making clay sculptures, painting with natural pigments, or creating ephemeral art pieces."
- Holiday Combinations - 5 years +, "a great option for Outside School Hours Care groups":
  - Farm adventure + nature games (2h): "The adventure experience may include activities such as a treasure hunt, bird watching, seasonal observations, tasting, and tree climbing. Participants will also enjoy nature-based games that expand their senses and foster a deeper connection with the outdoors, while having fun."
  - Farm adventure + gardening (2h): "The adventure experience may include activities such as a treasure hunt, bird watching, seasonal observations, tasting, and tree-climbing. For the gardening session, depending on the season, participants may be invited to plant, make seed balls, investigate soil creatures, make compost, build insect habitats, and/or do some weeding, mulching and watering."
  - Farm adventure + Seasonal Arts and Crafts (2h): "The adventure experience may include activities such as a treasure hunt, bird watching, seasonal observations, tasting, and tree climbing. Then, participants are invited to express their creativity using natural, repurposed, and recyclable materials to design amazing artwork! This grounding, sensory activity may involve making clay sculptures, painting with natural pigments, or creating ephemeral art pieces."
  - Shelter building + Nature-based games (2.5h): "Participants are invited to flex their muscles and build some awesome kid-sized shelters in the woodlot using bamboo grown at the Farm! Then, enjoy nature-based games to expand their senses and foster a deeper connection with the outdoors."

**duration:** Basic Tour: 1.5h. Farm tour + hands-on activity: 2-2.5h. Compost-making: 2h. Propagation: 2.5h. Soil investigation: 2h. Australian Bush/Native plants exploration: 2h. Earth Connection: 2h. Salad-making: 2.5h. Eco-arts making: 2h. Mapping: 2h. Holiday combinations: 2-2.5h.

**group_size_min_max:** none listed as a stated headcount minimum or maximum for school tours as a category. Biomes and Food Security hands-on activities are stated as "in groups of up to 20 participants." All other options are priced per person with a dollar group minimum rather than a headcount minimum (see participant_thresholds and price_and_structure).

**participant_thresholds:**

- Biomes and Food Security hands-on activities: "groups of up to 20 participants"
- First Nations Schools: "free tours to one year level for at least one First Nations school per year"

## Inventory

**facilities:** food gardens; fruit orchards; food forests; recycling systems; bush tucker garden; bush regeneration area/site; market garden; kitchen garden; backyard garden; native food and medicine garden; orchard; allotment gardens; woodlot; composting systems / community compost hub.

**equipment_provided:** none listed.

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "Tours and activities are hosted by our experienced Education Team members" (Group Tours page: role described as "experienced," no formal qualification named for tour staff). Separately, for the teacher-facing professional development workshops (not the school tours themselves): presenter Emma Brindal "holds a Bachelor of Environmental Science and a Master of Education (Social Ecology)."

**accessibility_provision:** none listed.

**animal_species_held:** European honeybees (Top Bar, Langstroth and Flow hive types); native "sugarbag" bees; chickens ("over ten chickens"); black soldier flies (seasonal, "in the warmer months"); worms (worm farms). The Animals at the Farm page also describes wildlife using the site as habitat rather than being held by the farm: "over 50 species of birds," three species of flying foxes (including the Grey-headed flying-fox), and unnamed reptiles and invertebrates.

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** "These activities... have also been designed to work across curriculum and fit a number of learning outcomes" (School Activities page). "An education kit with curriculum-linked worksheets and resources for teachers is available for the 'Biomes and Food Security' Year 9 Geography unit." Teacher workshop content is stated to relate to specific numbered sections of "the Australian professional standards for teachers" (e.g. "sections 2.1, 2.2, 2.3, 3.1, 3.2, 3.3, 3.4, 4.1 and 4.2" for Successful School Gardening).

**teacher_materials:** Education kit with curriculum-linked worksheets for the Biomes and Food Security Year 9 Geography unit (referenced, not confirmed as directly downloadable from the page read). "Fact sheets – useful tips and recipes" and "Self – Guided Walks & activities" are listed in site navigation but not read as part of the four assigned pages. Teacher professional-development workshops ("Successful School Gardening", "Outdoor Learning and the Curriculum") each provide "a resources list and a certificate for 6 hours of professional development."

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** none listed.

**insurance_stated:** none listed.

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed.

**accreditations_displayed:** none listed.

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** From the School Activities page (per participant, GST inclusive, with a stated dollar group minimum): Basic Farm Tour $16 p/participant ($240 group minimum); Compost-making $20 p/participant ($300 group minimum); Propagation $25 p/participant ($375 group minimum); Soil investigation $20 p/participant ($300 group minimum); Australian Bush plants exploration $20 p/participant ($300 group minimum); Earth Connection $20 p/participant ($300 group minimum); Mapping (Biomes and Food Security option) $20 p/participant ($300 group minimum). The Group Tours and Experiences page states different, slightly higher prices for what appears to be the same option set: Basic Tour $18 per person ($270 group minimum); Compost-making $22 per person ($330 minimum); Propagation $28 per person ($420 minimum); Soil investigation $22 per person ($330 minimum); Australian Native plants exploration $22 per person ($330 minimum); Salad-making $28 per person ($420 minimum); Earth Connection $22 per person ($330 minimum); Eco-arts making $24 per person ($360 minimum). Holiday Combinations: Farm adventure + nature games $24 p/participant ($360 group minimum); Farm adventure + gardening $26 p/participant ($390 group minimum); Farm adventure + Seasonal Arts and Crafts $26 p/participant ($390 group minimum); Shelter building + Nature-based games $28 p/participant ($420 group minimum). "Prices are for weekdays only and are on a per person basis. Weekends may be arranged upon request and will have a higher cost."

**adults_free_ratio:** none listed.

**catering:** none listed.

**testimonials_from_schools:** Yes, one, on the School Activities page under "Teacher Testimonials": "OMG, our trip was absolutely amazing! The children had so much fun and learned so many new things. They had numerous experiences, from rubbing leaves between their fingers to feeding chooks, checking out a bush turkey's nest, picking and eating fresh vegetables and wild plums, exploring the inside of a beehive, and tasting the honey. I was in the group led by Richard, who was truly amazing with the kids — so patient and accommodating. The other educators were fantastic as well. We really want to thank them for their patience with our kids and for giving us the incredible opportunity to visit your farm. A special thank you to Bob! Many of the children mentioned that meeting him was one of the highlights of the trip." — Anna Megueria, Classroom Teacher, 1/2A, Runcorn Heights State School. # Perth City Farm

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "Farm Tours"; "Programs for primary schools"; "Programs for high schools"; "Interactive Workshops" (also called "Interactive demonstrations" and "Hands-on Workshops").

**activities_verbatim:**

From the Schools page, "Some of our most popular activities include" (Programs for primary schools):

- Super Seeds: Origami seed packet and seed saving
- Birds and Bees: Animals in the ecosystem
- Insects in the Garden: Learn about native bees and visit our bee hotels to see how they live
- Wonderful Worms: Learn about worm farming and plant a seed with castings

From the Primary School Brochure 2026, "ACTIVITIES/DEMOS — Applicable for LOWER PRIMARY AGE":

- Super Seeds: Origami seed packet and seed saving
- Birds and the Bees: Animals in the Ecosystem
- Insects in the Garden: Learn about native bees and visit our bee hotels to see how they live
- Wonderful Worms: learn about worm farming and plant a seed with castings
- Secrets of the soil: Learning about soil life and using magnifying glasses to find some interesting bugs
- Flower Power: Discover the magic of flowers & make a flower crown or mask to take home
- Growing Food in the City: Why we should grow seasonally/locally. Kids will also do a propagation or planting activity

From the Primary School Brochure 2026, "ACTIVITIES/DEMOS — Applicable for UPPER PRIMARY AGE":

- Growing Food in the City: Why urban agriculture is important (propagation or planting activity included)
- Super Seeds: Learn how to save seeds and why it is important
- Animals in the ecosystem: with a focus on chickens and bees
- Composting and worm farming: Making fertiliser from our organic waste
- Secrets of the soil: Learning about soil life and using magnifying glasses to find some interesting bugs

From the Schools page, "Some of our most popular activities include" (Programs for high schools):

- Sustainable food growing – Why organic may not be enough and how to make food truly sustainable
- Zero Waste living – Waste reduction and management
- Permaculture – Designing for resilience and in line with ecological rules
- Amazing Soil Life – Composting / worm farming with a focus on the importance of living soil

From the High School Excursions 2026 brochure, "ACTIVITIES/DEMOS — Applicable for HIGH SCHOOL":

- Amazing soil life – Composting / worm farming with a focus on the importance of living soil
- Sustainable food growing – Why organic may not be enough and how to make food truly sustainable
- Zero Waste Living – Waste reduction and management
- The Wonders of Native Bees – Biodiversity in the city, why it's important and how to protect our native bees
- Sustainable Cities – From urban agriculture to sustainable transport, we can focus on a topic of your choice
- Permaculture – Designing for resilience and in line with ecological rules
- Food security for the future – what does it look like and how can we contribute to it?

High School Excursions 2026 brochure, "Interactive demonstrations" (up to 40pax, 60mins):

- Waterwise Gardening
- Balcony Gardens
- Plant Propagation
- Edible Flowers OR Weeds
- Intro to Fermentation
- Low-Waste Kitchen
- Low-Waste Living
- Plastic Free Living

High School Excursions 2026 brochure, "Hands-on Workshops" (various groups/lengths):

- Edible Flowers: up to 15pax, 1hr
- Plant Propagation: up to 20pax, 2.5hrs
- Healthy & Sustainable Meal Prep: up to 15pax, 1hr
- Kokedama Plant Balls: up to 15pax, 1hr
- Recycled Succulent Planters: up to 15pax, 1hr
- Homemade Body Products: up to 15pax, 2.5hrs
- Create Your Own Terrarium: up to 15pax, 1hr
- Breadmaking; the Simple Way: up to 15pax, 2hrs
- DIY Natural Cleaning Porducts [sic]: up to 15pax, 2hrs
- Edible Gifts: up to 15pax, 2hrs

Farm Tours page, "Themes": "Food Growing: Sustainable urban food production"; "Animals: Chickens, ducks, frogs, bees & their care"; "Zero Waste: Composting, worm farms & recycling"; "History of the Farm: Be inspired by the tale".

**duration:** Farm Tours: "Our standard tour lasts for about 40-60mins (generally shorter for younger groups)." Brochure package durations: "Single 1hr tour"; "2hr visit with a tour + an activity/demo"; "3hr visit with a tour + 2x activities/demos... Each session is 50mins, allowing for 10min swaps + morning tea break"; "4hr visit with a tour + 3x activities/demos... Each session is 50mins, allowing for 10min swaps + morning tea break". Tours run "Tuesday – Thursday between 9am and 3pm."

**group_size_min_max:** "Our minimum fee is based on a group of up to 10 participants, but we can accommodate groups of up to 80 with a blend of workshops and talks/demos focusing on particular topics. Larger groups require a half or full day visit." Book-a-Tour page, "Participant Numbers": "This depends on the age of the participants... max. of 15 (7yo & under), max. of 20 (8-13yo), max. 25 (14yo & over). These limits are a suggestion to ensure everyone gets the most out of their tour. These numbers include any teachers or helpers attending."

**participant_thresholds:**

- "max. of 15 (7yo & under)"
- "max. of 20 (8-13yo)"
- "max. 25 (14yo & over)" — numbers include teachers/helpers
- "Single 1hr tour for up to 10pax — $190"
- "Single 1hr tour for up to 20pax — $260"
- "2hr visit with a tour + an activity/demo up to 20pax — $540"
- "2hr visit with a tour + an activity/demo for up to 40pax — $950"
- "3hr visit with a tour + 2x activities/demos for up to 60pax — $1800"
- "4hr visit with a tour + 3x activities/demos for up to 80pax — $2650"
- Interactive demonstrations: "up to 40pax, 60mins"
- Hands-on Workshops: various caps of 15pax or 20pax per workshop, as listed under activities_verbatim.

## Inventory

**facilities:** Living Soil Centre; Community Recycling Hub; Bush Tucker Garden; Market Garden; Native Fauna Habitat, including a Frog Bog, bee hotels, gabion structures, and bird boxes; Chook Palace; Home Demonstration Garden; an onsite cafe (Be Free).

**equipment_provided:** none listed.

**accessibility_provision:** none listed.

**animal_species_held:** chickens (hens and roosters), ducks (Chook Palace); native birds, small reptiles, frogs, bees, other insects (Native Fauna Habitat).

## Commercial

"LARGER GROUPS / FULL CO-HORT: 2hr visit with a tour + an activity/demo for up to 40pax $950; 3hr visit with a tour + 2x activities/demos for up to 60pax $1800 (Each session is 50mins, allowing for 10min swaps + morning tea break); 4hr visit with a tour + 3x activities/demos for up to 80pax $2650 (Each session is 50mins, allowing for 10min swaps + morning tea break)." High School brochure adds "Interactive demonstrations – up to 40pax, 60mins" priced $530 or $580 depending on topic, and "Hands-on Workshops – various groups/lengths" priced $680 to $1250 depending on topic (see activities_verbatim for the topic-by-price breakdown).

If you CANCEL with more than 21 DAYS NOTICE: 50% of the booking will be held, and 50% refunded. If cancelled within 21 days: full payment is forfeited. Alternatively, you can opt to CHANGE DATES (within a 6 month window) for a $100 fee." No weather-specific policy is stated.

**booking_lead_time:** none listed as a specific number of days; the brochures publish only the specific Tuesdays and Thursdays open for booking, by term, through the 2026 school year (e.g. "Available on Tuesdays & Thursdays (10th Feb – 26th March)" for Term 1).

If the number of tour participants increases, let us know before your tour." Contact: education@perthcityfarm.org.au, 08 9325 7229, 1 City Farm Place, East Perth WA 6004.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "School Excursions"; "School Incursions"; "Planting Session"; (also listed on the farm-school page, outside the Prep–Year 6 programme detailed below: "Little Green Thumbs", ages 0-5, and "Early Learning Programs", ages 3-5)

**activities_verbatim:** Excursions open with "a guided farm walk, in which they follow educational signs and discover key features of regenerative farming, composting, bees and how we manage the farm", then two rotations chosen from five: "Worm Farming & Compost: Students learn about what goes into making a good compost, as well as seeing samples. We discuss the role of compost in organic farming and why it is so important to be composting food scraps at home. We then look at the different worm farms we have on the farm, and the children do a hands-on activity with the worm farms and compost bays"; "Seeds & Bees: Students discover seeds growing on the farm and learn about the role of pollinators in natural ecosystems. Children can spot our insects and pollinators, we talk about making homes for them, and they can see our bee hotels, native bees and honey bee hives on the farm. They have hands-on experience with a sample of seeds collected from the farm which they can take home to grow"; "Herbs and Growing a Kitchen Garden: The students are encouraged to explore our kitchen garden, and are invited to see, touch, smell and taste a wide selection of culinary and medicinal herbs, including edible flowers. The students have the opportunity to prepare a herb blend of their choice to take home and use"; "Food and Fibre: We will discuss the food we grow on the farm that has a dual purpose as a fibre that can make things such as rope, baskets and clothing. They will see it grown, handle the raw material and see samples of products we can make. They get to experience weaving first hand and make a woven product"; "Making Healthy Soil + How to Make a No-Dig Garden Bed: The students look at our hugelkultur bed, raised beds, and in ground beds and we talk about natural ways that we build healthy soil through composting, worm farms, fertigation system, soil amendments and minimal till methods of farming. They are able to make a mini no-dig garden of their own to take home". Incursions offer a choice of three: "Exploring Herbs: Students explore herbs and their varying uses, discovering the wide range of culinary and medicinal benefits. We look at native and non-native herbs and how you can grow herbs at home. Students receive propagated herbs to grow in the classroom along with samples of herbs that they can dry and use"; "Farm-to-Fork Journey: Students map out the journey that food takes as it travels from farm to fork in varying situations. We consider environmental and sustainable considerations, the differences between organic and non-organic foods and the health benefits of eating fresh food. Students get to plant a pot of microgreens to grow at school or home"; "Living Soils: Students dive into soil exploration, record their findings and make predictions. We discover what makes great soil and how we can turn all soils into great soil for growing. Students make a liquid fertiliser from plants that they can watch over the coming weeks as it develops. This can then be used at school or at home"

**duration:** Excursion: the schools page states "10 am - 1pm"; the linked Rezdy booking page states the same product runs "210 minutes" (approximately 3.5 hours), a discrepancy between the two sources on the same offer, not reconciled on either page. Incursion: "Sessions run for approximately 45 minutes to 1 hour" on the schools page detail text, though the top-of-page summary box states "60-90 Minutes per session". Planting Session: "1 - 2 hours. Start time can be adjusted to suit your needs"

**group_size_min_max:** Excursion: base package "up to 30 students", "\$24.50 each extra student, up to 55 participants in total", "Capacity: Maximum 55 students per session"; rotations: "students are divided into groups of 15-20 participants"; no minimum group size stated for excursions or incursions

**participant_thresholds:** "Maximum 55 students per session" (excursion); "Each group requires a minimum of two supervising adults, with the expectation that younger year levels may require additional support"; groups of 15-20 for rotations

## Inventory

**facilities:** guided farm walk with "educational signs"; kitchen garden; worm farms and compost bays; bee hotels, native bees and honey bee hives; hugelkultur bed, raised beds and in-ground beds; fertigation system; "Free parking for cars and buses onsite"; the separate Farm Tours page additionally describes "the mandala garden, 6-bay compost system, pollinator area, fruit tree pathway, food forest, and herb and flower gardens" as the areas typically included on a farm tour, though that page is not one of the four counted sources

**equipment_provided:** none listed for excursions or incursions; the Planting Session states "We bring the seedlings and everything needed for a healthy garden"

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "passionate, qualified farm educators" (schools page); "guided by passionate and qualified Farm Educators" (farm-school page); no named qualification, certification, or training body given

**accessibility_provision:** none listed on the four pages read. The home page states the farm welcomes "all ages, backgrounds, and abilities" including "NDIS participants" as a matter of general audience, not a stated physical or programme accessibility provision

**animal_species_held:** none listed by species name. The only living creatures named across the programme content are worms (worm farms) and bees (native bees and honey bee hives); no livestock, poultry, or other farm animals are mentioned on any of the four pages read

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** "immersive, QLD curriculum-aligned excursions and incursions provide hands-on depth in Biological Science, HASS, and Health & PE" (school-programs page); the farm-school page adds "Comprehensive, curriculum-linked Primary School Excursion and Incursion programs focusing on Biological Science, HASS (Humanities and Social Sciences), and Sustainability"; no specific curriculum codes or year-level outcome statements given

**teacher_materials:** none listed

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** Excursion: "\$750.00 (inc GST) for a group of up to 30 students | \$24.50 each extra student, up to 55 participants in total". Incursion: "\$650.00 + GST per session", "Back-to-back sessions: 2nd session only \$250.00 + GST". Planting Session: no price stated, "Contact us to schedule a planting session"

**catering:** none listed

**cancellation_and_weather:** from the Rezdy booking page for the primary school excursion product: cancellations "14+ days prior" to the visit receive a full refund, "7-13 days prior" incur a "50% cancellation fee", and "less than 7 days" before the visit receive "No refund issued"; "Exceptions to this policy may be considered in unforeseen circumstances"; no weather-specific policy stated on any of the four pages read

**price_visible_without_asking:** yes, both excursion and incursion prices are stated directly on the schools page and repeated on the Rezdy booking page

**testimonials_from_schools:** yes, four dated to one school: "Excited! I saw cool things, like I got to hold a worm!" (N.H., Class 2, Albany Creek State School); "Everything! My favorite thing was everything that we did!" (A.C., Class 2, Albany Creek State School); "It was awesome! I loved visiting the farm, I might come back with my family." (E.C., Class 2, Albany Creek State School); "I loved it! I loved holding the worms!" (H.A., Class 2, Albany Creek State School)

**title_and_opening_line:** Title: "Hands-on Education in Food Growing, Soil Health, & Sustainability". Opening line: "Welcome to our Primary School Programs, where we bridge the gap between the classroom and the natural world."

## Note

Animal feed making: no page read for this venue publishes anything resembling a recipe-based animal feed mixing or making activity. Green Thumb Farm is a horticulture and composting-led education venue; it holds no named livestock or poultry species on any of the four pages read, and the only creature-related hands-on content is a worm-farming and compost activity ("the children do a hands-on activity with the worm farms and compost bays") and a bee/pollinator-habitat activity, neither of which involves feeding an animal. The closest analogue to a "making" activity is soil and compost making, explicitly framed around plant and soil health rather than animal feed: "Students learn about what goes into making a good compost" and "we talk about natural ways that we build healthy soil through composting, worm farms, fertigation system, soil amendments and minimal till methods of farming", culminating in a take-home product ("They are able to make a mini no-dig garden of their own to take home") that is a soil/garden product, not a feed product. No wording using "feed", "feeding", or "mix" in connection with an animal appears on any of the four pages read.

Students will learn about the different roles bees hold in the hive, their unique communication methods and the valuable contribution bees make to the economy and ecosystem" (Live Bee Show); "Students will explore the ginger growing industry and learn the Buderim Ginger story as they tour Australia's only ginger factory. Explore thirteen different cultural geographical zones of the world on the Overboard Boat Ride. This attraction is fully indoors and has more than 200 animated puppets" (Overboard Boat Ride); "Hop aboard on one of Queensland's original cane trains and take a step back in time. 'Moreton' enjoys taking students on a leisurely ride through our rainforest and Ginger Factory site as they learn about the history of Yandina and the local ginger industry" (Ginger Train Ride); "This 30-minute self-drive activity is an exciting new augmented reality experience at The Ginger Factory which is completely free but better enjoyed with a ZOG Activity Pack. Simply follow the route and complete activities, quizzes, and challenges along your journey into the rainforest. You will encounter characters from the ZOG stories, uncover hidden pictures and images and collect stars as you learn different lessons - just like ZOG did at Dragon School!" (Zog: A Forest Adventure); "Walk beneath hundreds of suspended rainbow umbrellas and listen to the relaxing soundscapes as you explore our beautiful subtropical gardens and rainforest" (Rainbrella Project & Soundscape); optional extras "Decorate your own gingerbread person. Includes icing & lollies. Take home or enjoy at The Ginger Factory" (DYO Gingerbread Person, \$7 per student)

**duration:** Live Bee Show: 25 minutes. Ginger Growing & Processing Tour: 25 minutes. Overboard Boat Ride: 15 minutes. Ginger Train Ride: 15 minutes. Zog: A Forest Adventure: 30 minutes, self-drive. ## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** yes, a full "Risk Assessment" section is published in the school packages document, covering the Live Bee Show ("Guests are separated from the bee population by a shade cloth enclosed area which prevents interference with the bee's regular flight path... poses some risk of stings"), the Train Ride ("Each carriage has a security gate and guests are regularly instructed via the PA system to 'remain seated at all times, keeping arms and legs inside the carriages'. For groups with a high child to adult ratio we require that a least one adult rides in each carriage for supervisory purposes"), Overboard ("Each boat must contain a supervising adult. Each boat can hold up to 9 adults or equivalent"), First Aid, Other Risks & Hazards, and Emergency Procedures

**insurance_stated:** none listed

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed

**accreditations_displayed:** none listed as an external accreditation. The document states "The Ginger Factory complies with all QFES standards for emergency evacuation and our staff receive regular training updates on the implementation of these procedures", a standards-compliance statement rather than a displayed accreditation

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** Live Bee Show: \$8 per student. Ginger Growing & Processing Tour: \$8 per student. Overboard Boat Ride: \$7 per student. Ginger Train Ride: \$7 per student. Bundle "2 x Rides": \$12 per student (minimum 1.5 hour). Bundle "2 x Rides, 1 x Educational Tour": \$17 per student (minimum 3 hours). Bundle "2 x Rides, 2 x Educational Tours": \$25 per student (minimum 4 hours). Self-guided Zog: A Forest Adventure and Rainbrella Project & Soundscape are free of charge. A "Business/Marketing discussion (15-20min)" is listed at \$30 total

**adults_free_ratio:** "Teachers and supervising adults are free of charge for the tours and rides", subject to the stated ratios of one adult per 8 students (ELC & Primary) or one adult per 10 students (Secondary); early education children under 4 pay for every child while staff and adult supervisors remain free

**catering:** "Self-provided morning tea / lunch: Students bring their own packed morning tea and/or lunch to enjoy." Catering (minimum 10 students): "Option 1: Boxed lunch \$15 per student" ("Box with mixed sandwiches, seasonal fruit, vegetable sticks, crisps, apple popper and lollies"); "Option 2: Aussie BBQ plate \$18 per student" ("Plate with BBQ beef brisket, chicken tenderloin, corn on the cob, coleslaw, pickled ginger and turkish bread"); "Option 3: Platters (serves 10 people)" comprising Fruit Platter \$85.00, Sandwich Platter \$100.00, Hot Platter \$100.00, and Wrap Platter \$100.00; beverage option "Buderim Ginger Refresher Station - \$3.50 per person"; "Please note: no changes can be made to menu | Please contact us with any dietaries or allergies"

**cancellation_and_weather:** none listed as a cancellation policy. On weather, the only statement is "The Ginger Factory is an all-weather venue"

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** via the Group Enquiries page enquiry form (fields for name, email, phone, number of people, date, and enquiry details); phone +61 7 5447 8431 or 1800 067 686; email enquiries@gingerfactory.com.au

**price_visible_without_asking:** yes, per-activity and bundle pricing for school groups is published directly in the downloadable "GF School Packages 2026/2027" document linked from the School Groups section, with no enquiry required to see prices

**downloadable_pack:** yes, "GF School Packages 2026/2027", a six-page PDF covering educational tours, rides, self-guided activities, bundle pricing, catering options, terms and conditions, risk assessment, and a park map

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed

**title_and_opening_line:** Title: "School Groups". Opening line: "The Ginger Factory offers education and entertainment all in one great location! Our education programs can be modified to complement your current classroom learning and are suitable for primary and secondary students."

## Note

The attractions page states general admission is "Free Admission", with tours, rides, and activities individually priced or bundled, including for school groups. Across all four sources and the linked school packages document, the venue publishes no activity resembling animal feed making, meaning children following a recipe to mix or make feed and then feeding it to an animal. The only animal-related school activity is the "Live Bee Show" (25 minutes, \$8 per student), which is described purely as an observational talk: "Experience the fascinating world of the honeybee as our beekeepers share the hidden secrets of the hive. Students will learn about the different roles bees hold in the hive, their unique communication methods and the valuable contribution bees make to the economy and ecosystem." It contains no reference to hand feeding, mixing, or preparing feed. The venue's other named "making" activity, "DYO Gingerbread Person" ("Decorate your own gingerbread person. Includes icing & lollies. Take home or enjoy at The Ginger Factory"), is a human-food decorating activity, not animal feed making, and is noted here only to rule it out. No petting, hand-feeding, or animal-contact activity of any kind is published for school groups; the only animal species named is the honeybee, encountered behind "a shade cloth enclosed area" rather than through direct contact.

Activity-level: "Bird Feeding" is "a fully accessible activity"; the Cedar Creek Falls 500m "Lookout Trail is rated grade 1 and suitable for wheelchair access, with paved concrete and wooden boardwalk paths" while the Base Trail is "grade 3" and "not wheelchair accessible"; Pickleball courts are "currently accessed via steps or a grassy incline"; Dig It is described as "perfect for people of all abilities"; Mini Golf is "not wheelchair accessible" but "you can easily view the course from the Terrace Cafe"

**title_and_opening_line:** No school-specific page exists to title. The nearest page, "Groups", opens: "Unforgettable Events for Every Group" / "Whether you're planning a high-energy team building day, a milestone birthday bash, or a professional conference with a difference, Thunderbird Park offers the perfect blend of nature, adventure, and hospitality."

## Note

Thunderbird Park is an adventure and events park (ziplines, treetop ropes courses, mini excavators, gemstone fossicking, mini golf, pickleball, laser skirmish, waterfall walk) with one small nature activity, the free daily bird feeding session, rather than a farm or animal-education venue. Its own site navigation has no schools, education, or excursions section; the "Groups & Events" section is built entirely around team building, birthday parties and conferences. "School holidays" appears repeatedly on the site but always refers to the school-holiday calendar period for family/leisure visitors, not a school-group education booking.

On the specific research question of animal feed making: the only animal feeding activity published is "Bird Feeding", and the page is explicit that the park, not the visitor, prepares the feed: "At Thunderbird Park, we feed them a special nectar mix that keeps them healthy and strong!" The page further warns "Giving them the wrong food, like bread or seeds, can hurt their beaks and make them sick", framing feed composition as something staff control rather than something visitors, let alone schoolchildren, mix or make. Visitors watch and get "up close" while "guided by our friendly and experienced staff" as the birds feed; no wording anywhere on the pages read describes children following a recipe or mixing ingredients to prepare the feed. Nothing resembling animal feed making is published.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "School Excursion Program"; "Kindy/Daycare Program"; a holiday excursion programme for Outside School Hours Care organisations (not given its own name on the page)

**activities_verbatim:** "Horse Experience – a connection with horses through touching and grooming"; "Tractor Trailer ride & learn about gardening / vegetables"; "Smaller Animals – Handling, feeding and observing typically of goats, chickens, guinea pigs, rabbits."; "Large Animals – meeting the larger animals: horses, donkey, sheep, pigs, and alpacas."; "Feathers and Fibres – exploring the non-food products that our animals provide"; "Orienteering – Learning to use mapping skills around the farm"; "Paddock to Plate – investigating practical ways our food is produced."; "Egg exploration – looking at the various stage of embryo growth (seasonal)"; "Animal feed making – students following a "recipe" to make & then feed our animals"; two further rotations offered beyond the core eight: "Weed Investigation – discovering what problems weeds might cause for farmers, the animals, and the environment." and "Farm Design Technology – explore how technology can be used in a farm environment"; the page states "currently over 8 to choose from" and that teachers select which rotations to include. The Kindy/Daycare Program is described as offering "opportunities to meet and interact with our animals which can be fed and cuddled(small animals)/touched" and "an opportunity to touch and brush a pony at the stables". A separate marketing passage lower on the page describes the Kindy offer as "connection with a horse or pony through brushing, a ride on a tractor-trailer where they are taught about gardening and vegetables; handling and feeding of small animals such as goats, chickens and lambs...meeting horses, donkeys and sheep; a bush scavenger hunt and paddock to plate exercise".

**duration:** "A program duration of 3-4 hours with time allowed for morning tea & lunch." Kindy/Daycare Program: "Excursions last for 3-3.5 hours"

**group_size_min_max:** "Each excursion group will rotate through the experiences in sub-groups of a maximum 20 children every 30-40mins." The Conditions of Entry & Risk Assessment document (Rev 11, 1 January 2025) states "The usual group sizes are up to 25kids plus 4-5 adult helpers minimum with one being first aid trained. There will be one TG staff member per group." No minimum whole-school-group size, and no stated maximum for the total school visit, is given on the excursions page itself.

**participant_thresholds:** sub-groups rotate at a "maximum 20 children every 30-40mins"; the risk assessment document adds "up to 25kids plus 4-5 adult helpers minimum with one being first aid trained" as the usual group size, and states "A ratio of 1:4 for parent helpers is required to ensure that adequate supervision is provided on farm at all times" (worded "A ideal ratio of 1:4" in the current Rev 11 document).

## Inventory

**facilities:** "Access to a large fenced outdoor area which is semi shaded for morning tea & lunch"; covered infrastructure described in the cancellation section as allowing "many activities to be carried out under cover"; tractor-trailer, stables, party shed, arena/round yard, tree house under the main fig tree, and (per the risk assessment document) a jumping castle offered "for larger holiday groups" and a miniature train ride; an on-site café is named in the site's main navigation ("Cafe & Coffee") but was not detailed on the pages read

**equipment_provided:** none listed as general equipment for the school programme; the risk assessment document notes, for the horse rotation only, "Participants are provided with a helmet prior to..." (sentence continues describing the horse activity)

**accessibility_provision:** none listed on the four pages read; a separate "Accessibility" page exists in the site's main navigation but falls outside the fixed four-page set for this research pass

**animal_species_held:** alpacas, bunnies, chickens, donkeys, goats, guinea pigs, horses, pigs, sheep (Our Animals page); the excursions page also names "lambs" once, in the general Kindy description, alongside goats, chickens, horses, donkeys and sheep

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** yes. "Once a date has been confirmed, we provide a quotation, a comprehensive risk assessment, and other support documentation to make it as easy as possible for teachers and organisers" (excursions page). The Documents page links a "Risk Assessment" download, retrieved directly as a PDF titled "Conditions of Entry & Risk Assessment" (Rev 11, dated 1/1/2025), a nine-page document combining signed conditions-of-entry terms with an itemised hazard table across 12 numbered activities (welcome and farm safety guidelines, animal handling, wagon ride, a horse-related rotation, two further shed/arena rotations, a combined "hard feed, orienteering, Paddock to Plate, Feathers and Fibres, egg explore" rotation item, jumping castle, mini train ride, tree house, lunch, and farewell), each row giving the risk, a consequence rating, hazard controls, likelihood and an overall risk level.

**insurance_stated:** a "Public Liability Insurance" document is listed and downloadable from the Documents page; a "WorkCover Certificate" is also listed there

**child_safety_checks_stated:** a "Child Risk Management Strategy" document is listed on the Documents page; it was not opened, as it is a general WHS strategy document rather than an information pack, price list or risk assessment PDF, so falls outside this research pass's scope for following linked documents. No explicit Working with Children Check statement appears on the pages read.

**accreditations_displayed:** the Documents page lists an "NDIS Provider Registration Certificate", a "Rabbit Exhibition License", a "WorkCover Certificate" and an "Emergency and Disaster Management" document as available for download; their contents were not opened

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** none listed for the school excursion itself; pricing is by individual quotation only: "Once a date has been confirmed, we provide a quotation...". A general "Tours Price List" PDF (dated "From: Jun-25"), linked from the Documents page and unrelated to the school programme, prices the venue's general-public tour range: Public 1hr \$22; Private 1hr \$87 (1 person)/\$47 (2)/\$42 (3-4)/\$37 (5-8)/\$32 (more than 8); Private 2hr \$174/\$87/\$82/\$78/\$72 across the same group-size bands; Private 3hr \$234/\$117/\$112/\$107/\$102; Train \$7 (1)/\$6 (2)/\$5 (3 or more); Pony ride \$15; Pony ride (extended) \$30.

This could be due to: Adverse weather conditions; Government or education department regulations; Physical conditions at the farm; Staffing emergency." "In any of these situations we will contact you at least by the day prior to the planned excursion to discuss and advise. We will then work with you to arrange an alternative date." Cancellation by the organiser: "More than 1 month notice (\$50 admin fee)"; "Less than 1 month notice (the greater of \$100 or 10% of the quote)." On weather specifically: "we have built up infrastructure to allow many activities to be carried out under cover. This means that we will only cancel if we expect significant rain or wind. If showers are expected, visitors are advised to bring suitable wet weather gear (coats, boots, umbrellas). In some situations we may need to swap rotation choices if horse riding is not possible due to safety."

**booking_lead_time:** no minimum lead time is stated; the page notes as context that "many groups plan and book their excursions up to a year in advance", and separately that "You should then receive a quote within 7 days which you can accept to confirm your booking."

Phone: 32894257. Email: info@trevenaglen.com.au.

**price_visible_without_asking:** partial. School excursion pricing is quote-only and not published on the pages read; general tour pricing (Public 1hr \$22, pony rides \$15/\$30, mini train \$5-\$7, gift cards \$60-\$200) is published on the Tours page and the downloadable Tours Price List.

The "Risk Assessment" (Conditions of Entry & Risk Assessment) and "Public Liability Insurance" documents on the same page are freely downloadable without login.

**testimonials_from_schools:** Brighton State School: "Thank you to you and your team for a great day at the farm! Everyone had a great time and it was evident that each farmer helped us see us much as possible in the short time we were there. It was also very aligned with our curriculum units which was amazing." Homeschool Group: "Our homeschool group had at amazing time on our excursion to Trevena Glen. We loved the way the staff showed true respect to the animals making sure the children were calm before approaching any animals for pats or feeding...The tractor wagon ride at the end was a big hit." Prep Group: "We took our three Prep classes for an excursion and it was an incredible experience! Everything ran smoothly and the students had the most wonderful day. Thank you!!"

**title_and_opening_line:** Title: "School Excursion Program". Opening line: "Looking for one of the most truly memorable school excursion ideas in Brisbane? Trevena Glen Farm, just 40 minutes from central Brisbane, provides a tailored farm experience for everyone that is "hands on", enjoyable and educational."

## Note

Special research question on animal feed making: Trevena Glen Farm publishes this as a named rotation, distinct from plain animal feeding elsewhere in the programme. The excursions page states, verbatim: "Animal feed making – students following a "recipe" to make & then feed our animals." This is listed as one of the "currently over 8" rotation choices teachers select from, alongside plain animal-contact rotations such as "Smaller Animals – Handling, feeding and observing" and "Large Animals – meeting the larger animals". The risk assessment document (Rev 11) corroborates this as an active rotation, grouping it under item 7, "Rotation choices, Hard feed, Orienteering, Paddock to Plate, Feathers & Fibres, Egg Explore", with a stated risk of "Allergy to feed or materials" and a control that "TG staff member to be advised if there are visitors who suffer from anaphylaxis shock. It is recommended that these visitors bypass this aspect of the rotation." No recipe content, ingredient list, or further detail of the feed-making method is published on the pages read.

**accessibility_provision:** "Our pathways are designed to be wheelchair and stroller-friendly, and we have accessible parking spaces available."; "90% of our animal farm experience is accessible to wheelchairs, walkers, ect."; "The eating area is wheelchair accessible too."; "The part of the farm that is tricky for wheelchairs is the mini golf area."; sensory provision stated as "we strive to create a sensory-friendly environment by minimizing excessive noise levels and providing quiet areas for those who need a break"; each animal carries "a QR code linked to an audio recording" and the welcome sign has "a QR code linked to an audio recording" for visitors who are deaf or have low hearing; amenities are "signed in Braille" though "we do not supply maps in Braille"; assistance animals require "48 hours notice" and must be "certified" with "identification jackets and paperwork"; the venue partners with the Cerge app for "Know Before You Go" accessibility information; from the terms and conditions, a recommended (not admission-linked) child-to-adult supervision ratio is stated: "Children under 5 years: 1 adult to 3 children", "Children 5-8 years: 1 adult to 6 children", "Children 9-15 years: 1 adult to 10 children"

**animal_species_held:** llamas, alpacas, horses, camels (named individual "Gypsy the Camel"), pigs, goats, sheep and lambs, rabbits, guinea pigs, chickens, ducks; the come-to-the-farm page also states visitors can "have a go at milking our fake cow", indicating no live cattle for milking; about-us states the farm was "originally started with just a few chickens and a goat" and is "registered members of the Australian Alpaca Association and Llama Association"

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** none listed

**teacher_materials:** none listed

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** none listed

**insurance_stated:** none listed

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed

**accreditations_displayed:** none in the form of a named external certification body. The venue claims recognition rather than accreditation: "For seven years in a row, White Ridge Farm has been recognised as one of Queensland's best family experiences!" and "With award-winning inclusivity and accessibility, we make sure everyone feels welcome". Separately, About Us states the farm is a "registered member" of the "Australian Alpaca Association and Llama Association", a breed-association membership rather than a visitor-safety or education accreditation.

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** the Schools and Vacation Care page states only "Email us for further information and pricing"; no school-specific price is published. The general admission schedule published sitewide (Book Now, Home, and other pages) is: Child (2-17 years, under 2 free) \$17; Concession/Student/Pensioner \$27; Adult \$34; Family pass (2 adults + 2 children) \$98; Annual Child pass \$55; Annual Adult pass \$90, all marked "*Excluding group bookings"

**adults_free_ratio:** none listed for school-group free entry. The terms and conditions state a general visitor supervision recommendation, not a free-adult-entry ratio: "Children under 5 years: 1 adult to 3 children", "Children 5-8 years: 1 adult to 6 children", "Children 9-15 years: 1 adult to 10 children"

**catering:** none listed as a school catering package. General visitor provision: an on-site "kiosk onsite that has gluten free options"; "we also allow you to bring your own food and are welcome to the seating area"; a "BYO Picnic Area" is listed among general attractions

**cancellation_and_weather:** from the Terms and Conditions: "Tickets are non-refundable"; "There will be no ticket refunds or rain checks for inclement weather, animals resting, off-exhibit animals or animals sleeping"; "White Ridge Farm has the authority to close the Farm or any part thereof, including any animal exhibit (animals off display) or show, at any time for reasons of adverse weather, equipment failure, maintenance and construction, or in the interests of safety and will not be liable for any loss or expense incurred by a guest as the result of such closure"

**booking_lead_time:** none listed for school bookings. General visitor booking policy states the opposite of a lead time requirement: "Bookings are NOT essential. You can ALWAYS purchase your tickets at the gate on the day" and "We never book out." Assistance-animal visits ask for "48 hours notice" and guide-dog visits ask that guests "contact us at least 72 hours prior to your visit", but neither is a school booking lead time.

The farm was clean and the kids had a really great time. We will definitely be back next year. Thanks again."

No page read (Schools and Vacation Care, Book Now, Animal Experiences Brisbane/come-to-the-farm, Accessibility, About Us, Terms and Conditions) publishes anything resembling animal feed making or mixing, meaning a recipe-following activity where children combine ingredients to make animal feed before feeding it out. What is published is plain animal feeding only: the come-to-the-farm page states "Hand feed all of our amazing animals including a camel!" and "Feed the chickens and ducks"; the schools page caption reads "School Children feeding Gypsy the Camel at White Ridge Farm"; the accessibility page states "whether it's feeding the llamas, petting the rabbits, or taking a guided tour" and shows a photo captioned "man in wheelchair feeding a horse", with the caption naming the pair as "Dane feeding Gypsy"; the Terms and Conditions state "Animal food is provided at the White Ridge Farm Ticket office for all animals. No other food is permitted to be fed to the animals," which describes farm-supplied, ready-made feed handed to visitors to feed out, not a mixing or recipe activity. No recipe, ingredient list, mixing station, or "make your own feed" wording appears anywhere across the pages read.

note: "The Education page serves both the schools/education role and the programme role, since it is the only page describing the "Choose Your Own Adventure Tour" that a booked school group runs; the linked 2026 Education Program Brochure PDF was followed as a school-specific information pack and read for the same two roles, adding the suggested itinerary and per-animal detail the web page omits. Opening Hours & Prices served the pricing/booking role, though it prices general admission and add-on experiences rather than the school rate, which sits only on the Education page/brochure. Interactive Experiences served the general attractions role. Facilities was read as one small extra page, beyond the fixed four, because neither of the four names on-site facilities such as catering, parking or amenities, which the Inventory section requires; it is cited only for that field. Vacation Care Groups was also read as an extra page to confirm the school programme (Education) is a distinct, term-time offer from the holiday-programme (Vacation Care) product, and is cited only where that distinction matters."
---

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "Choose Your Own Adventure Tour"

**activities_verbatim:** "You select five of our native animals and your Ranger will talk about and engage with your students at their own level on a variety of selected topics such as animal adaptations, ecosystems or biodiversity, with time for students to interact with each animal." The brochure describes the same tour as choosing "up to 4 animals for your study (depending on the age level of students)" from a set illustrated as kangaroo, black cockatoo, crocodile, dingo and echidna, and states "Our Rangers can talk about and engage with your students on a number of topics including animal adaptations and features, ecosystems, animal reproduction, the needs of living things, food webs and caring for our biodiversity." On the level of contact: "The level of interaction is dependent upon the animal and may include patting, holding or feeding. Some animal groups due to safety have no level of interaction." The brochure's suggested itinerary reads: "9:30am Arrive at Billabong Sanctuary; 9:45am Morning tea and toilet break; 10:00am Guided Educational Tour begins (STEM based); 11:05am 5min break for the children to use our toilet facilities - prior to the final crocodile feeding presentation; 11:30am Tour concludes - supervision free-time commences; 12 noon Lunch break and / or depart Billabong Sanctuary." The web page adds: "Classes studying related topics such as tourism or geography are also welcome—we will work with you to create a tour that complements your classroom teaching."

**duration:** "The tour will go for approximately 1½ hours and comprise five 15 minute sessions" (brochure); the suggested itinerary places the guided tour from 10:00am to 11:30am within a visit that can start at 9:30am and run to 12 noon, "If necessary we can accommodate the whole grade on one day by setting up stations with our rangers."

**group_size_min_max:** none listed as a minimum or maximum for the school Education tour itself; the separate Vacation Care Groups product (school-holiday, not term-time) states "Minimum of 15 children" and "we can accommodate only one group per day"

**participant_thresholds:** none listed as a numeric group-size threshold for the school Education tour; the brochure states the whole-grade accommodation option is by arrangement ("If necessary we can accommodate the whole grade on one day by setting up stations with our rangers")

## Inventory

**facilities:** the Facilities page (read as an extra source) lists "PARKING", "EFTPOS", "SHOP", "FOOD SERVICES" (Blinky Bill's Café, described as "open all day, offers freshly made light meals, Devonshire tea and coffee featuring homemade scones, cold drinks and yummy desserts", with grill lunch options from 10am, and free gas barbecues for visitors' own food), "POOL" (a "resort-style pool, open every day till 3:30pm"), "GUEST SERVICES" (umbrellas, strollers, high chairs free of charge; "Main show areas are wheelchair-accessible"; a "Sandcruiser All-terrain chair" and a "MOOV 3-wheel stroller" available), "AMENITIES" (showers, biosystem toilets, baby change tables), "WIFI" (free, "around the main show area and photo hut"), and "FIRST AID" ("All permanent staff regularly receive up-to-date first aid training"); the education brochure separately states "SHADED PICNIC AREA: An area will be allocated to your group for the duration of your stay" and "SHADED RESERVED AREA: An area will be allocated to your group for the duration of your stay"

**equipment_provided:** none listed as school-specific equipment; general Facilities-page provision (umbrellas, strollers, high chairs, the Sandcruiser chair, the MOOV stroller) is for general visitors, not stated as part of the school programme

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "your Ranger will talk about and engage with your students"; the brochure adds contact detail for "Billabong Sanctuary's Curator Ray Stephens"; no formal qualification, certification or training standard for rangers is stated on the pages read, beyond the general Facilities-page statement that "All permanent staff regularly receive up-to-date first aid training"

**accessibility_provision:** none listed on the Education page or brochure specific to school groups; the Facilities page states, for general visitors, "Main show areas are wheelchair-accessible" and describes the free Sandcruiser all-terrain chair and the refundable-deposit MOOV 3-wheel stroller

**animal_species_held:** koalas, kangaroos (including joeys), cassowaries (named individuals Hope and Troppo), meerkats, black kites (named individual Yasi), red-tailed black cockatoos (named individual Waru), lorikeets, crocodiles (freshwater and estuarine, including baby crocodiles held by visitors), pythons, blue-tongued lizards, whistling ducks, Krefft's turtles, long-finned eels (Interactive Experiences page); the brochure's animal-choice illustration additionally names dingo and echidna among the animals a school group may select for the tour

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** "Our educational tours are based on the Australian Curriculum across all Key Learning Areas and incorporate inquiry-based learning and the 5 E's model through engaging, exploring and explaining." The brochure repeats this in near-identical wording: "Our educational tours are based on the Australian Curriculum across all key learning areas and incorporate inquiry-based learning and the 5 E's model including engaging, exploring and explaining." It adds: "Classes studying topics such as Geography or Tourism can be catered for."

**teacher_materials:** the 2026 Education Program Brochure (PDF) is offered as a download from the Education page ("Billabong Sanctuary Education Plan DOWNLOAD 2026 EDUCATION PROGRAM BROCHURE") and was read in full for this profile; an older "Education-Flyer-2023.pdf" is also linked from the same page but was not followed, being superseded by the 2026 brochure

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** the brochure states, under "Available upon request – an electronic copy of our": "Certificate of Currency for Public Liability" and "Risk Assessment for School Excursions", both available on request rather than published or linked as a document

**insurance_stated:** "Available upon request – an electronic copy of our Certificate of Currency for Public Liability" (brochure); no insurer name or coverage figure is published

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed on the pages read

**accreditations_displayed:** "The Sanctuary enjoys Advanced accreditation with the Australian Ecotourism Association for the role we play in protecting the environment through education." "The welfare of our animals is paramount and this is recognised through animal welfare accreditation with the Zoo and Aquarium Association." "We are involved in captive breeding programmes for several endangered species including the Southern cassowary, Greater bilby, Southern hairy nosed wombat and the recently classified - koala."

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** education page and brochure both give the school rate. Education page: "\$26 per student (Optional bag of animal feed- \$3 per student)"; "One FREE adult for every 10 students"; "\$42 for each additional adult"; "Club Billabong Membership entitlements do not apply"; "1 carer FOC per disabled person in wheelchair". The brochure states the same student and feed price but a lower additional-adult figure: "\$26 per student, Kangaroo / duck feed \$3", "One FREE adult for every 10 students", "\$40 for each additional adult" (the brochure's figure of \$40 differs from the web page's \$42; both are transcribed as published, without reconciling which is current). General admission, published on the separate Opening Hours & Prices page rather than the school rate: Adults \$54; Children (4-16 yrs) \$37; Family (2A + 3C) \$152; Additional child \$25; Pensioners \$49; Seniors/Students \$49; a 10 per cent "Group Discount" applies to "Your Group of 10 or More".

**adults_free_ratio:** "One FREE adult for every 10 students" (education page and brochure, consistent); "1 carer FOC per disabled person in wheelchair" (education page only)

**catering:** "OPTIONAL CATERING (Primary Schools only): Morning Tea: \$12.50 per student, Biscuit platter, fruit platter, water, cordial; Sausage Sizzle: \$20 per student (min. 15 students), Sausage on bread, fruit platter, water, cordial" (education page). The brochure gives a lower Sausage Sizzle price: "Sausage Sizzle \$18 per student (min. 15 students)" with the same morning tea price of "\$12.50 per student"; both figures are transcribed as published. The brochure adds: "Please ensure that if you bring your morning tea or lunch, that they are in animal proof containers."

**cancellation_and_weather:** none listed on the pages read

**booking_lead_time:** none listed as a minimum number of days; the brochure asks organisers to "Please have an alternate date in mind" and, for bookings, to "have on hand: school, grade level, number of students, time of arrival and departure, organising teacher – email address or mobile number"

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** "If you would like to book, phone Billabong Sanctuary on 4778 8344" (brochure). The education page states: "If you would like to discuss the education program further, please contact Billabong Sanctuary's Curator Ray Stephens at info@billabongsanctuary.com.au."

**price_visible_without_asking:** yes. The school-group rate (\$26 per student, adult ratio, catering add-ons) is published directly on the Education page and in the downloadable brochure, without requiring an enquiry.

**downloadable_pack:** the 2026 Education Program Brochure PDF is freely downloadable from the Education page without login or registration, and includes the suggested itinerary, per-animal choices, pricing and catering detail

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed on the pages read
