## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "Primary School"; "High School Groups"; "Early Learning"; "Home Schooling"; "Community Groups"; "Adults"; within the booking form's Additional Experiences: "Nature Ed Session (CWS Expert Led Experience)"; "Nature Ed First Nations Experience"; "Walking Tours"; "Koala Connections Experience"; "Koala Photo"; "Meet Moose"; "Nest Boxes"; the Early Learning lesson options "Animal Tales (literacy-based)", "Counting Critters (numeracy-based)" and "Animal Treasures (nature-based)"

**activities_verbatim:** Primary School page: "Primary students get hands-on with nature while exploring the Sanctuary on a self-guided journey. Greeted by an Education Officer upon arrival, your class can also add on an engaging 45-minute Education Session, aligned to v8.4 of the Australian Curriculum." High School page: "For students in Years 7 to 10, Currumbin Wildlife Sanctuary offers unforgettable, curriculum-linked experiences. After being welcomed by an Education Officer, groups can explore at their own pace or add a 45-minute Education Session focused on biology, conservation, and the real-world impact of environmental science." Booking form, on the "Nature Ed Session (CWS Expert Led Experience)": "Led by one of our experienced and qualified wildlife education officers, this interactive experience is linked to the Australian Curriculum and tailored to your students' year level. Your students will have the opportunity to meet and learn about three of our amazing animals; while participating in interactive activities to facilitate learning." On the "Nature Ed First Nations Experience": "This 45-minute hands on experience has been designed specifically to entertain all age groups. The audience will explore our collection of Indigenous Cultural items such as ochre and a variety of artefacts including weapons, instruments and plants that ensured the survival of the world's longest continuing culture. Participants will learn of cultural teachings and the importance that story telling held within First Nations cultures." On "Walking Tours": "Our 45 minute walking tour routes are a fantastic way for your students/participants to see the Sanctuary, whilst learning about wildlife", with routes named "Iconic Aussies (E.g. koalas, kangaroos, crocodile, dingo)", "Conservation Champions (koala, Tasmanian devil, brush-tail rock wallaby)" and "Exotic Species (Lost Valley and Outback Springs)". On "Koala Connections Experience": "Koalas like never before as you wander through a purpose-built, immersive habitat...This isn't a typical photo-op; it's a slow-paced walk where you can observe, not intrude...knowledgeable Wildlife Keepers will share fascinating insights", with inclusions listed as "15-minute experience", "Wildlife keeper present, providing information about Koalas and conservation", "Take unlimited selfies and photos" and "Professional photo, including a print and digital copy". On "Meet Moose": "Add something unforgettable to your visit with a live conservation detection dog demonstration...a complimentary 15-minute presentation by Moose and Mikey, subject to availability". On "Nest Boxes": "Why not set up a nesting box back at your school, it's as easy as 1, 2, 3: 1. Identify which native species are in your local area 2. Select the appropriate box when you book your excursion 3. Collect during your excursion, assemble and set up back at your school." The booking form also states general purpose: "This booking form is for Australian schools and educational organisations...Our objective is to increase awareness about our native animals and their environment."

**duration:** self-guided base visit (no stated whole-visit duration; the form asks each school for its own "Arrival Time" and "Departure Time"); optional add-on Education Session "45-minute" (Primary and High School pages); "Nature Ed First Nations Experience" is "This 45-minute hands on experience"; "Walking Tours" are "45 minute"; "Koala Connections Experience" is a "15-minute experience"; "Meet Moose" is a "complimentary 15-minute presentation"

**group_size_min_max:** "To receive these education rates, groups must be pre booked and be a minimum of 10 paying individuals"; the booking form's own visit-detail field caps sub-groups at "(max 30 students/session)" and notes "Schools requiring multiple sessions can be run either simultaneously or across the day"; weekend Nature Ed and First Nations sessions require "minimum 25 students"; the separate Group Bookings page states a general (not school-specific) minimum of "20 or more people" for corporate/other group enquiries, distinct from the school booking form's 10-paying-individual minimum

**participant_thresholds:** "Early Learning Students* (3 - 5 years) \$18.50 per student (1 free teacher/adult per 5 paying students)"; "Primary Students* (4 - 12 years) \$18.50 per student (1 free teacher/adult per 5 paying students)"; "High School Students* (12 - 18 years) \$18.50 per student (1 free teacher/adult per 10 paying students)"; "Adult Students* (18 years+) \$35.00 per student (1 free teacher)"; "Additional teachers/adults above the FOC ratio must be pre booked and will be subject to an admission fee of \$35.00 per person (no further discounts apply)"

## Inventory

**facilities:** "TreeTop Challenge featuring over 100 fun tree-high obstacles"; the Currumbin Miniature Railway with four stations ("Lorikeet Station", "Conservation Station", "Wild Station", "Kangaroo Station"); the Lorikeet Arena; dining venues "Sanctuary Cafe", "Wild Burgers" and "The Homestead" (in Kangaroo Paddock); "Sanctuary Gifts" retail outlet; the "Cooee tunnel" connecting the east and west sides of the Sanctuary under the Gold Coast Highway; "Various conference locations are available throughout the Sanctuary", including the "Sir Walter Campbell Centre"; a "Health Centre (First Aid station)"; the site covers "twenty-seven (27) hectares"

**equipment_provided:** none listed specifically for the school programme; general visitor equipment noted on the accessibility page includes stroller and wheelchair hire ("Stroller and wheelchair hire is currently available from the Visitor and Member Services booth") and an assisted listening service "which amplifies sound through headphones or induction loop...at the Wild Skies Free Flight Bird Show"

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "Greeted by an Education Officer upon arrival" (Primary School page); "After being welcomed by an Education Officer" (High School page); the Nature Ed Session is "Led by one of our experienced and qualified wildlife education officers"; on accessibility, "All Astra Lumina staff and volunteers are encouraged to complete the Hidden Disability SUNFLOWER training program" and "Our team has undergone specialist training with Spinal Life Australia" (this second item applies to general accessibility support, not confirmed as specific to the education team)

**accessibility_provision:** "We are committed to providing suitable access for our guests, whatever their individual needs may be"; eight reserved accessible parking spaces, level pedestrian crossing from car park to entrance, and a retail entrance with "tiled ground with level access"; "Guests accompanying a Companion Card or Hāpai Access Card holder in the capacity of a registered Companion or Carer are entitled to complimentary entry"; pathways vary "from concrete, pavers, stone, timber walkways and sand substrate", with "Paths with steeper inclines...clearly marked via signage"; the Cooee tunnel is "2500mm" wide with "LED lighting" though "considerably darker inside"; "All restrooms within the Sanctuary are currently gendered however all accessible restrooms are gender neutral"; a "PWD toilet with shower facility is located at the Treetop Challenge amenities"; the closest Changing Places facility is off-site, at "John Laws Park Public Amenities"; named accessible animal encounters are "Koala Encounter", "Koala Breakfast and Grazy Arvo with Koalas" and "Wildlife Insider Tour", while "The Echidna Encounter, Goat Encounter, Tree Kangaroo Encounter and Wildlife Hospital Tour currently do not meet accessibility requirements"; "A hearing loop with eight (8) receivers is available in the Sir Walter Campbell Centre" conference venue; "Guide, Hearing and Assistance Dogs are welcome within the Sanctuary" subject to certification, but "Assistance dogs are not permitted into areas where direct interaction with animals can occur"

**animal_species_held:** koalas, lorikeets, pelicans, eels, radiated tortoises, Tasmanian devils, Kroombit tinkerfrogs (named in the Shows and Times schedule); capybaras, cotton-top tamarins, lemurs, echidnas, goats, tree kangaroos (named among Animal Encounters on the accessibility page); kangaroos, crocodiles, dingoes and brush-tail rock wallabies (named in the booking form's walking-tour route descriptions, "Iconic Aussies" and "Conservation Champions")

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** Primary School page: "aligned to v8.4 of the Australian Curriculum"; High School page: "curriculum-linked experiences" "focused on biology, conservation, and the real-world impact of environmental science"; the booking form lets schools choose "v8.4 Australian Curriculum", "v9.0 Australian Curriculum" or "Other Curriculum" and then select a specific lesson by year level, for example under Primary v9.0: "Prep/Foundation - Classifications", "Year 1 - Habitats", "Year 4 - Food Chain Relationships", "Year 6 - Impact on/Threats to the Environment"; under Secondary v9.0: "Year 7 - Classifications and Food Webs", "Year 9 - Biomes and Ecosystems", "Year 10 - Evolution"; both primary and secondary lists also offer "Other - Special Needs Presentation" and "Other - General Walking Tour"

**teacher_materials:** the Educational Resources page states "we've got resources and tools to fuel their love for the world around them. Cancellation Fee - Bookings cancelled prior to 48 hours of booking date will incur a cancellation fee of 50% of the booking price. This is to cover wages of educators rostered, resources being compiled and venues at the Sanctuary being booked. The full booking price will be charged for groups who fail to attend on the day with no prior notice." The educational-visits hub's FAQ adds, on weather generally: "Currumbin Wildlife Sanctuary is primarily an outdoor venue, with some enclosed learning spaces. ## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "School Group – Self-guided tour"; "Wild Ed"; "Into the Wild"; "Ranger-guided Park Tour"

**activities_verbatim:** "self-guided tours – school groups can coordinate their own visit and tour enabling students to discover some of the weird and wonderful wildlife that inhabits Queensland's national parks"; "Wild Ed – (up to 45 mins) – A 45-minute ranger led presentation, aided by our native wildlife ambassador team, aligned to your curriculum learning outcomes. The presentation is followed by a practical Wild Challenge workshop where students have the opportunity to put learning into action."; "Into the Wild (up to 45mins) – a wildlife discovery show led by our ranger team involving multiple species while also showcasing Queensland's National Parks and threatened species"; "Ranger Guided Park Tour (up to 1hr) – A guided tour around the park providing students with insights into the unique species, and their habitats, that call Queensland home. Maximum group number is 30 students/tour."; the booking-system listing separately summarises "Our activities include: Education talk with a Park Ranger; Wildlife Presentation; Self-guided walk around the Park"; the PDF information pack gives "Into the Wild" a different length, "up to 30 mins", against the schools page's "up to 45mins" for the same programme; the schools page adds that "The main focus points of the education programs are threatened species, habitats and animal adaptations, life cycles and food webs. Programs for secondary and tertiary students can also focus on classification, biodiversity conservation and the adjacent mangroves along Tallebudgera Creek."; "Aboriginal cultural education programs may be available upon request."

**duration:** Wild Ed "up to 45 mins"; Into the Wild "up to 30 mins" (PDF pack) or "up to 45mins" (schools page, inconsistent with the pack); Ranger-guided Park Tour "up to 1hr"; self-guided tours carry no stated duration

**group_size_min_max:** "A minimum number of 10 students is required to guarantee the group rate" (PDF pack); the Ranger-guided Park Tour is capped at "no more than 25" (PDF pack) or "Maximum group number is 30 students/tour" (schools page, inconsistent with the pack); no overall maximum whole-visit group size is stated

**participant_thresholds:** group rate requires "a minimum number of 10 students" and the pricing table is headed "Student Price (10 or more paying students)"; Ranger-guided Park Tour groups are capped at 25 (PDF pack) or 30 (schools page); free-of-charge (FOC) supervision ratios are given by year level (see adults_free_ratio)

## Inventory

**facilities:** "Information centre"; "Parking area"; "Refreshments"; "Toilets"; "Wheelchair access (may require assistance)"; "Wifi"; nocturnal house named "Nalu Theatre"; "Living Rainforest Classroom"; "David Fleay Outdoor Amphitheatre"; "Wallaby Way"; Fleay's Cafe, open "9.00am to 2.00pm" per the PDF pack (the general attractions page separately states the park itself is open "9am–4pm daily"); boardwalks through "7.5 acres of parkland"; a Tallebudgera Creek Mangrove boardwalk adjoining the park; bus parking described as "Large buses are able to park within the designated bus parks to drop students off"; "The restaurant and surrounding park grounds make David Fleay Wildlife Park a great place for events, whether special occasions or regular meetings" for birthdays, weddings and venue hire

**equipment_provided:** none listed as provided to students; the PDF pack instead lists what the school must bring: "students have hats, sunscreen, insect repellent, warm clothing and rain gear for wet weather" and "sturdy, closed shoes"

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "Park Rangers delivering activities for students at the Park have been issued a Positive Notice Blue Card for child related employment"; "Park Rangers at David Fleay Wildlife Park are trained in first aid"; the schools page states the excursion is "Led by our passionate Rangers" and that the "booking includes a QPWS ranger as the tour guide"

**accessibility_provision:** "The Park is suitable for wheelchairs with some assistance" (PDF pack); "There is accessible parking available in the car park"; the general attractions page separately lists "Wheelchair access (may require assistance)" among park facilities; the PDF pack notes "Stairs are present in David Fleay Wildlife Park. Wheelchair access is available."

**animal_species_held:** southern cassowary, platypus (named "Waddirrang"), freshwater and estuarine (saltwater) crocodiles, Lumholtz's tree-kangaroo, yellow-bellied gliders, mahogany gliders, squirrel gliders, wedge-tailed eagle, koalas, dingoes, emus, Bridled nailtail wallaby (named individuals "Hana" and joey "Brigalow"), greater bilby, Julia Creek dunnart, brolgas, pelicans, red-legged pademelon, black-necked stork, plumed whistling duck, eastern water dragon, lace monitor, Proserpine rock-wallaby, grey kangaroo, swamp wallaby, tawny frogmouth, Spinifex hopping mice, snakes ("a variety of snakes"), dusky moorhen, Australian brush-turkey; the risk-management guidelines note that "water dragons, dusky moorhens, brush-turkeys, ducks and ibis" are free-roaming wild animals within the park rather than exhibited stock

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** "Our excursions feature environmental education activities linked with the Australian Curriculum, including topics such as habitats, animal adaptations and life cycles"; Wild Ed is described as "aligned to your curriculum learning outcomes"; Into the Wild is described in the booking-system listing as "Non-curriculum linked presentation", which is inconsistent with the schools page's characterisation of the whole programme suite as curriculum-linked; "Programs for secondary and tertiary students can also focus on classification, biodiversity conservation and the adjacent mangroves along Tallebudgera Creek"; "Special programs are available for tertiary students and English as a second language (ESL) students"

**teacher_materials:** "Our downloadable information pack (PDF, 1.1MB) includes an excursion guide, education activities and risk management guidelines"; "The Program Pocket Guide (PDF, 11.1MB) includes further details about our educational programs and their learning focus'" (Program Pocket Guide itself was not one of the four fixed pages or a price list/risk assessment/information pack and was not opened); "Download the Visitor map (PDF, 4.9MB) to navigate your way around the park"

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** yes. The PDF information pack's pages 3-6 are headed "2023-2024 EXCURSION VENUE RISK MANAGEMENT GUIDELINES", an itemised hazard table across 15 named activities and areas (site construction, COVID-19, park access, bag storage, student behaviour, walking on tracks, outdoor activities, student/live animal interactions, wildlife shows, visiting specific areas such as the nocturnal house and Wallaby Way, meal breaks and toilet access, student/plant and soil interactions, bad weather, and photography/CCTV), each row giving the hazard, the potential risk or consequence, and control strategies; the guidelines note this document "will be provided with your booking confirmation, unless provided prior"

**insurance_stated:** "Insurance: This venue has public-liability cover" (PDF pack, in the risk management guidelines)

**child_safety_checks_stated:** "Park Rangers delivering activities for students at the Park have been issued a Positive Notice Blue Card for child related employment" (PDF pack); "We adhere to the Child Protection Policy and Procedure (part of the Child and Youth Risk Management Strategy) for our venues to foster a safe and supportive environment for children and young people by identifying potential risks for harm and implementing strategies to minimise these risks. This is reviewed annually and is available to all clients upon request."

**accreditations_displayed:** none listed by name of an external accrediting body; the venue instead states its own government-operator status: "As one of only two Queensland Government owned and operated wildlife parks, our focus is not only on our wildlife, but the conservation of their habitats and the education of all those who visit" (booking-system listing)

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** "Prices for education bookings" table, "Student price for 10 or more paying students": "School group – self-guided tour \$9.80"; "Wild Ed – Ranger led curriculum aligned experience within living classroom *GST free in accordance with educational tax legislation. \$15.50*"; "Into the Wild – Ranger led wildlife discover show \$15.50"; "Ranger guided park tour \$15.50"; "Above group rate available to school groups with 10 or more paying students. All prices are inclusive of GST and available only to Australian primary and secondary education institutions – noting that as WildEd is linked to curriculum outcomes no GST applies and therefore the price is GST free. Prices updated on the first of July every year and are subject to change without notice."; a separate "Friends of Fleays – Education Experience Program" is named in the site's navigation, described in a search-result snippet (not one of the pages read) as providing "a subsidy of up to \$800 to Primary and Secondary schools in Queensland undertaking a school excursion at David Fleay Wildlife Park between February and June 2025"; this was not verified against the page itself since it falls outside the fixed four-page set and the note above's extra-page allowance

**adults_free_ratio:** "Free of charge (FOC) ratio for teachers/supervisors" table (schools page and PDF pack give slightly different bands): schools page states "Childcare: 1 FOC to 3 children"; "Prep–Year 3: 1 FOC to 5 students"; "Years 4–6: 1 FOC to 10 students"; "Years 7–12: 1 FOC to 20 students"; "Tertiary: 1 FOC to 20 students"; the PDF pack states "Early Childhood (under 3 years): 1 FOC to 2 students"; "Early Childhood (under 4 years): 1 FOC to 3 students"; "Foundation – Year 6: 1 FOC to 5 students"; "Years 7 – 12: 1 FOC to 15 Students"; the two documents disagree on the Years 4-12 bands, and only the PDF pack breaks out early-childhood by age rather than by "Childcare" as a single band

**catering:** "Fleay's Cafe is open from 9.00am to 2.00pm"; "The Fleay's Cafe sells tea and coffee, cold drinks, sandwiches, some hot food, snacks and confectionary. Students wishing to visit Fleay's Cafe and purchase food/drinks will be given set times to visit, so as not to affect the lunch catering. Please speak to the Park Ranger about organising for students to visit Fleay's Cafe prior to your visit. Catering for your group can be arranged with at least two weeks' notice."; "Please be aware that there are many wild animals at the Park in addition to the exhibited animals. Feeding is not permitted and you must supervise students during their meal breaks to prevent food being given to the animals or left on the ground. Please ensure that students keep their bags zipped up to prevent wild animals from going through the contents of their bags."

**cancellation_and_weather:** "In the event of adverse weather, activities may need to be altered, postponed or cancelled. Please encourage children/parents to pack a raincoat in case of wet weather."; the risk management guidelines add, under Bad weather: "In the case of severe weather (torrential rain, lightning strikes, strong winds etc.) management will determine whether it is appropriate to close the Park in the interest of public safety. Park Rangers guiding the group will return visitors to areas of safe shelter or evacuate the Park."; under Outdoor activities: "If weather conditions are deemed unsuitable for the activity, the activity may be altered or cancelled."; no cancellation fee, notice period, or refund policy specific to school bookings is stated on the pages read

**booking_lead_time:** none listed as a minimum; catering "can be arranged with at least two weeks' notice"; no minimum lead time for the excursion booking itself is stated

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** "Head to our online booking console to select and confirm your program needs. Should you have further questions or require customisation just contact our Education team for assistance via email: fleays@des.qld.gov.au or phone (07) 5669 2051", linking to the book.parks.qld.gov.au listing "David Fleay Wildlife Park - School Group Bookings"; "Payment can be accepted on the day of your excursion via Visa/MasterCard or an invoice can be sent to your school (cash not accepted). Please advise us prior to your visit."

**price_visible_without_asking:** yes. The full per-student price table for all four school programmes, and the FOC teacher/supervisor ratios, are published directly on the schools page and repeated in the downloadable PDF information pack, with no requirement to enquire first

**downloadable_pack:** "School Excursion Information Pack" (PDF, 1.1MB), freely downloadable without login, containing the excursion guide, standard-of-behaviour points, pricing table, FOC ratio table and the full risk management guidelines; a separate "Program Pocket Guide" (PDF, 11.1MB) is also linked, described as containing "further details about our educational programs and their learning focus", but was not opened as it falls outside the four fixed page types and the price-list/risk-assessment/information-pack allowance for linked PDFs; a "condition of entry (PDF, 921.7KB)" is also linked but not opened for the same reason

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed on the pages read; the general Google Reviews carousel embedded on multiple pages carries reviews from members of the public, not identified as school-group testimonials, so none are quoted here

**title_and_opening_line:** Title: "School excursions". Opening line: "Discover an educational adventure at David Fleay Wildlife Park, where learning comes alive amid Queensland's iconic wildlife. Programs, led by expert QPWS Rangers, are crafted to inspire and empower young environmental advocates."

## Note

The schools page and the downloadable PDF information pack disagree on two points of detail: the stated duration of "Into the Wild" (45 minutes on the schools page against 30 minutes in the PDF pack), the maximum group size for the Ranger-guided Park Tour (30 students on the schools page against 25 in the PDF pack), and the FOC teacher/supervisor ratio bands for Years 4 and up (the schools page gives coarser Years 4-6 and Years 7-12 bands of 1:10 and 1:20, while the PDF pack gives a single Foundation-Year 6 band of 1:5 and a Years 7-12 band of 1:15). Both documents are current official QPWS material; this profile records both readings rather than resolving the discrepancy. All source pages were retrieved via web.archive.org cached snapshots because parks.qld.gov.au and book.parks.qld.gov.au returned HTTP 403 to direct WebFetch and curl requests; the cached snapshots carry site-reported "Last updated"/"Last reviewed" dates of 16 June 2025 or later (up to 29 July 2025), so they reflect the current published content rather than a stale crawl.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** activity_list_no_timings

**programme_names:** "The Core Program"; "Our extra optional half hour segments (choose maximum of three)"

**activities_verbatim:** Core Program: "Damper making - Dough making, open fire cookery in camp ovens (demonstration)"; "Puppet show - Animated wooden toys tell the story of land settlement on (in the Gallery) the Darling Downs, from 1840 to 1900 (suit all ages)"; "Slab cottage Housing - Grinke slab hut and Raatz stone cookhouse (1889) (guided tour), including homemade furniture, hides as carpets, handmade fly screen, open fire, fountain, vegetable garden, washing machines"; "Damper eating - Billy tea, damper, golden syrup at tables in the damper shed (Morning tea)". Optional half-hour segments: "The Big Cow- Walk inside and see how this incredible sculpture was made, learn about it's history and the Dairy Industry. Includes a Butter Making Demonstration on request."; "Heritage chapel - History and current use of the Meringandan Congregational Church (1909), conducted inside the chapel"; "Blacksmithing Working with iron - forge and anvil (demonstration) and horseshoe making"; "Water wisdom water - guttering and tanks, divining for underground water, (participatory) boring and windmills, wood-fired pumps, log stock troughs. Manually operated geared water pump demo (suit Years 4, 5, 6)"; "Harvesting machines - A guided visit to the machinery shed. Stripper, winnower, thresher, reaper & binder, Harvesters 1838 to early 1900's"; "Southern Cross - A guided walk through the Southern Cross Museum. Products made by Toowoomba Foundry 1876-1980's"; "Toowoomba Fire Brigade Historical Museum- A guided tour of the History of our local Fire Brigade and their trucks, technology and how it has changed. Fire Safety Talks can be included via prior arrangement"; "12. Free time - There are many buildings with stationary exhibits to explore the authentic slice of Australian heritage. Pupils to be in small groups with an adult"; "Aboriginal Culture Talk - Didgeridoo performance, dance workshop and boomerang throwing workshop. This involves an extra charge, please contact us for a quote."; "Sheep Shearing- the process by which the woollen fleece of a sheep is cut off. The person who removes the sheep's wool is called a shearer. This involves an extra charge, please contact us for a quote."; "Transport Museum and the James MacDonald Bike Collection - A guided walk through the Transport Museum: featuring penny farthings, horse drawn transport and vintage cars"; "Queensland Energy Exhibition Centre - A guided walk through the Energy Museum featuring the history of Energy usage in Queensland and the very first electrical appliances ever made"; "Horse works - Corn growing, husking, and cracking for farm use and as a cash crop. The use of draught horses as a source of power to work stationary machinery, horse whim demonstration. This involves an extra charge, please contact us for a quote."; "Ride Henry the Model T Ford Bus- was manufactured in 1924 and originally used as a school bus for the Kilcoy State School from 1924 to 1940. \$5 Adults and \$3 Child. (minimum booking of 12 persons)."; "School's In- The Upper Forest Spring's School was opened in 1898 and closed in 1961, sit in school and experience what it was like to be a student in the olden days."; "Coach House- Includes a Princess Coach, German Wagon, Sulkies, Buggies and an Ambulance Wagon. Let your volunteer guide take you on a walk through the history of Horse Drawn Transport in Australia." A closing note reads: "Please Note: If one of the demonstrators is away sick another segment will be substituted"

**group_size_min_max:** no general school group minimum or maximum is stated; a minimum applies only to one optional segment: "Ride Henry the Model T Ford Bus... This involves an extra charge, please contact us for a quote."

## Note

The Centre is a free, council-run facility rather than a commercial excursion operator, and its web presence reflects that: one facility page with opening hours, a donation-based entry policy, and a single line inviting schools and large groups to pre-book, but no named programme, no curriculum links, no risk assessment, no teacher pack and no price list distinct from general admission. A partial closure is in effect from 20 April to September 2026 (northern half of the Centre, boardwalk replacement), with works continuing to early 2027; access during the closure is via a side gate on Goleby Avenue. This is a visitor-impact fact current at date_read, not a scope judgement.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "Kindy Groups and Childcare Centres" and "Local Schools (Prep-Year 12)" Discovery Session packages, plus a "Self-Guided Day" option; within the Local Schools tier, discrete named Discovery Sessions organised by year band: "Kindy Sessions"; Prep - Year 3: "Backyard Explorers", "Hatchers & Growers", "Wildlife Scientists"; Year 4 - 6: "Backyard Explorers", "Guardians of the Endangered", "Talkin' 'Bout Your Biomes!", "Web of Life"; Year 7 - 9: "Australian Terrestrial Vertebrates - Classified!", "Guardians of the Endangered", "Talkin' 'Bout Your Biomes!", "Web of Life"; Year 10 - 12: "Australian Terrestrial Vertebrates - Classified!", "Web of Life", "Working with Wildlife".

**activities_verbatim:** "Lead your own self-guided visit, or strengthen your classroom teaching with hands-on, curriculum-linked Discovery Sessions, run by an experienced Wildlife Education Officer."; "With kangaroo feeding, immersive shows, and an astonishing array of Aussie wildlife to visit, a Lone Pine excursion is sure to be a day to remember!"; "30-minute custom animal presentation per class with a focus on colours, textures and animal bodies. 1 bag of free-range animal food per student to feed our kangaroos, wallabies and emus." (Kindy); "Choose from one of our 45-minute curriculum-linked Discovery Sessions (one session per class) with an Education Officer and meet up to 4 animals. 1 bag of free-range animal food per student to feed our kangaroos, wallabies and emus." (Local Schools); "We also offer custom Discovery Sessions for students with special needs and English language learner students and are able to tailor custom sessions to meet your group's specific requirements."; "Backyard Explorers" (Prep-Year 3): "Lone Pine's 'Backyard Explorer' creates a journey of discovery while introducing habitats, diets, adaptations and plenty of problem-solving. Students are challenged to use their new Backyard Explorer knowledge to piece together animal clues like scale sheds, footprints and food sources to learn which animals may be living in their backyards before meeting some of these animals face-to-face!"; "Hatchers & Growers": "Explore the birth, growth and development of Australian wildlife by meeting a mixture of egg layers, marsupials and other live birth animals. How do these animals grow and change to survive in the wild? Let's discover the answers by exploring their life cycles through hands-on experiences and animal encounters guided by Lone Pine's resident 'Hatcher and Grower'!"; "Wildlife Scientists": "Join a Lone Pine 'Wildlife Scientist' to explore early level classification using body coverings. From skin to scales, feathers and fur, students will investigate similarities and differences between animal groups and consider how their coverings help them adapt and survive. Through hands-on matching of animal pictures to real biofacts, and meeting some of Lone Pine's special wildlife up close, students will discover the fascinating ways body coverings help animals thrive and be inducted as Lone Pine's newest Wildlife Scientists!"; "Guardians of the Endangered": "Students are introduced to threatening issues facing Australian wildlife and tasked with uncovering 'danger zones'. After exploring relevant artefacts and meeting ambassador animals, students are challenged to identify and share practical solutions to dangers in the form of daily actions."; "Talkin' 'Bout Your Biomes!": "This game show-style session investigates the main biomes of Australia, introduces the fragile ecological links within these complex zones and considers how animals use adaptations to survive in their environments. Led by their education game show host, students work in teams to match native animals to the correct Australian biome before meeting some of these animals up close."; "Web of Life": "Students will discover how every creature plays a part in keeping nature in balance by exploring the roles within a food web and working together to create their own food web. Using their creation, students investigate what happens when natural or human impacts disturb the system. By meeting real animals and considering their roles, students will gain a deeper understanding of the web of life."; "Australian Terrestrial Vertebrates - Classified!" (Year 7-9): "Students dive into the world of animal classification by examining and grouping species based on shared characteristics. They'll explore how and why scientists use taxonomy, and work together using their classification knowledge and dichotomous keys to identify native animal species. Their learning is brought to life by meeting an amphibian, bird, reptile and mammal face-to-face."; "Working with Wildlife" (Year 10-12): "Students explore the wide range of career pathways within the wildlife industry, gaining insight into roles such as wildlife keepers, veterinarians, education officers and horticultural staff. Through examples from actual Lone Pine Koala Sanctuary professionals and encounters with live animals, students learn how scientific knowledge, practical skills and personal interests combine to shape careers working with wildlife, helping them understand the steps and pathways into this industry."; the Planning Your Excursion Guide gives a "SAMPLE ITINERARY" running from "9:25am Arrival, sign off and welcome" through a Discovery Session, morning tea and toilet break, visits to "cassowary, dingoes and crocodiles", the "Amphibian and Reptile Habitat", the "Brisbane Koala Science Institute", "Free range kangaroo feeding", lunch, the "Free Flight Raptor Show" and "Sheepdog Show", to "2:00pm Depart Lone Pine"; the same guide notes "Due to legislative restrictions on koala handling time, koala interactions other than meeting a koala during your Discovery Session are not open to school groups. This includes public photo and 'Meet a Koala' sessions."

**duration:** Discovery Session: "45-minute Discovery Session (30 minutes for Kindy, Vacation Care, and some custom sessions)"; the daily keeper shows that a school itinerary can incorporate "generally run for approximately 15 minutes"; no stated duration is given for the Self-Guided Day itself, though the general tickets page states, for visitors generally rather than school groups specifically, "Most people spend three to four hours here".

**group_size_min_max:** Discovery Session: "A minimum of 10 & a maximum of 30 students per session"; the Group Bookings page states "For groups of 10 or more" a Group Booking Request Form applies, and "For groups smaller than 10, please book directly through the tickets page on our website"; the sample itinerary notes "Not all public presentations are suitable for groups over 80 people and some smaller activity venues are not suitable for groups over 15 people."

**participant_thresholds:** Chaperone Free of Charge Ratios (Local Student Groups page): "Kindergarten 1:3"; "Prep to Year 2 1:5"; "Year 3 onward 1:10"; "Additional needs 1:1"; "Over free of charge ratio: Standard adult entry"; the Planning Your Excursion Guide's Teacher Pre-Visit Checklist restates the same figures as "Prep – Year 2 free of charge ratio: 1 adult/5 students", "Year 3+ free of charge ratio: 1 adult/10 students", "Special needs students free of charge ratio: 1 adult/1 student (A carers card or a letter from the principal is required stating that this level of care is needed)", and requires "All chaperone numbers (school staff and student family members) must be confirmed with the Education Team at least 10 working days before your excursion."

## Inventory

**facilities:** the Barn; undercover "presentation venues" used for Discovery Sessions, described as "Education venue spaces undercover/shaded"; an undercover seating area for school groups near Sleepy Koala Café; "a specialised ambulant toilet block with shower facility and fold-down bench"; "Charter bus parking" at the front of the sanctuary; the Brisbane Koala Science Institute; the Amphibian and Reptile Habitat; the Platypus House; the free-range Kangaroo Reserve; a First Aid room; the Sensory Garden, described as "a purpose-built space...fully wheelchair accessible...beside the lorikeet feeding area and café"; an air-conditioned wildlife hospital, also available for the temporary care of assistance animals; three on-site cafés: Riverside Café ("Located just before the entrance to the sanctuary"), Butterfly Café ("Nestled within the sanctuary") and Sleepy Koala Café.

**equipment_provided:** "1 bag of free-range animal food per student" is included with both the Self-Guided Day and Discovery Session packages, for feeding kangaroos, wallabies and emus; the risk assessment document states that students with allergies to Kangaroo food ingredients "will be provided with gloves or an alternate food source"; manual wheelchairs are available but not free: "A small maintenance fee of \$6 applies, and guests are asked to sign a liability waiver on hire."

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "All Lone Pine Koala Sanctuary Wildlife Education Officers are required to hold a Blue Card for working with children as well as a Provide First Aid & CPR certificate. Our team hold a variety of tertiary qualifications, and all have experience in the field of education."; "Lone Pine Koala Sanctuary has a team of designated First Aid Officers who are qualified to provide first aid if needed."; the risk assessment adds "Sanctuary keepers trained in the safe handling of animals" and "Education officer first aid trained and certified."

**accessibility_provision:** "Lone Pine sits on naturally hilly terrain, so not every slope is easily navigated in an unassisted wheelchair. Alternate routes are marked on our visitor map, and our team can help you plan a comfortable path."; "A limited number of manual wheelchairs are available for hire at the Ticket Entrance. A small maintenance fee of \$6 applies, and guests are asked to sign a liability waiver on hire."; "Accessible toilets are available throughout the sanctuary"; "Accessible car parking spaces are located close to the entrance."; Companion Card entry: "Your support person or carer receives complimentary admission when attending with the guest being supported. The guest being supported simply needs a standard admission ticket."; assistance animals are "welcome in public spaces such as the Riverside Café and the outdoor lawn, with no prior arrangements needed", but "Pre-booking is required" for the sanctuary grounds, "Visits are guided by a team member and limited to two hours", and the animal "can be cared for in our air-conditioned wildlife hospital if you would like to visit a restricted zone"; the Sensory Garden is described as fully wheelchair accessible with "Plants chosen to be touched and smelled, alongside wind chimes, water features and a range of textures and sounds"; the Planning Your Excursion Guide separately confirms "Wheelchairs are available for hire and can be reserved if needed" for school groups.

**animal_species_held:** the homepage states "Meet over 100 koalas and 70 native Australian species" in its banner and, further down the same page, "We are lucky enough to be the caretakers of over 100 koalas and 75 species of native Australian animals. Many wild animals also call Lone Pine home, such as birds, snakes, lizards, insects, and possums."; named species and groups appearing across the pages read include koalas, kangaroos, wallabies, emus, dingoes, a quokka (named "Quincy"), platypus, cassowary, freshwater and estuarine crocodiles, Tasmanian devils, common wombats, southern hairy-nosed wombats, wedge-tailed eagle and other birds of prey, reptiles generally, guinea pigs, chicks/chickens, goats, sheep, sheepdogs (working dogs, per the Sheepdog Show), turtles and flying foxes; the sanctuary map (in the Planning Your Excursion Guide) additionally lists "Main Koala Exhibit", "Amphibian & Reptile Habitat", "Tasmanian Devils", "Turtles", "Platypus", "Cassowary", "Brisbane Koala Science Institute", "Kangaroos, Wallabies & Emus" and "Estuarine Crocodile" as numbered map points.

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** each named Discovery Session carries a "Curriculum Links:" heading listing Australian Curriculum Version 9 achievement-standard codes by year level, for example "Backyard Explorers" for Year 1: "AC9S1U01, AC9S1H01, AC9S1I01, AC9S1I02, AC9S1I03, AC9S1I05, AC9HS1S01, AC9HS1SO3, AC9HS1SO4, AC9HS1SO5, AC9HS1SO6, AC9E1LA09, AC9E1LY02", and, at the senior end, "Working with Wildlife" for Year 11-12: "ACEEE001, ACEEE002, ACEEE008, ACEEE010, ACEEN028, ACEEN029, ACSBL001, ACSBL102"; codes are given separately for every year level from Prep to Year 12 across the nine named sessions, spanning Science, Humanities and Social Sciences, English, and, at senior secondary level, Biology and career-education syllabus codes; the Teacher Pre-Visit Checklist states "The resources in this guide correlate with the Australian Curriculum."

**teacher_materials:** the Local Teachers Portal's "Teacher Resources" section links "planning your day" (the Planning Your Excursion Guide PDF, opened for this profile), "free time activities" (a "Free Time Activities Prep-Year 6" PDF, linked but not opened), "classroom resources" (a "Classroom Resources" PDF, linked but not opened), "Excursion risk assessment safety notes" (opened for this profile), "Accessibility Information" and "Sanctuary Map"; none of these links were gated behind a login. The Planning Your Excursion Guide itself contains a Teacher Pre-Visit Checklist and a Chaperone Pre-Visit Checklist, a sample itinerary, and a note that "A pre-excursion Teacher Inspection visit can also be booked in through our Education Team", with "Free entry will be available for teachers only (friends/family not included) and a teacher ID or proof of employment must be shown to the Entrance Team on arrival."

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** yes. A dedicated "Lone Pine Koala Sanctuary - Education School Groups - Risk Assessment" PDF ("Current – February 2026", "Next Review Date: March 2027") was downloaded directly. It opens with a responsibilities statement and a five-by-five risk matrix, then itemises ten numbered risks with hazard details, listed controls and a residual risk rating: medical conditions/allergies, contamination through animal feeding, animal bite/scratch and insect bites/stings, slips/trips/falls, bodies of water, observing in exhibits, lost group members, heat and UV exposure, emergency evacuation, and severe/extreme weather. The Planning Your Excursion Guide separately states "Teachers responsible for planning an excursion to Lone Pine are expected to have conducted a risk assessment prior to their visit. Please refer to our Excursion Risk Assessment Safety Notes in the Teacher Portal to assist with conducting your risk assessment", and describes a documented "Emergency Procedure...which includes first aid, bomb threat, animal escape and fire situations."

**insurance_stated:** "Insurer: Sompo Japan Nipponkoa Insurance Inc"; "Amount: Public Liability: \$20,000,000 Any one occurrence"; "If a Certificate of Currency is required, please contact education@koala.net" (Planning Your Excursion Guide, Public Liability section).

**child_safety_checks_stated:** "All Lone Pine Koala Sanctuary Wildlife Education Officers are required to hold a Blue Card for working with children as well as a Provide First Aid & CPR certificate." (Planning Your Excursion Guide, Wildlife Education Officer Information section). No further Working with Children Check policy, for staff generally or for volunteers, appears on the pages read.

**accreditations_displayed:** footer logos appearing sitewide on every page read include a "ZAA_logo" (Zoo and Aquarium Association, per the file name), a "Travellers Choice Logo", an "international zoo educators association" logo, an "Eco_Certified_Nature_Tourism_Logo", and a "Sustainable1" logo; none of these are explained further, linked, or given accompanying text on the pages read.

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** school programme, from the Local Student Groups page: Self-Guided Day "\$20 per student (inc. GST)", described as "Explore the Sanctuary at your leisure. Includes 1 bag of free-range animal food per student."; Discovery Session, Kindergarten to Year 12, "\$25 per student (inc. GST)", described as "Choose one of our curriculum-linked Discovery Sessions. Includes a 30-45 minute presentation led by our Education Officer and 1 bag of free-range animal food per student. A minimum of 10 & a maximum of 30 students per session."; the Planning Your Excursion Guide adds "To be eligible for any education rates, all participants must be included in one bulk payment, otherwise standard retail rates apply." General public admission, from the Tickets & Memberships page, is published separately: Adult (18+) \$63; Child (3–13) \$45; Student (14–17, or valid student ID) \$50; Senior/Pensioner (65+, or valid ID) \$50; Family (2 adults + up to 3 children) \$193; Mini Family (1 adult + up to 3 children) \$133; an Adult Yearly Pass is \$101 per year.

**adults_free_ratio:** "Kindergarten 1:3"; "Prep to Year 2 1:5"; "Year 3 onward 1:10"; "Additional needs 1:1"; "Over free of charge ratio: Standard adult entry", meaning chaperones above the free ratio pay the standard adult admission rate rather than the school rate; the Planning Your Excursion Guide's Special Needs section states "We offer a free of charge ratio for students with special needs of one free adult per student. To receive this ratio a letter from the school Principal must be sent to the Education Team before your visit stating that this level of care is needed and explicitly stating how many students require it."

**catering:** "Groups are expected to bring their own food for lunch and morning tea. Lunches can be brought in backpacks, click-clack boxes or other storage containers. Bag storage racks are available near the undercover seating area near the Sleepy Koala Café. Please note that these are not locked. Please ensure that lunches are stored securely so that they cannot be accessed by brush turkeys or other wildlife."; "There are two cafes at the Sanctuary where food and beverages can be purchased. Please note that the cafés are not set up to cater for large groups unless this has been pre-organised."; the Dining page names three cafés available to general visitors: Riverside Café, Butterfly Café and Sleepy Koala Café, and states "we take pride in offering meals made with fresh ingredients and catering to a variety of dietary needs", but does not describe a school-specific catering package.

**cancellation_and_weather:** "Excursion bookings cancelled or postponed 10 or more working days prior to the excursion date will not be charged. Bookings cancelled or postponed less than 10 working days leading up to the excursion will be charged a 50% cancellation fee. This charge does not apply for wet weather conditions. Postponement charge will be waived if the excursion is rebooked before the original date passes. Failure to attend an excursion without notice will be charged at the full cost of the excursion."; on weather specifically: "Lone Pine Koala Sanctuary is mostly outdoors; however we do have some undercover areas and enclosed spaces (including our undercover seating area for school groups). Discovery Sessions can go ahead in rainy conditions at one of our under-cover Education venues. It is recommended that students bring rain jackets if showers are predicted. Excursions can be rescheduled due to wet weather if notice of postponement is given before your scheduled arrival time."

**booking_lead_time:** "All chaperone numbers (school staff and student family members) must be confirmed with the Education Team at least 10 working days before your excursion."; "Bookings are essential to be eligible for Education Rates." (Local Student Groups page); the separate Group Bookings page, which covers general (non-school) groups of ten or more, states "Groups of 10 or more can secure their booking by completing the form below and submitting payment at least 3 business days before their visit."

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** "View Education Programs & Book" (Local Student Groups page) links through to the Local Teachers Portal, where each Discovery Session tier carries its own "Request Booking" button; Email: education@koala.net; Phone (Planning Your Excursion Guide): "(07) 3130 4432"; Phone (site footer, all pages): "(07) 3378 1366"; a general "Contact Us" button also appears on the Local Student Groups page.

**price_visible_without_asking:** yes. Both the Self-Guided Day (\$20 per student) and Discovery Session (\$25 per student) school prices are published on the Local Student Groups page without requiring an enquiry, alongside general admission pricing on the Tickets & Memberships page.

**downloadable_pack:** the Local Teachers Portal offers, without any login gate observed: "planning your day" (Planning Your Excursion Guide PDF, opened for this profile, containing insurance details, arrival logistics, cancellation terms, a sample itinerary, and Teacher and Chaperone Pre-Visit Checklists), "free time activities" (Free Time Activities Prep-Year 6 PDF, linked but not opened), "classroom resources" (Classroom Resources PDF, linked but not opened), "Excursion risk assessment safety notes" (the risk assessment PDF, opened for this profile), "Accessibility Information" and "Sanctuary Map".

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed on the four fixed pages or the extra pages read. General visitor testimonials appear on the site (for example on the Wildlife Tours hub page, attributed to named individual guests dated August/September 2024), but none of these identify the reviewer as a teacher or school group, so none qualify as a school testimonial.

**title_and_opening_line:** Title: "Local Student Groups". Opening line: "Connect your students with Australian native wildlife like never before!"

## Note

In scope without qualification: the venue runs a structured, timed, curriculum-coded excursion programme for booked school groups on weekdays, priced per student, with a dedicated teacher portal, risk assessment, and named contact channel (education@koala.net), leaving no ambiguity about whether it caters school excursions.

The venue states two slightly different figures for its non-koala species count on the same homepage: "70 native Australian species" in the banner text near the top of the page, and "75 species of native Australian animals" further down the same page. Both are transcribed above as found, without reconciling them.

## Note

The school offer here is a discounted entry ticket sold through a standard Rezdy catalog, not a staffed or structured excursion programme. Both school products are named "School Group - [tier] Entry" and their descriptive text is close to verbatim copy of the general-admission product descriptions used elsewhere on the site ("Explore the lush and expansive gardens and come and meet our miniature farm animals" / "...come and meet our colourful family of birds"), rather than school-specific programme copy. No page read describes a teacher-led rotation, a booked Bird World tour slot reserved for the school group, a risk assessment, or curriculum tie-in of any kind. The venue nonetheless meets the collection's selection standard: the school-specific Rezdy products and the FAQ's own listing of "school excursions" among events catered for confirm it takes booked groups of school-age children on a weekday with an organised (if minimally described) offer, so it is included as `activity_list_no_timings` rather than `no_school_offer`, on the strength of the two named, priced school products.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "School Programs & Educational Groups"; "Cairns Vacation Care Programs"; from the brochure: "All Australian Experience", "Rainforest & Wildlife", "Dreamtime Discovery", "Indigenous Experience"

**activities_verbatim:** From the School Programs page: "Our All Australian Experience includes a 45 minute Army Duck Tour on which children can learn about various rainforest flora and fauna."; "Your class can enjoy a cultural experience on the Pamagirri Aboriginal Experience."; "View indigenous dances at the Pamagirri Dance Show and embrace Indigenous culture on the Dreamtime Walk. Children will watch a didgeridoo and spear throwing presentation and will learn to throw a boomerang themselves!"; "Students can get up close to a plethora of Native Australia animals on a 30 minute interactive wildlife tour, including tree kangaroos, Tasmanian Devils, snakes and koalas."; "We also offer solely indigenous experiences so that students can experience Indigenous culture in an authentic setting in the midst of World Heritage Rainforest. Students join a Pamagirri Aboriginal Guide as he shares a personal insight into his culture on the Pamagirri Rainforest Walkabout. They will learn about traditional customs including totems, body painting and rainforest survival." From the brochure, per named combo package: "Army Duck Tour (45 Minutes) — Students will board an amphibious World War II Army Duck for a land and water adventure through the rainforest, as their Duck Captain identifies and explains fascinating plants, including ferns, orchids, strangler figs and the infamous stinging tree. Students will keep an eye out for turtles, fish, eels, birds, pythons and other reptiles on land."; "Pamagirri Dance Performance (30 Minutes) — The Pamagirri Aboriginal Experience offers students the opportunity to see a traditional dance performance in the setting of a beautiful Rainforest Amphitheatre. This cultural experience is not only educational, but fun, with the opportunity for willing students to get up on stage and join a dance!"; "Dreamtime Walk (30 Minutes) — This guided tour, along an all-weather walk-way, gives insight into Aboriginal traditions and beliefs, and how several ancient implements were used. After a Boomerang throwing demonstration and lessons from their guide, students can test their boomerang throwing skills. Next their guide will explain how the didgeridoo is made and used in ceremonies, then will demonstrate techniques used to play this ancient instrument. Students will also be amazed at the distance their guide can throw spears using a Woomera during the spear throwing demonstration."; "Wildlife Park (30 Minutes) — Students will have 30 minutes to explore the wildlife park, hand feed the free-roaming Kangaroos and learn about a variety of native Australian wildlife including the three most special to Tropical North Queensland – the Saltwater Crocodile, Tree Kangaroo and Southern Cassowary."; "Optional Tree Planting Experience (60 minutes, includes Rainforest Tour) — Along the track, the Army Duck will make a stop allowing the students to disembark and begin their tree planting experience. Students will be planting locally sourced native rainforest species selected for their ability to attract wildlife. Their driver will guide them within the rainforest provide instructions on how to plant the tree and teach them about the importance of reforestation."; "Aboriginal Face Painting — Many Aboriginal communities have been painting their bodies for thousands of years. During this face painting session, students learn about the significance of body painting and experience it first-hand."; "Pamagirri Rainforest Walkabout or Yarning Circle (45 minutes) — For groups of 12 students or less, the Rainforest Walkabout is an interactive tour that will teach students about the diversity of Indigenous cultures around Australia and some common customs such as totems and body painting before embarking on a guided walk in the Rainforest to identify plants used for bush tucker or medicinal remedies as well as traditional artefacts, tools and weapons. To cater for larger groups, the Yarning Circle provides a safe forum for open sharing and discussion of issues related to Indigenous Culture. Each discussion is led by Pamagirri Guides and subject matter can be directed by the participating students or their attending teachers."; "Boomerang Painting (30 mins) — With guidance from a Pamagirri Artist, each member of the group paints their own Boomerang to take home on the day – 1 Boomerang per person."; "Canvas Art Workshop (30 mins) — With guidance from a Pamagirri Artist, each member of the group creates their own small 5"x7" canvas of Aboriginal style artwork. All canvases can be taken home on the day." From the Group Activities & Team Building page, offered to groups of all kinds including schools: "Tree Planting — A fun and rewarding way for guests to learn about forest conservation. Each student is presented with a certificate of appreciation and a commemorative plaque for each school is placed on our Honour Board."; "Aboriginal Art Workshop — Enjoy a group lesson with a Pamagirri Artist to learn about painting techniques, storytelling and traditions. Each guest will create their own mini canvas of Aboriginal style artwork or transform plain boomerangs into unique pieces of art (guest's choice)."; "Tropical Fruit Tasting — Taste a selection of seasonal tropic and exotic fruit- 1 skewer per person."; "Eco Sustainability Tour — The Behind The Scenes Tour provides you with a first-hand look at the eco-efficient initiatives in place at Rainforestation including the vermiculture (worm farm) and bio cycle waste treatment plant."; "Behind the Scenes Tour — The Behind-the-Scenes Tour with a Wildlife Guide is available for pre-booked groups...Visit koala & leaf preparation areas; Learn about breeding and conservation programs and how our animals are cared for; Enter a koala enclosure and get up close to our koalas; Pat this Australian icon and learn more about koalas; Snake holding option also available (subject to availability)."

**duration:** "Army Duck Tour (45 Minutes)"; "Pamagirri Dance Performance (30 Minutes)"; "Dreamtime Walk (30 Minutes)"; "Wildlife Park (30 Minutes)"; "Optional Tree Planting Experience (60 minutes, includes Rainforest Tour)"; "Pamagirri Rainforest Walkabout or Yarning Circle (45 minutes)"; "Boomerang Painting (30 mins)"; "Canvas Art Workshop (30 mins)"; Kindy/school-equivalent activities on the Group Activities page state "Duration: Allow 45 Minutes" (Army Duck), "Duration: 30 minutes for the Show and 30 minutes for the Dreamtime Walk" (Pamagirri), "Duration: Allow 40 minutes" (Koala & Wildlife Park), "Duration: Allow 1 hour" (Tree Planting, done in conjunction with the Army Duck tour), "Duration: Allow 30 minutes" (Aboriginal Art Workshop), "Duration: Allow 15-20 minutes" (Tropical Fruit Tasting), "Duration: Allow 30 minutes" (Eco Sustainability Tour), "Duration: Allow 15-30 minutes" (Behind the Scenes Tour). No single combined clock-stamped whole-day itinerary is published; durations are given per named activity and packages are built from these named, timed blocks.

**group_size_min_max:** "Please note: the rates apply to local Cairns, Port Douglas and Atherton Tablelands region schools only. For schools in other regions, please contact us for rates." The brochure states "*All prices are for a minimum group size of 10 students" for the All Australian Experience, Dreamtime Discovery and Indigenous Experience packages, and "*Not including Tree Planting" as a footnote on the Rainforest & Wildlife package. Lunch options state "No minimum or maximum numbers required." Optional extras carry their own minimums: "Boomerang Painting (30 MINS) Minimum: 10 pax | Maximum: On application"; "Canvas Art Workshop (30 MINS) Minimum: 10 pax | Maximum: On application" (large-canvas variant: "Minimum 25 pax"). On the Group Activities page, the Rainforest Walkabout is capped "Up to 15 pax per group and prior booking required", the Tropical Fruit Tasting requires a "Minimum of 10 pax per booking", the Eco Sustainability Tour a "Minimum of 10 pax per booking", and the Behind the Scenes Tour a "Minimum of 10 pax per booking". The school-page brochure separately notes the Pamagirri Rainforest Walkabout applies "For groups of 12 students or less", with a Yarning Circle offered "to cater for larger groups".

**participant_thresholds:** none listed as an overall whole-visit maximum; per-activity thresholds are as given in group_size_min_max above, and the Pamagirri Dance Performance venue capacity is stated on the brochure's Indigenous Experience page as "The rainforest provides a natural backdrop for the show and serves as the theatre walls. The sounds of the forest provide accompaniment...while the dances depict the unique animals, traditional history and food gathering of this ancient culture" with the amphitheatre described elsewhere in the same brochure section as seating "approximately 400 people".

## Inventory

**facilities:** "Rainforestation Nature Park is an all-weather attraction"; a 100 acre (40-hectare) property (brochure: "set on a 100 acre (40-hectare) property located in the middle of Kuranda's World Heritage Rainforest"); Rainforest Amphitheatre; all-weather walk-way for the Dreamtime Walk; Tropical Fruit Orchard with "over 40 different species of exotic tropical fruits"; Koala & Wildlife Park; Outback Restaurant, described on Visitor Info as "Our café is open daily from 9am to 3pm, with lunch options available between 10am and 2pm"; restaurant facilities with "a maximum capacity of 500 pax" (Group Activities page); on-site parking, "Free parking is available onsite. Please note that the car park is locked overnight"; Army Duck Platforms for boarding; a shuttle bus service between Kuranda Village (Australian Butterfly Sanctuary) and Rainforestation.

**equipment_provided:** boomerangs and canvases for the art activities are supplied and taken home ("1 Boomerang per person"; "each participant... can be taken home on the day"); no other equipment provision (e.g. safety gear) is stated on the pages read.

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "Chris Grantham, General Manager is a fully qualified Workplace Health and Safety Officer and also the Workplace Representative of Rainforestation Nature Park" (Risk Statement PDF); "Key personnel of Rainforestation Nature Park are qualified First Aid Officers in the workplace" (Risk Statement PDF); "All staff under-go training in emergency procedures as a part of their induction" (Risk Statement PDF); "Informative "Duck" Drivers share their valuable knowledge and sense of humour during a tour through the rainforest" and guides are described throughout as "Pamagirri Guides" and "wildlife keeper"/"Wildlife Guide"; no formal teaching or education qualification is stated for guides.

**accessibility_provision:** "Rainforestation is an entirely accessible experience."; "Upon arrival, please drive up the ramp to the top carpark where you will find our Accessible Parking. There is no ramp access via the front of house."; "Rainforestation has Accessible Toilets and ramps to facilitate access to the entire Pamagirri Experience (Dance Show and Dreamtime Walkabout) and Wildlife Park."; "Army Ducks can be boarded and disembarked on the Army Duck Platforms. Army Ducks can be boarded via leaving the wheelchair and sliding onto the rear seat which is positioned at the same height."; "Guests should be aware that in the event of an Army Duck breakdown whilst on tour, rather than being transferred to another vehicle, visitors using wheelchairs would need to wait on the vehicle whilst it is being repaired."; "Please note that Shuttle Bus services between Rainforestation Nature Park and the Australian Butterfly Sanctuary in Kuranda Village are also accessible—they have low floor entry, a ramp and an allocated space for wheelchairs or mobility scooters."; "Local visitors with a Companion Card can present it to receive one free entry and one entry at the local rate, while non-local Companion Card holders can avail themselves of one free entry and entry at the standard Rate, both offering discounts off entry only. Additionally, seniors with Australian Seniors Cards can enjoy a 20% discount on park entry."

**animal_species_held:** kangaroos ("hand feed the free-roaming Kangaroos"), Southern Cassowary, Saltwater Crocodile ("our giant crocodile Jack the Ripper"), Lumholtz's Tree Kangaroo, Tasmanian Devils, koalas, snakes, wallabies, Dingoes, a Wombat, and turtles/fish/eels/birds/pythons/reptiles seen from the Army Duck ("Students will keep an eye out for turtles, fish, eels, birds, pythons and other reptiles on land"; "keep an eye out for water dragons, pythons, turtles and more").

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** "All school excursion programs can be customised to suit the current Australian Curriculum Achievement Standard and can be moulded to be suitable for different abilities and age groups."

**teacher_materials:** "Download Program Brochure" (the 13-page "Local Educational Group Programmes 2026" PDF read in full for this profile, covering Rainforestation and its three sister CaPTA Group parks) and a separate "Download Vacation Care Brochure" for the Cairns Vacation Care Programs; a set of named Risk Assessment Forms is also listed for download (see risk_assessment_available below), but no curriculum-mapped lesson plan or worksheet document is named.

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** yes. The School Programs page lists, under "Risk Assessment Forms": "Boomerang Throwing Risk Assessment"; "Behind The Scenes Eco Tour Risk Assessment"; "Bush Walk Activities Risk Assessment"; "Rainforestation Nature Park Risk Statement"; "Traditional Painting Risk Assessment"; "Tree Planting Activities Risk Assessment"; "Behind The Scenes Wildlife Experience Risk Assessment"; "Dance Show Risk Assessment"; "DUKW Tour Risk Assessment"; "Habitat Captive Management Policy"; "Wildlife Experience Risk Assessment". The "Rainforestation Nature Park Risk Statement" was downloaded and read in full: a one-page "Risk Management Policy Statement" stating "Rainforestation Nature Park, Kuranda is committed to ensuring the safety of staff and patrons. As such, all staff under-go training in emergency procedures as a part of their induction. Procedures are reviewed annually to take into consideration workplace changes / hazards.", naming an emergency procedure sequence ("Evaluate the situation immediately"; "Notify emergency services 000 fire, police, ambulance depending on the nature of the emergency"; "Ascertain if an evacuation is needed"; escalating through evacuation, muster-point headcounts and awaiting emergency-personnel direction).

**insurance_stated:** none listed on the pages or documents read.

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed on the pages or documents read.

**accreditations_displayed:** a gallery of accreditation and membership badges is displayed on the site, including images captioned in file name and surrounding context as: an "Eco Certified Nature Tourism" logo (alt text: "Eco Certified Nature Tourism logo: Green leaves forming a globe, promoting sustainable travel."); a "Rainforest Rescue Organisation Certified" logo (alt text: "Rainforest Rescue Organisation Certified logo, symbolizing environmental protection and sustainable practices."); a "ZAA Accredited" badge (Zoo and Aquarium Association); an ATEC (Australian Tourism Export Council) member badge; a "BOQ EP" (Bank of Queensland Employer Partner) stamp; a "Family-Owned Emblem Australasia"; and a further "Sustainable" practices badge. Alt text for the ZAA, ATEC, BOQEP and Family-Owned badges was not separately machine-readable from the fetched HTML beyond the image file names and gallery captions cited.

## Note

Rockhampton Zoo passes the spec's selection standard on a narrow basis: it explicitly states it "is unable to provide private tours or talks for schools and groups," so a school's weekday visit is organised only to the extent that any visitor's is, through the daily public Keeper Talks and the online self-guided tour, both available to a booked-in-advance school group as they would be to any visitor. ## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "School tours"; the underlying tour taken is named "Sugar Shed Tour" elsewhere on the site

**activities_verbatim:** "Sarina Sugar Shed provides educational and informative tours ideally suited to school students from primary to secondary age." "Tour the facility where students will be excited to see the processes involved in producing sugar from sugar cane." "Students will have the opportunity to taste fairy floss and ginger beer." "If the tour is fully booked (20 or more participants) by a school, then the tour guide can personalise the tour for the age of the students attending." The Sugar Shed Tour page describes the underlying tour content in full: "This one-hour guided interpretative tour takes you on an interactive journey through the sugar cane growing and crushing process, from getting an up-close view of sugar cane in our own patch to tastings of products made with sugar that are produced onsite. We offer the only such tour in Australia." "During the tour, we will share a brief background of the history of sugar cane farming in the region and explain how sugar cane is grown and harvested. Then it's into our own miniature mill area where we show the same crushing process that the operating mills use, featuring a variety of demonstrative aids. The tour includes the various tastes of sugar cane production, such as freshly-crushed juice, molasses and maybe even a nibble of the cane stalk itself." "The whole paddock-to-bottle sugar journey is completed with tastings of our liqueur products, our chutneys, relishes and sauces, our non-alcoholic ginger beer, and our freshly-spun fairy floss." "Please note, this is an interpretative tour explained through a replica non-operating miniature sugar cane mill in our sheds. The Sarina Sugar Shed is not associated with the Wilmar Plane Creek Mill located behind us, and tours do not operate through the Plane Creek Mill."

**duration:** "Bookings and payment required in advance" (School tours section); the tour itself is stated at "Tours take approximately one hour" on the School tours section, matching the general "one-hour guided interpretative tour" description on the Sugar Shed Tour page

**group_size_min_max:** "If the tour is fully booked (20 or more participants) by a school, then the tour guide can personalise the tour for the age of the students attending" implies 20 as a full-booking threshold rather than a stated maximum; the general Group Tour Bookings section above the School tours section states group rates apply "For groups of 10 or more adults" and that bookings "with more that 20 people" require contacting the team directly, but does not restate these thresholds as specific to school bookings; no minimum group size is stated for a school booking itself

**participant_thresholds:** "If the tour is fully booked (20 or more participants) by a school, then the tour guide can personalise the tour for the age of the students attending"; "Up to two teachers per group of 20 can accompany the students for FREE"
