---

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "Stayin' Alive" (Prep, Year 1); "Dive into the Past" (Year 2); "Let's Get Sorted" (Year 3, Year 7); "From Little Things, Big Things Grow" (Year 4, Year 6); "The Hunger Games" (Year 4, Year 7); "Antarctic Adventure" (Year 5, Year 6); "Ever Changing Environments" (Year 5, Year 10, Years 11 & 12); "Bear Essentials" (Year 8, Year 9); "Marine Investigators" (Year 9, Year 10, Years 11 & 12); "Reef Romance" (Year 9); "How to Train a Seal" (Year 10, Years 11 & 12, Vocational); "Go with the Flow" (Years 11 & 12); "What's in the Water" (Years 11 & 12); "Paws, Claws and Roars" (All years); "Environmental Issues" (All years); "Getting Smart About Sharks" (All years); "In Depth with Dolphins" (All years); "The Real Deal with Seals" (All years); "Currents and Creatures" (All years); "Marine Wildlife First Aid" (Primary and Secondary, run by the Sea World Foundation); "SharkSmart" (a free self-guided education trail at Shark Bay, not tied to a year level); "Tropical Reef Snorkel" (an add-on, 14 years and over, participation criteria apply, priced separately from the core \$80.00 Educational Program fee)

**activities_verbatim:** "During this full day program, students will investigate the basic needs of plants and animals for their survival, and the effects of seasonal change and human impact on their habitats." (It's Alive and I Know Why / Stayin' Alive family, Prep-level framing on the shared page); "During this program students will focus on either Dolphins or Sharks for an up-close encounter to identify the external features of the chosen animal!" (Stayin' Alive, Year 1); "Year 2 students will Dive into the Past of Sea World Marine Park to assist in developing their knowledge and understanding of patterns of observation and connections to history...Through observations and interactions with a range of the objects, animals, technologies and scientific activities at Sea World's Shark Bay Exhibit, students will review the past vs the present." (Dive into the Past); "In this program, students will be introduced to the diversity of life in the ocean to assist them in developing their knowledge and understanding of classification. Through observations and interactions with the amazing animals found at Sea Worlds Shark Bay exhibit, students will review what makes something living or non-living." (Let's Get Sorted); "During this program, students will gain an understanding of the variety of ways offspring are born (such as eggs and live young). Using sharks as a focus animal, students will compare life cycles in different species." (From Little Things, Big Things Grow); "Year 4 students will observe the inhabitants of Shark Bay to determine their interconnectedness with each other and their environment and how humans are interlinked to all life in marine ecosystems. Students will construct food chains." (The Hunger Games, Year 4); "During this program, students will observe two species of Antarctic Penguins to learn how animals' bodies are equipped to suit the conditions of their environment." (Antarctic Adventure); "This program allows students to consolidate their understanding of climate change with a focus on how it is affecting the survival of Polar Bears." (Ever Changing Environments, Year 10 framing); "During this program, students will learn how reef ecosystems consist of communities of interdependent organisms...in Shark Bay's underwater viewing gallery." (Marine Investigators); "Students will explore the reproductive strategies of a range of marine animals, including sharks and rays, and investigate how these strategies are adapted to the challenges of life in the ocean." (Reef Romance); "This program enables students to gain a firsthand understanding of how Sea World's animals are cared for. Students will get an in-depth look at the care and management of seals and sea lions." (How to Train a Seal); "This interactive program provides a unique behind-the-scenes look into the mesmerizing world of Sea Jellies at Sea World." (Go with the Flow); "Students will use technical equipment to identify, measure and compare water quality parameters in a closed aquarium setting and an open, natural system." (What's in the Water); "This interactive program provides a broad overview of polar bear biology and ecology for students of all ages." (Paws, Claws and Roars); "This program introduces students to the numerous ways humans are interconnected to the oceans and their inhabitants...how fishing activities, spark nets, marine debris and pollution are collectively contributing to loss of biodiversity." (Environmental Issues); "During this interactive program, students of all ages will learn about the amazing biology and ecology of sharks!" (Getting Smart About Sharks); "This interactive program provides a broad overview of dolphin biology and ecology for students of all ages." (In Depth with Dolphins); "During this interactive program, students of all ages will learn about the fascinating biology and ecology of seals." (The Real Deal with Seals); "Step into the glowing world of sea jellies and uncover the science behind one of the ocean's most misunderstood creatures." (Currents and Creatures); "During this interactive program students are introduced to the marine wildlife species commonly found in Australia [and] the threats they face that can result in marine wildlife incidents. Students will be equipped with tools and information to contact the relevant rescue organisations and provide first aid for a marine animal in distress." (Marine Wildlife First Aid); "Learn how to be SharkSmart in our interactive education trail at Sea World! Proudly supported by the QLD Government, students will learn about shark species and biology, fascinating facts about sharks, how to be SharkSmart in the water and new technology being used in Queensland to monitor sharks and keep people safe." (SharkSmart)

**duration:** none listed in hours or minutes on the pages read. The booking form states only that "Educational Programs have pre-scheduled arrival times" and that "Education program times are unable to be adjusted. All education programs run to a fixed schedule to align with park operations and daily schedules," directing enquirers to a separate program-times page (themeparks.com.au/education/program-times) that falls outside the four pages read.

**group_size_min_max:** "Minimum of 10 students per school group booking is required" for general admission. For the Educational Program itself, the booking form states only that "minimum and maximum participants apply" without giving the numbers.

**participant_thresholds:** "We do not have a specified teacher-to-student ratio for excursions, however we recommend no more than 20 students per teacher." The \$80.00 Educational Program fee is "charged per session, not per student"; "Selected programs incur a per student fee of \$13.00 or an additional fee for the Tropical Reef Snorkel" (\$40.00, 14 years and over, "participation criteria applies").

## Inventory

**facilities:** Shark Bay (named as the setting for multiple programs, including an "underwater viewing gallery" and a "Tropical Reef Lagoon"); a general-attractions page names Leviathan (Thrill Level - Max, thrill ride), Trident (Thrill Level - Mild, family ride), Vortex (Thrill Level - Max, thrill ride), the ONE Dolphin Presentation, the Thunder Lake Stunt Show (listed as in its "Final Days - Ends 31st Aug"), and The New Atlantis (Thrill Level - Moderate, outdoor precinct), out of a stated "34" total attractions of which only these six had loaded on the page read; lockers are available for hire "with costs ranging from \$15 to \$25 for a large size locker" (FAQ)

**equipment_provided:** none listed as general equipment; the Year 11 & 12 Marine Investigator Program offers an option to have "quadrats and a transect set up in the Tropical Reef Lagoon for a more-in-depth survey during your snorkel," requiring set-up prior to arrival

**staff_qualifications_stated:** none listed by title or credential; programs are described as delivered by "our team" and, for the Marine Investigators and How to Train a Seal programs, framed around exposure to Sea World's own animal-care staff practice

**accessibility_provision:** "Accessible and private changing facilities are available. Please contact our Education Team prior to your visit to discuss your needs and ensure the appropriate facilities are reserved." A separate Accessibility page exists in the site's main navigation but falls outside the fixed four-page set for this research pass.

**animal_species_held:** dolphins, sharks and rays, seals and sea lions, polar bears, Antarctic penguins ("two species"), sea jellies, and reef animals generally (named across the program descriptions read); the attractions page adds no further species beyond the ride and presentation list

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** "Our Australian Curriculum aligned educational programs are available for each year level." Several programs carry a "Version 9" tag (aligned to Australian Curriculum Version 9.0) and others carry a "QCAA" or dated "QCAA 2024"/"QCAA 2025" tag, indicating alignment to the Queensland Curriculum and Assessment Authority syllabus for that subject.

**teacher_materials:** "Yes. Curriculum-aligned worksheets and learning resources are available for our Sea World programs. These will be provided on arrival, depending on your program." A "Pre-Visit Questionnaire" is provided as part of the booking form, asking teachers to state the unit of study, where the excursion sits within it, whether it links to an assessment task ("Yes. Please attach the task sheet" / "No"), any request for specific inclusions, and any student requirements such as "ESL or special needs."

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** none listed on the pages read

**insurance_stated:** none listed

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed; the only identification requirement stated is that "Bona-fide Teachers/PWD Carers are eligible for free of charge park admission upon presentation of relevant school, OSHC or carer identification," which is an admission-eligibility check, not a child-safety credential

**accreditations_displayed:** "Sea World is proudly positive animal welfare accredited by the Zoo and Aquarium Association Australasia." (attractions page)

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** Education admission: Prep & Primary Students \$30.50; Prep & Primary Accompanying Adults \$30.50; Prep & Primary Teachers \$0.00; Secondary Students \$42.00; Secondary Accompanying Adults \$42.00; Secondary Teachers \$0.00; Tertiary Students \$45.00 ("Requires Participation in Education Program"); Persons With Disabilities Groups \$29.50; Persons With Disabilities Carers \$0.00. Educational Program: \$80.00 "per program," "charged per session, not per student," with "Selected programs" also incurring "a per student fee of \$13.00" or the separate Tropical Reef Snorkel fee of \$40.00 (14 years and over). A flat Booking Fee of \$6.00 "applies to all bookings." Rates are stated as "valid 19 December 2025 - 18 December 2026."

**adults_free_ratio:** Teachers attend free of charge ("Prep & Primary Teachers \$0.00"; "Secondary Teachers \$0.00"), as do "Bona-fide...PWD Carers," but no numeric ratio of free teachers per student group is stated; "Accompanying Adults" who are not teachers are charged the same rate as students in their age band (\$30.50 Prep & Primary, \$42.00 Secondary).

**catering:** Prepaid meal vouchers: Maxi Meal Voucher \$20.00 ("Choose from Beef Burger, Chicken Burger, Chicken Nuggets, Veggie Burger...served with chips and regular soft drink or Mt Franklin Water," or a pizza-slice option, pickup at "Plaza Prime Burgers or Pizza Shack"); Ultimate Triple Treat Deal \$29.00 (burger meal, bottomless-refill drink, ice cream, pickup at "Plaza Prime Burgers"); Sandwich Pack \$16.00 (choice of fillings plus "Mini fruit salad and a Mini Muffin," a water or juice popper, vegetarian option available on request, pickup at "Fresh Focus"); Mini Meal Voucher \$15.00 (cheeseburger or kids' chicken nuggets, chips, small soft drink or water, pickup at "Plaza Prime Burgers"); Single Serve Ice-Cream Voucher \$6.50. "All meal vouchers require minimum 48 hours notice of cancellation or full cost incurred."

**cancellation_and_weather:** "Cancellation Fees apply within 72 Hours of scheduled program" for the Educational Program component. Meal vouchers separately "require minimum 48 hours notice of cancellation or full cost incurred." No weather-specific policy is stated on the pages read.

**booking_lead_time:** "All orders must be received a minimum of five business days in advance" (ticket order). "Educational Programs are only available during Queensland School Terms, excluding weekends and public holidays."

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** "Simply click on your location, download the form, and email your completed form to education@vrtp.com.au" (schools page). The Sea World-specific completed booking form, with the Pre-Visit Questionnaire attached, is emailed to the same address. "For further information, please phone our School Excursions & Education Department. Phone: 07 5519 6210. Email: education@vrtp.com.au." The schools page also carries an "Enquire Now" contact form and states "contact us on 07 5519 6210 to plan your next excursion."

**price_visible_without_asking:** yes. The full education admission schedule, the \$80.00 Educational Program fee, the \$13.00 per-student add-on for selected programs, the \$40.00 Tropical Reef Snorkel fee and the \$6.00 booking fee are all published on the freely downloadable 2026 booking form, with no login or quote request required.

**downloadable_pack:** the Sea World Education Booking Form 2026 (four pages: booking-details cover sheet, ticket and meal order, educational program selection, and pre-visit questionnaire) is freely downloadable from the schools page without registration; a separate "Booking Form" is likewise downloadable for each of the other Village Roadshow Theme Parks.

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed on the pages read

**title_and_opening_line:** Title: "School Excursions & Programs" (themeparks.com.au/education, the shared schools page); the Sea World-specific booking form is separately titled "School Excursions & Educational Programs 2026 Booking Form." Opening line (shared schools page): "Searching for your next school excursion, hands-on learning experience, or end-of-year celebration? Bring the curriculum to life in the ultimate immersive learning experience at Australia's best theme parks!" Opening line (Sea World booking form): "Sea World Education rates are available to Australian Domestic and Secondary Schools as well as pre-approved Outside of School Hours Care providers."

## Note

Several Sea World education programs carry titles borrowed from film franchises with no continuity to their content ("The Hunger Games" at Year 4 and Year 7 is, per its own description, a Shark Bay food-web and marine-ecosystem session with no stated connection to the film beyond the name). This is reported as published, not corrected. Two FAQ answers on the shared schools page bear directly on scope for a comparator business: "Are Home School groups eligible for Education rates? Yes, home school groups are eligible to receive education rates, provided registration is supplied for each child attending. Please note, Teacher Free of Charge tickets do not apply to home school bookings," and, on tailoring, "Education programs at Sea World are unable to be tailored to specific tasks or assessments, however our team are more than happy to answer additional questions for inclusion" — a more restrictive tailoring stance than Warner Bros. Movie World's, which is stated on the same page as open to tailoring requests "considered" case by case.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** "School Camps & Educational Programs at Splitters Farm"; "Vacation Care Farm Tours"; "Half-Day School Farm Experience"; "Overnight Camping for Students"; the School Camp Options pack additionally gives suggested itineraries titled "2 Day / 1 night Camp" and "5 Day / 4 night Camp"

**activities_verbatim:** "At Splitters Farm, we believe not all classrooms have four walls. Our farm provides an immersive, hands-on learning environment where students of all ages can connect with nature, understand where their food comes from, and experience sustainable farming practices firsthand." Topics offered: "Paddock rotation and livestock management"; "The importance of pollination and native bees"; "Organic farming and healthy soil practices"; "Composting and waste management"; "Crop cultivation and seasonal produce"; "Ethical and sustainable farming methods." Vacation Care Farm Tours: "Students can collect eggs, feed animals, and interact with farm babies, all under the guidance of our experienced staff." Half-Day School Farm Experience: "Guided farm tours with our farmers"; "Meeting the livestock and learning animal care"; "Visits to the orchard and market garden"; "Discussion time on sustainable farming and ethical practices"; "A BBQ lunch to finish the day." Overnight Camping for Students: "Learn campfire skills and cook damper"; "Participate in star gazing and evening farm activities such as cinema under the stars"; "Take part in farm tours and educational programs"; "Work in teams to race kayaks across the creek." The School Camp Options pack's "2 Day / 1 night Camp" suggested itinerary reads, verbatim in full: "DAY ONE 9am arrival at Splitters Farm; 9am – 9:15am - Safety Briefing and Welcome to Splitters Farm; 9:15am – 10am – Tents will be set up prior to arrival – Students to set up bedding/pillows etc; 10am – 10:30am – Morning Tea – provided by Splitters Farm – students split into 3 groups with corresponding wristbands; 10:30am – 12noon – Rotational Activities x 30 students each group (1. Guided Tour / 2. Kayaking / 3. Go Karts & Paddle Boats); 12noon – 12:45pm - Sausage sizzle Lunch & Free time – provided by Splitters Farm; 12:45pm – 2pm - Rotational Activities x 30 students each group (1. Guided Tour / 2. Kayaking / 3. Go Karts & Paddle Boats); 2pm 2:30pm – Fruit & Water Break – provided by Splitters Farm; 2:30pm – 3:30pm - Damper Making Group 1 -kids split into 2 groups. Damper teams of 2 will cook one damper between 2 students / Other students have free time to swim / fish etc; 3:30pm – 4:30pm - Damper Making Group 2 -kids split into teams of 2 and cook one damper between 2 students / Other students have free time to swim / fish etc; 4:30pm – 6pm Showers & Free time (5min each); 6pm – Dinner – Nacho Bowls – To be provided by Splitters Farm; 6:30pm – Cinema Under the stars (movie list to be provided) Please bring a rug/towel or camp chair to sit on; 9pm – Lights Out. DAY TWO 7am – Breakfast – Mixed Cereals/Milk – provided by Splitters Farm; 8am – 9:30am - Rotational Activities x 30 students each group - (1. Guided Tour / 2. Kayaking / 3. Go Karts & Paddle Boats); 9:30am – 10:30am Morning Tea – Provided By Splitters Farm; 10:30am – Self-guided Farm Access / pack up bedding etc in tents ready for bus; 11:30am – Lunch – Provided by Splitters Farm – sandwiches and/or wraps; 12noon – Board Buses and depart Farm." No equivalent timed detail is given beneath the "5 Day / 4 night Camp" heading in the same pack.

**duration:** Half-Day School Farm Experience is described only by its name, no clock hours given beyond "half-day"; Vacation Care Farm Tours state no duration ("Tours can be tailored to your school holiday or vacation care program"); Overnight Camping for Students is offered as a "2 Day / 1 night Camp" (per the timed itinerary above) and a "5 Day / 4 night Camp" (heading only, no published detail)

**group_size_min_max:** the School Camp Options pack's general Guided Farm Tours listing (not school-exclusive) states "Private tours available for groups of 15 people and upon request" and "Min 2 pax Max 120 pax"; the 2 Day/1 Night itinerary splits students into "3 groups" of "30 students each group" for rotational activities; accommodation capacities given are Glamping "able to accommodate 48 students in total (6 per tent with trundle beds)", tents "set up to accommodate up to 200 students if required", and the Grazing Station & Sunset Deck dining space "can accommodate over 100 students seated"

**participant_thresholds:** the general (non-school-specific) Guided Meet the Animals Walking Tour states "Numbers are limited to 25 guests for safe social distancing"; the general Farm Entry (Self Guided) page states "Under 12 must have an accompanying adult"; the itinerary's rotational activities run at "30 students each group"

## Inventory

**facilities:** "Highland Cow Photoshoot Experience"; "Farm Entry (Self Guided)"; "GUIDED FARM TOUR"; "VIP Farm Tour"; "GO KART HIRE"; "CINEMA UNDER THE STARS"; "Damper Making"; "Barra Feeding"; "Canoe & Kayak Hire"; "CATCH N RELEASE FISHING"; "Nature Walks"; "Swimming"; "Private Farm Tour"; "Day Trippers"; powered and unpowered camping with tent hire; glamping ("2-bedroom deluxe tent-style cabin with private bathroom, kitchen & balcony"); "The Grazing Station & Sunset Deck are available for exclusive hire for your school camp and can accommodate over 100 students seated"; the dining space "includes a bar, fridges & freezers, a deep fryer, a commercial electric oven, a woodfired pizza oven & full commercial kitchen so we can cater to your school camp"; "Bus parking and turnaround points are available on site"; "Disabled access parking, accommodation, and toilets are available"

**equipment_provided:** "Tents sleep 4 people and can be set up prior to arrival or as a group activity. Students must bring their own pillow & bedding" for standard camping; "Powered & Unpowered camping is available with tent hire also available for schools that do not own their own"; glamping "Includes linen, continental breakfast & firewood" (Group Stay Options pack); Farm Entry (Self Guided) "includes food for our animals", i.e. feed is provided to visitors for hand-feeding; paddle boat hire notes "Lifejackets are also available"

**accessibility_provision:** from the Accessibility page: "We offer a mobile check-in option for checking in via tablet device, phones, and EFTPOS device"; "Glamping Tent (ANGUS) is built with wider access inside of the tent and a large accessible bathroom with grab rails and a shower seat"; "Two disability car park spots are allocated in the parking lot behind the main office"; "The Campground amenities block has an accessible toilet that is ambulant in sizing with grab rails present and a concrete pathway surrounds the perimeter"; "The Grazing Station Camp Kitchen has 8 park bench style tables and seats allowing for wheelchair access at each table"; "Assistance dogs welcomed"; "Roads – either bitumen or blue metal road base. Note some parts of the park are undulating but can be easily navigated by most wheelchairs"; a passage on sensory needs states the venue will "design an environment that best suits your needs" for guests "high on the spectrum or with social anxieties." The School Camp Options pack separately states "Disabled access parking, accommodation, and toilets are available."

**animal_species_held:** Highland cows (named "Maisey", "Fergus" and "Freya"); camels (named "Alice & Loco"); donkeys (named "Hovea" and "Divina"); peafowl/peacocks; nine horses; goats; alpacas; dachshunds (family pets, named "Doug, Lulu & Phoebe"); dorper sheep; approximately 40 head of cattle, described as "currently separated into two herds, of which a majority are Droughtmasters and Brahman" (a prior Dexter herd is stated as sold on); chickens (described as "Point of Lay" or "Isa Browns"); a turkey; geese; ducks (mostly Muscovy); guinea fowl; pigs

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** school-programme pricing itself is not published; the schools page states "Schools can download our School Camp Options and Group Stay Options or contact us directly for customised programs, pricing, and availability at [email]", repeated for each of the four programmes. The Booking Terms & Conditions PDF's "School Camps Payments" section states: "School Camp bookings are quoted in advance and will be based on the individual length of stay, meal requirements, activities and accommodation type." The School Camp Options pack does publish day-trip prices for the general Farm Tours (not stated as school-exclusive): Farm Entry (Self-Guided) "TEACHERS \$22", "CHILD 2-16Y \$16", "INFANT UNDER 2 \$5", plus an optional "+ PICNIC HAMPER" at "TEACHER \$60" / "STUDENT \$40"; Guided Farm Tours "TEACHERS \$35", "STUDENTS 2-16Y \$25", "INFANT UNDER 2 \$5". The Group Stay Options pack (general group product, not school-exclusive) additionally prices: Guided Farm Tour "ADULT \$40" / "CHILD UNDER 16 \$30"; Self-Guided Farm Tour "ADULT \$30" / "CHILD UNDER 16 \$20" (noted as "included in your overnight stay at Splitters Farm - no additional charges apply"); "DAMPER MAKING PER PERSON \$10"; "GO KARTS FROM \$20"; "KAYAK & CANOE HIRE FROM \$20"; "OUTDOOR CINEMA FROM \$150". The general public Farm Entry (Self Guided) page separately publishes: "Adults \$30", "Children 2-16 years \$20", "Infants under 2 years \$8", "Aged Concession \$25", "Disability Carer \$25", "Family of 4 (2A/2C) \$90".

**adults_free_ratio:** none listed; the School Camp Options pack prices teachers at \$22 (Farm Entry) or \$35 (Guided Tour), not free of charge, and no free-teacher ratio is stated anywhere on the pages read

**catering:** "Meals are prepared by our food safety-qualified staff with all dietary needs catered for"; the venue states it caters to "Gluten-free, dairy-free, and vegetarian options", "Respect for religious dietary requirements" and "Consideration for animal welfare-conscious meals." The School Camp Menu PDF lists full breakfast, lunch, dinner, morning/afternoon tea and supper menus, each item coded for dietary alternatives (GF/DF/LF/VEG/V/H), whether it is a takeaway option for off-site activities, whether it suits groups over 50 students, and a budget scale of \$/\$\$/\$\$\$; items marked with an asterisk carry the note "*Not Suitable for Egg / Nut or seed allergies."

**cancellation_and_weather:** from the Booking Terms & Conditions PDF's "School Camps Payments" section: "Once a school has accepted a written quote, a non-refundable 50% deposit is due to confirm the dates based on min numbers agreed"; "6 weeks prior to the arrival date, schools must confirm the following: Number of Students / Teachers; Dietary needs & allergies; Bedding configurations (if required); Any students with special needs"; "Final invoices are issued and payable 30 days prior to arrival based on the information provided above. Where there is a credit due to a decrease in numbers, the credit is held until the end of the school camp and can be refunded"; "School Camps that are cancelled are not eligible for a refund however bookings can be placed into credit for future dates agreed by both parties." No school-camp-specific weather clause is stated; the general (accommodation-wide) clause reads: "Weather Cancellations: If you or your group do not wish to travel due to extreme weather events (proof of said event must be evident and recognised by the BOM) you will need to contact us immediately to discuss. A change booking fee will be applied and may be credited for a period a 1-year from the date of the cancellation." The general Events/Tour Refunds section adds: "Guided and Self-Guided Farm Access Tour bookings are not refundable however can be changed to an alternate date."

**booking_lead_time:** no minimum lead time is stated for school camps specifically, beyond the 6-week-prior confirmation and 30-day-prior final-invoice deadlines quoted above; the general accommodation terms state "Splitters Farm only take bookings 12-18 months in advance."

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** timed_programme

**programme_names:** the page is titled "Farm School"; the FAQ refers throughout to "the Farm School program" and "Farm School visits"; no further discrete programme titles (for example a separate kindy or vacation-care programme name) are given, though the FAQ confirms the same programme is offered in modified form to vacation care groups: "We offer the Farm School program in school holidays for vacation and outside hours school care groups."

**activities_verbatim:** "Your school group visit can be as simple as immersing your students into a real working farm environment where they'll see our cows being milked, explore sustainable production of vegetables, grain, dairy, eggs and meat, and get up close to our menagerie of farm animals, right through to the full farm to fork supply chain or business case study for secondary subjects."; "We have chickens, ducks, sheep, goats, calves, pigs and cows on the farm. Whilst we may not get to feed all the animals during the visit, the children are able to feed and pat farm animals as part of the experience on the farm."; "Visits to the dairy include watching some of our Jersey Girls being milked. For safety reasons and the welfare of our cows, children don't have the opportunity to milk the cows but will see the whole process in our 100 year old dairy."; "The farm is a learning environment where students can see the processes of growing, harvesting, and rearing, to the processing and packaging stages, right through to marketing, sales and distribution at the farm shop and beyond."; "hands-on learning around historical artefacts, changes over the past 6 generations, and the important role history plays in our future"; "Our program facilitates genuine Q&A and natural inquiry processes, assisting with the development of critical and creative thinking as your students gain firsthand knowledge from real farmers and their lived experience."

**duration:** "We encourage school groups to arrive between 10 and 10.30am so the students can have some time to enjoy morning tea in the picnic paddock along with a chance to stretch their legs and use the bathroom after their bus trip. A break for lunch is included, with departure time generally between 1 and 1.30pm."

**group_size_min_max:** "We prefer groups of up to 60 students at one time but will consider higher numbers on a case-by-case basis. We understand the challenges around transport costs and are happy to discuss group sizing to get the best outcome for everyone." No minimum group size is stated.

**participant_thresholds:** the only numeric threshold published is the preferred ceiling of "up to 60 students at one time", offered as a flexible rather than hard limit ("will consider higher numbers on a case-by-case basis"). No sub-group rotation size, adult-to-child supervision ratio, or first-aid-officer ratio specific to the Farm School programme is stated on the pages read; Conditions of Entry states only the general, non-numeric rule that "Children must be supervised at all times. Farm staff are not responsible for supervising children without parent/caregivers."

## Inventory

**facilities:** "picnic paddock" used for morning tea; "our 100 year old dairy" (the walk-through milking dairy shown to visiting groups); the Farm Larder, the farm's on-site produce and ice-cream shop, referenced as the point of sale for the school-price ice cream; general site navigation additionally lists The Barn (an 1888 building offered as an event venue, not part of the school offer) and a Farm Shop, neither described as part of the Farm School visit itself

**equipment_provided:** none listed for the school programme itself; visiting groups are instead asked to bring their own first aid kit, drinking water, hats, sunscreen and insect repellent (see catering and duration fields for the full "What to Bring" wording)

**staff_qualifications_stated:** "The farmers are your guides – Dave, Kay and Harry have a passion for educating young people about sustainable farming practices and how food is produced, the importance of agriculture in today's society, our collective role in ensuring food security, animal welfare, as well as the value of individual agency in creating positive change for our environmental future." No formal teaching, first-aid or animal-handling qualification or certification is named for the guides themselves; the school group, not the farm, is asked to supply its own "trained first aid officer if this is a requirement of your school."

**accessibility_provision:** none listed on the pages read

**animal_species_held:** chickens, ducks, sheep, goats, calves, pigs, cows (Farm School FAQ: "We have chickens, ducks, sheep, goats, calves, pigs and cows on the farm."); the Farm Stay Activities page (read as an extra page, farm-stay-guest context only) additionally names guinea pigs among animals fed on the farm, but guinea pigs are not stated as part of the Farm School offer specifically

## Curriculum

**curriculum_links:** "Dairy Farm School aligns with a broad range of curriculum areas across the Australian Curriculum V9 and QCAA Senior Syllabuses (2025). Our curriculum links documents assist in identifying these areas in detail." The linked "Curriculum Links Primary" PDF, read in full, maps each year level from Prep to Year 6 against named curriculum strands (Science, Humanities and Social Sciences, Technologies) and specific ACARA codes, for example Prep Science "Living things have basic needs, including food and water (ACSSU002)" and Year 6 Humanities and Social Sciences "The reasons businesses exist and the different ways they provide goods and services (ACHASSK151)"; the same PDF states "We are happy to work with teachers to align our program with particular learning outcomes. Please discuss this with us at the time of booking." and closes with a further table of downloadable classroom lesson plans hosted externally at dairy.edu.au (for example "Managing a Dairy Farm", "The Milk Cycle", "Journey from Farm to Plate"), each cross-referenced to an ACARA code. Separate secondary-level PDFs are linked but not opened: "Australian Curriculum V9 Science Links – Yrs 7 to 10", "Australian Curriculum V9 Technologies Links – Yrs 7 to 10", "Australian Curriculum V9 Humanities Links – Yrs 7 to 10".

**teacher_materials:** "we have established a detailed suite of documents from curriculum links, through to parent information letters, and a complete risk assessment. These documents are available to download below." Freely downloadable without login: "Curriculum Links Primary", "Australian Curriculum V9 Science Links – Yrs 7 to 10", "Australian Curriculum V9 Technologies Links – Yrs 7 to 10", "Australian Curriculum V9 Humanities Links – Yrs 7 to 10". A further "Download Full Excursion Pack" button is gated behind a contact form requiring first name, last name, email and school ("Please complete the form below to receive your copy of the Tommerup's Dairy Farm Full School Excursion Pack."), and was not accessible without submitting those details, so its contents (including any parent information letter or risk assessment referred to in the page text) cannot be described further.

## Trust

**risk_assessment_available:** the Farm School FAQ states only that "regular risk assessments conducted to ensure the safety of our visitors" take place, and the page's own text refers to "a complete risk assessment" among the documents mentioned as part of the (form-gated) Full Excursion Pack; no risk assessment document is directly downloadable on the pages read, and none was accessed since obtaining it required submitting a name, email and school via the gated request form

**insurance_stated:** "Full public liability insurance cover is held by Tommerup's Dairy Farm with regular risk assessments conducted to ensure the safety of our visitors. As part of our insurance cover, all visitors are required to complete a Consent to Attend form."

**child_safety_checks_stated:** none listed on the pages read; no Working with Children Check, Blue Card, or equivalent is mentioned

**accreditations_displayed:** "Agritourism Queensland Foundation Member" appears in the footer of every page read, a founding-membership claim rather than a named safety or education accreditation; no other certification or accreditation body is displayed

## Commercial

**price_and_structure:** no per-student or per-group tour price is published on any of the pages read. The only numeric figure published for the Farm School visit is the ice cream add-on: "Our tour price doesn't include food however, our Jersey Girl ice creams are available to purchase from the Farm Larder at the special school price of \$5 each. These can be chosen as an added extra to the price per person should you wish for the school to organise full payment." The phrase "the price per person" implies a base tour charge exists, but its value is not stated anywhere on the pages read.

**adults_free_ratio:** none listed

**catering:** "Our tour price doesn't include food however, our Jersey Girl ice creams are available to purchase from the Farm Larder at the special school price of \$5 each."; "There are local caterers that can supply catered lunches should that be of interest. Generally we recommend simple lunches such as rolls, focaccias or sandwiches for this purpose. We can liaise with caterers and include this as an extra cost to the visit."; visiting groups are asked to bring "Drinking water (we don't supply drinking water as we are on rain and river water here at the farm)" and "All food and drinks unless you have organised for catering. There are no shops in Kerry – the nearest town is around 20 minutes drive from the farm."

**cancellation_and_weather:** none listed specific to Farm School bookings on the pages read. The Booking Terms & Conditions page, read as an extra page but written for farm-stay and event guests rather than school groups, states: "Please be aware that it is up to our discretion to determine if the weather or emergency conditions are sufficiently inclement to warrant cancellation of your booking. In the event this should happen you will be notified as soon as possible."

**booking_lead_time:** no minimum lead time in days or weeks is stated. "Term 2 and Term 3 are our busiest times for school group visits. We suggest making a tentative booking as soon as possible, then confirming once school approval has been obtained. Our Farm School visits are limited in number across the year to allow us to focus on our farm. Though we do have schools that book year on year, we're always happy to welcome new schools and will try our best to find a date that suits your requirements."

## Conversion

**how_to_book:** "To find out more or book a visit, please complete our contact form." The Farm School contact form collects Name, Email, Phone Number, School, Year Level, Unit of Study, Preferred day/dates, and Comment or Message. Phone: Kay 0400 076 556 (also listed on the general Contact page alongside Dave 0412 727 742). Email: visit@tommerupsdairyfarm.com.au (given in the footer of the Curriculum Links Primary PDF).

**price_visible_without_asking:** partial. Only the \$5 school-price Jersey Girl ice cream add-on is published; the base tour "price per person" referred to on the page is not disclosed without enquiry.

**downloadable_pack:** "Curriculum Links Primary", "Australian Curriculum V9 Science Links – Yrs 7 to 10", "Australian Curriculum V9 Technologies Links – Yrs 7 to 10" and "Australian Curriculum V9 Humanities Links – Yrs 7 to 10" are freely downloadable PDFs, no login or form required. A "Download Full Excursion Pack", described in the page text as including curriculum links, parent information letters and a risk assessment, is gated behind a request form requiring name, email and school, and was not obtained.

**testimonials_from_schools:** none listed on the pages read

**title_and_opening_line:** Title: "Farm School". Opening line: "An authentic multi-faceted learning experience"

## Note

Tommerup's Dairy Farm publishes no dedicated pricing or booking page at all for its Farm School product, in contrast to a general public-facing tariff sheet: the only public tools for a school are the Farm School page's own embedded FAQ and its enquiry form, plus a phone number and email address; the base "price per person" is referenced in passing but never stated in dollars anywhere on the site's public pages. This distinguishes it from venues that quote school pricing only after enquiry but at least confirm a pricing structure exists (per-child banded rates, for instance); here neither the structure nor the figure is public. Farm Stay Activities (milking, calf and pig feeding, rock hopping, swimming) are explicitly scoped to overnight farm-stay guests only ("Please note these activities are for farm stay guests only.") and are not offered as part of the day-visit Farm School programme; the two products should not be conflated when comparing this venue's animal-contact offer against day-excursion competitors.

## Programme

**school_offer_detail_level:** no_school_offer

**programme_names:** none listed. The site's navigation and sitemap carry a page titled "Schools/Vacation Care", but the page itself has no body text beneath that title, and no programme name is published anywhere else on the four pages read.

The Animal Encounters page (read for the programme role) lists paid, general-public keeper-led encounters with individually named animals, transcribed under Inventory below, but none of these is stated to be part of a school excursion or school programme.

## Inventory

From the homepage: "Cafe Under Redevelopment. Cold drinks and ice creams available. For tea, coffee, and hot food, please visit the newly refurbished Big Pineapple Cafe located at our zoo entrance."

**accessibility_provision:** none listed

**animal_species_held:** the homepage states the zoo is "home to over 50 animal species from around the globe". Named on the Animal Encounters page: meerkat, Emperor Tamarin, ruffed lemur (individuals named "Tarvae and her 3 daughters: Izzy, Hasina, and Rihanna"), ring-tailed lemur, red panda, capybara (individuals named "Chip, Pico and Bruno"), marmoset, pygmy marmoset, cotton-top tamarin, koala, quokka, dingo (individuals named "Winter and Tully"), lizard, python, and "our critically endangered radiated tortoise". Additionally named on the zoo map: kangaroo, emu, wombat, snake, owl monkey, spider monkey, white-cheeked gibbon, alligator, crocodile, rhino iguana, Tasmanian devil, greater bilby, porcupine, potoroo, rufous bettong, Lumholtz tree kangaroo, maned wolf, eastern quoll, siamang, cassowary, Goodfellow's tree kangaroo, rock wallaby, sun bear, squirrel monkey, binturong, golden lion tamarin, black and white ruffed lemur, Hamadryas baboon, African painted dog.

## Note

The Schools/Vacation Care page is real (it appears in the site's main sitemap and site-map footer, not just a guessed URL) but its content area is empty: the raw HTML's `entry-content` div contains no text, and an independent WebFetch pass over the same URL confirmed no body text loads for it. This is treated per the spec's allowance for a venue that takes school groups but publishes no school page: profiled here with `school_offer_detail_level: no_school_offer`, with the other fields filled from what the remaining pages publish. Two of the other three fixed pages carry their substantive content as a single embedded image rather than page text: Prices (a pricing-chart JPEG) and Plan Your Visit (a zoo-map JPEG), both read and transcribed directly from the image. Wildlife HQ Zoo is a tenant business on the Big Pineapple site; per the brief, only Wildlife HQ's own animal encounters, schools offer (or lack of one) and pricing are profiled here, and no general Big Pineapple attraction (gift shop, macadamia tours, TukTuk rides, giant slide, market days) is folded in except the Big Pineapple Train, which whqzoo.com itself prices and packages as its own "Zoo & Train Combo Deal".
